BERR-mid-`<instruction>` tests for the 12 `ex_mem_stall` sources Phase 109 gave `mem_abort` coverage but never had a dedicated fault-injection test (single CAS, MOVEP, MOVE16, ADDX, ABCD, PACK, BFINS, CMP2, MOVE mem-mem, RTR, RTE, PMOVE64) — chaining 12 independent faults into one simulation (on top of the pre-existing interrupt-mid-CAS2/BERR-mid-CAS2/BERR-mid-PTEST tests ahead of them) surfaced four bugs, all invisible to every earlier test since none had ever exercised a *second* fault in one run. **Bug 1 (testbench, severe)**: the shared vector-2 handler (Phase 108) had no RTE and fell through into default-filled NOPs on completion — harmless for the single BERR-mid-CAS2 test it was built for, but fall-through doesn't stop at the edge of unused memory: it marches all the way to byte 0x100 (B-1's own code) and silently re-executes the *entire file* from scratch after every fault, corrupting shared register/SR/memory state (traced: SR reading 0x0000, `ext_fc=001` instead of `101` during a plain supervisor access, RTE reading back a stray address instead of a real SR value from a stack slot a replayed pass had overwritten). Fixed by parking the handler in a `BRA.B -2` self-loop instead. **Bug 2 (RTL, the real one)**: `exc_frame_valid`'s "latched until reset" design (BIU-090, Phase 108) means an edge-detector keyed off it — `m68030_top.sv`'s own Phase 108 fix for `eu_bus_err_r` — can only ever fire once per session; every EU-side bus error after the very first is silently dropped, since `exc_frame_valid` never returns to 0 for the edge-detector to re-arm on. A previously-undiscovered limitation in the Stage 1 BERR-hang fix itself, invisible until this phase chained more than one fault. **Fixed in `rtl/m68030_top.sv`**: `eu_bus_err_r` now latches directly off `eu_berr` (`biu_cache_if.sv`'s `CI_BERR` state, which unconditionally returns to `CI_IDLE` the next cycle regardless of anything else) instead of an edge detector on `exc_frame_valid` — `eu_berr` is already a genuine one-cycle pulse per fault, so it naturally re-arms every time; no edge-detect needed. **Bug 3 (testbench)**: the watch loop's `D5===999` completion check could fire on a stale value left by the *previous* test before this test's own leading `CLR.L D5` had even retired (decode_pc reaching a test's first byte doesn't mean anything there has executed yet) — fixed by snapshotting D5 at watch-loop entry and only trusting a later *transition into* 999. **Bug 4 (testbench, subtlest)**: even a correctly-addressed handler jump let hardware race ahead and run an entire short test (MOVEP, MOVE16, etc.) to normal, unfaulted completion before that test's own `run_berr_mid_test` call had even started watching, since hardware runs continuously in real time regardless of SystemVerilog testbench program order, and a short instruction sequence completes in far fewer cycles than the *previous* test's own trailing "EU recovered" wait takes to return. Fixed with a single fixed parking address (`PARK_ADDR`, a `BRA_SELF` self-loop by default) that every handler unconditionally jumps to, released via a new `claim_park()`/`next_addr` mechanism called as the *last* thing inside the *current* test's own task, right before it returns — the one moment provably safe, since hardware is still parked and the very next statement (zero simulated time later) is the next test's own settle-wait. Result: `stall_fsm_tb.sv` 157/157 checks pass (was hitting a 4.5M-ns hard timeout before these fixes), `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, and a full 121-suite Harte re-run (Verilator batch backend, needed since Bug 2 touches the shared EU bus-error path) — 696590 PASS, 2 FAIL (the pre-existing documented ASL.b corpus anomaly, not a regression), 0 TIMEOUT. See `plan.md §Phase 114`.

115. Memory-indirect EA (`([bd,An],Xn,od)`) Musashi-cosim investigation, confirming and fixing Phase 107's specific decode-bit hypothesis, then finding a second, deeper bug the same investigation surfaced. **Bug 1**: confirmed exactly as hypothesized — `dec_memind_is_post` read `fi_is_s` (ext bit 6, Index Suppress — an unrelated 68020 concept) instead of the real pre/post selector `fi_iis[2]` (I/IS field bit 2); a dedicated test (`tests/memind.s`, deliberately constructed so pre- and post-indexed land on the *same final EA* despite reading the pointer from *different* addresses, so a register-value-only check couldn't have caught it) showed the DUT reading the post-indexed pointer from `A0+Xn` instead of `A0` alone. **Fixed in `rtl/eu_seq.sv`**: `dec_memind_is_post = fi_iis[2]`; also fixed a related bug in the same blocks — `dec_is_idx` (gates whether Xn feeds the *inner* pre-indirection address) was `!fi_is_s` alone, wrongly including Xn in the inner address for post-indexed too (double-counting it, since post-indexed's own Xn addition is handled separately via the already-correct `memind_post_xn_r`) — fixed to `!fi_is_s && !fi_iis[2]`. **Bug 2**, found building a second test for a non-null base displacement (`tests/memind2.s`): the RTL never fetches the extra extension word a non-null bd/od needs at all, desyncing the whole instruction stream into decoding garbage. Root cause: `m68030_seq.sv`'s `is_move_idx_src` ext_count classifier hardcoded 1 extension word for *every* mode=110 form, correct only for brief `(d8,An,Xn)` and null-bd/od full-format — never caught since Harte has zero coverage of this 68020+-only mode and `tb/ea_modes_tb.sv`'s own existing P1-P6 memory-indirect suite injects `ext_data` directly at the EU boundary, bypassing `m68030_seq.sv`'s ext_count/drain mechanism entirely. **Fixed using infrastructure that already existed end-to-end but was never used for this** (`ifu_q3_word`/`ifu_ext34_data`, third/fourth extension-word data paths): `m68030_seq.sv` now peeks the extension word to compute `memind_ext_count = 1 + bd_words + od_words`, with a matching q1/q2 swap in `eu_ext_data`'s construction (the existing ≥2-ext-word convention puts q1 in the high half for immediate-value reconstruction, backwards for `eu_seq.sv`'s own descriptor extraction which always reads `ext_data[15:0]`); `eu_seq.sv`'s `fi_od` extraction — found to have a *third*, independent bug, only ever handling the pre-indexed word-od case — now covers both pre/post and selects `q3_word` vs `ext_data[31:16]` based on whether bd already claimed that slot. Verified via `tests/memind3.s` (word bd + word od combined, and null bd + word od pre-indexed). Long (32-bit) bd/od displacements and the identical gap in every *other* `f_mode==110` instruction family (ALU ops, CMP, Scc, TAS, CHK, LEA/PEA, JMP/JSR, etc.) are deliberately out of scope, documented as follow-up, not attempted — this phase's fix is scoped to `MOVE <ea>,dst` alone, matching what the cosim tests actually exercise. **Found and fixed a regression along the way**: `tb/ea_modes_tb.sv`'s own pre-existing P3 memory-indirect test had the *identical* IS-vs-pre/post conflation baked into its expected values (labeled "post-indexed (IS=1)", expecting `outer=pointer+D1`) — only ever passed because the old buggy RTL made the same mistake; a dedicated Musashi reproduction (`tests/memind4.s`) confirmed real 68020 semantics are that IS=1 suppresses the index register entirely, independent of pre/post, so the correct outer EA is the unmodified pointer — fixed the test's own data/expectations to match. Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, a new `make cosim_memind` target (2/2, following the `buscmp-grpN` pattern) clean, and a full 121-suite Harte re-run (Verilator batch backend) — 696590 PASS, 2 FAIL (same pre-existing ASL.b anomaly), 0 TIMEOUT, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 115`.

116. Stage 1 of extending full-format mode=110 EA support beyond MOVE (plan approved via `EnterPlanMode`, `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`): the "unary memory operand" family — TAS, NBCD, NEGX/CLR/NEG/NOT/TST, memory shift/rotate — sharing the exact `An`+`Xn`-only shape Phase 81's own "Bucket A" grouped for indexed-EA support. **Scope correction found before writing code**: the real gap is bigger than "generalize ext_count" — each family's own EA-decode block in `eu_seq.sv` *also* hardcodes the brief-only 8-bit-displacement interpretation (never checks `fi_is_full`), confirmed directly in TAS's own block; this pattern repeats at ~57 `dec_is_idx=1'b1` sites project-wide, so a full sweep is out of scope for one session — staged like Category B (4→21) and the BERR-abort rollout (4→16→19). **`rtl/m68030_seq.sv`**: added `is_tas_mode110`/`is_nbcd_mode110`/`is_negx_clr_neg_not_tst_mode110`/`is_shift_mode110` into a new `mode110_ea_src` OR-list (seeded with `is_move_idx_src`), widening `is_memind_full`'s gate; verified all five families' own mode=110 baseline ext_count is exactly 1 word, so the existing `ext_count = memind_ext_count` override (from Phase 115) applies unchanged. Had to move `is_memind_full`'s branch to be the very first check in the whole `ext_count` chain — the four new families' own baseline-1 branches sit earlier than the old position, which would otherwise resolve first and silently keep the bug. **`rtl/eu_seq.sv`**: each family's own mode=110 case arm gained `dec_ea_offset = fi_is_full ? fi_bd : <brief 8-bit>` — brief format (the vast majority of real usage) unchanged, full-format now correctly consumes `fi_bd`. **Scope correction found while implementing**: TAS/NBCD are RMW ops, and genuine memory-indirect (not just a longer bd) would need their own multi-phase FSMs (`tas_run_r` etc.) taught an extra pointer-read phase — qualitatively different work, not attempted; scoped to the "full-format, non-indirect" case only, with `fi_bd` used unconditionally as the least-wrong fallback for the deliberately-unhandled indirect sub-case (no worse than before, and `ext_count` still fetches enough words to avoid an IFU desync either way). **Tests**: `tests/memind5.s` (TAS+NBCD) and `tests/memind6.s` (CLR+ASL), both hand-verified rather than wired into `make cosim_memind` — TAS hits a genuinely bus-locked-RMW logging gap in the testbench (confirmed pre-existing via an unmodified baseline `TAS (A0)` test showing the identical gap), CLR/ASL hit the same benign prefetch-interleave difference as `memind.s`/`memind4.s`; every write value and fetch sequence matches Musashi exactly once accounted for. Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, full 121-suite Harte re-run (highest-value gate here since these are all Harte-covered instructions, unlike Phase 115's MOVE work) — 696590 PASS, 2 FAIL (same documented ASL.b anomaly), 0 TIMEOUT. Stages 2-4 (ALU-mem-src + dynamic BTST; Scc/CHK/CMP2-CHK2/ADDQ-SUBQ/LEA-JMP-JSR; MOVEM + MOVE mem-to-mem dst-side + long bd/od) remain deferred per the approved plan. See `plan.md §Phase 116`.

117. Stage 2 of extending full-format mode=110 EA support beyond MOVE: ALU-mem-src (ADD/SUB/CMP/AND/OR/EOR/ADDA/SUBA/CMPA/MULU/MULS/DIVU/DIVS memory forms, both directions) and dynamic BTST/BCHG/BCLR/BSET. Surveyed every remaining brief-only `dec_ea_offset` site in `eu_seq.sv` before touching anything (26 found after Stage 1's 4) and mapped each to its own instruction family by reading context, rather than trusting the ext_count table's own grouping — found 2 were already correctly handled (Phase 115's own MOVE/MOVEA blocks, false positives from the grep), 12 were ALU-mem-src (SUB *and* ADD share one physical code block via a `grp_aop(f_group)` helper, found by reading the code), 2 were dynamic bit-ops (mode=110 only — PC-relative `(d8,PC,Xn)` uses a differently-shaped `dec_abs_ea_val` computation, out of scope here same as Stage 1's own boundary), and the remaining ~10 belong to LEA/CHK/MOVE-to-from-SR-CCR/Scc/ADDQ-SUBQ/MOVEM/MOVE-mem-mem — identified for later stages. **`is_alu_mem_src_mode110` simpler than expected**: `m68030_seq.sv`'s existing `is_alu_mem_src` (for ext_count) already covers groups 8/9/B/C/D with `f_mode` including 110 and doesn't check `f_dir` at all, so narrowing it to `f_mode==3'b110` alone exactly matches all 12 ALU sites in both directions with no further logic — inlined rather than referenced directly since Icarus doesn't support forward-referencing a continuous-assign net across declaration order. `is_dyn_bit_mode110` mirrors the bit-ops' own existing inline ext_count condition. Both confirmed baseline-1 before relying on the unchanged `memind_ext_count` override. **`rtl/eu_seq.sv`**: same one-line `fi_is_full ? fi_bd : <brief>` template as Stage 1 applied to all 14 sites; `dyn_bit_get_Dn`'s register-conflict mechanism needed zero changes, confirming it's orthogonal to the EA-offset fix. **Found a testbench logging quirk while testing**: `tests/memind7.s` initially combined ADD+OR+BSET — ADD/OR compared cleanly via `buscmp.py`, but BSET's own byte RMW showed an alarming-looking mismatch that turned out to be the DUT's bus logger printing the full 32-bit register for a byte transfer instead of just the transferred byte (same shape as Stage 1's TAS finding, different trigger) — the actually-relevant byte matched exactly once inspected directly. Split into `memind7.s` (ADD+OR, wired into `make cosim_memind`) and `memind8.s` (BSET, hand-verified only). Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 3/3, full 121-suite Harte re-run (the highest-value gate in this rollout so far — ADD/SUB/CMP/AND/OR/EOR/BTST-family/MUL/DIV are among the most heavily-exercised instructions in the corpus) — 696590 PASS, 2 FAIL (same documented ASL.b anomaly), 0 TIMEOUT, zero regressions. Stages 3-4 remain deferred, sites already identified. See `plan.md §Phase 117`.

118. Stage 3 of extending full-format mode=110 EA support beyond MOVE: Scc, CHK, ADDQ/SUBQ, MOVE-to/from-CCR/SR, and LEA/JMP/JSR/PEA indexed. Picked up the site list Phase 117's own survey had already identified for Stage 3. Fixed 6 `dec_ea_offset` sites (LEA, CHK — reusing the pre-existing `dyn_bit_get_Dn` deferred-register swap unchanged, MOVE-to-CCR, MOVE-SR write-side, Scc-to-memory, ADDQ/SUBQ-to-memory) plus 3 more via a second signal, `dec_jump_offset`, that JMP/JSR/PEA use instead of `dec_ea_offset` for their target/push-address computation — 9 sites total. **CMP2/CHK2's indexed form turned out to be a different, larger gap**: `eu_seq.sv` has no `f_mode==110` case for CMP2/CHK2 at all (never implemented, not just brief-limited) — out of scope for this template, deferred to its own phase. `m68030_seq.sv`: added `is_chk_mode110`/`is_scc_mode110`/`is_move_sr_ccr_mode110`/`is_addq_subq_mode110`/`is_pea_mode110` plus a new `is_jsr_idx`, folded into `mode110_ea_src`. Hit the Icarus forward-reference issue twice more (`is_addq_subq_ext`/`is_pea` inlined) plus a self-inflicted third instance (moved `is_lea_idx`/`is_jmp_idx`/`is_jsr_idx`'s own declarations earlier instead of inlining, since they have no downstream forward-ref chain). **Found a genuine pre-existing gap while adding `is_jsr_idx`**: JSR `(d8,An,Xn)` had no `ext_count` classifier at all — `is_jmp_idx` only ever matched JMP's own `f_ss==2'b11`, never JSR's `f_ss==2'b10` — harmless for post-execution IFU drain (JSR's own PC redirect flushes/refills the queue regardless, which is presumably why Harte's 100%-passing JSR suite never caught it) but real for `eu_ext_valid` gating on the extra bd/od words a full-format target needs before decode reads them; fixed. **Musashi-cosim**: `tests/memind9.s` (LEA+CHK.L+ADDQ.L+MOVE-to-CCR — hits the same benign prefetch-interleave reordering already documented for `memind.s`/`memind4.s`/`memind6.s`, hand-verified, not wired into automation) and `tests/memind10.s` (PEA+JSR, directly exercising the new `is_jsr_idx` fix via a landing-pad at the computed target — compares cleanly, wired into `make cosim_memind`). **Found and deliberately left unresolved**: MOVE SR,(ea) (the write-side "RMW trick" site) performs an extra bus READ before the write that Musashi doesn't, for indexed EA specifically (brief or full) — pre-existing (predates this phase's `dec_ea_offset`-only edit), doesn't affect correctness (`MOVEfromSR`'s own Harte suite is 100%, since Harte diffs final state not bus-cycle timing) — documented in `memind9.s`'s header, not investigated further. Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 4/4, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, matching the Phase 112 baseline exactly, zero regressions across Scc/CHK/ADDQ-SUBQ/LEA/JMP/JSR/PEA/MOVEtoSR — all heavily Harte-exercised. Stage 4 and CMP2/CHK2's indexed form remain open. See `plan.md §Phase 118`.

119. Stage 4a of extending full-format mode=110 EA support beyond MOVE: MOVEM's own extended-EA form (additive, not override, `ext_count` arithmetic). MOVEM's baseline `ext_count` is already 2 (register mask word q1 + EA descriptor q2) before any full-format concept applies, unlike every Stage 1-3 family's baseline of 1 — so `is_memind_full`'s existing override machinery is wrong for it in two ways: `peek_fi_full`/`peek_fi_bdsz`/`peek_fi_iis` read q1's bits (correct for single-EA-word families, but q1 is MOVEM's mask, not its descriptor) and `fi_bd` reads a bd value from `ext_data[31:16]` (occupied by the mask for MOVEM). Added MOVEM-specific peek signals reading from the correct q2 bit positions, and `movem_ext_count = 2 + bd_words + od_words` (additive on the 2-word baseline). The bd VALUE itself needed a genuine third extension word — reused the project's existing but previously MOVEM-abs.L-only `q3_word` pass-through. **Found a real bug via cosim**: first draft based the new gating flag on the pre-existing `is_movem` signal, which structurally excludes the indexed EA mode entirely (`f_mode` ∈ {-(An),(An)+,(An)} only) — compiled clean but produced `$204` instead of `$304` in the first test (the bd silently dropped), and notably showed reads where a MOVEM store should write, a decode-corruption symptom that helped localize the bug fast. Fixed by basing the flag on `is_movem_2ext` instead (already covers `f_mode==110` alongside the other extended modes). Genuine memory-indirect and long bd for MOVEM still degrade to the brief interpretation (same "least-wrong fallback" boundary as every other family), but `movem_ext_count` still avoids IFU desync for those cases. Verified via `tests/memind11.s` (MOVEM.L store+load through full-format word-bd indexed EA, cleanly automated, wired into `make cosim_memind`). Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 5/5, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, matching the Phase 112 baseline exactly, zero regressions — MOVEM.l's own 100%-passing Harte suite is the gate that actually proves the common brief-EA MOVEM paths are undisturbed. Stage 4's other two items (MOVE mem-to-mem dst-side, long bd/od) remain open — both substantially larger than anything done in this rollout so far. See `plan.md §Phase 119`.

120. CMP2/CHK2's own indexed EA — the first item of the user-approved 3-item post-Stage-4 follow-up plan (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`). Unlike every other family in this rollout, `eu_seq.sv`'s CMP2/CHK2 decode had **no** `f_mode==110` case arm at all (genuinely unimplemented, not brief-limited). Added one, reusing the `dyn_bit_get_Dn` deferred-register swap already proven for CHK's own indexed form (Rn only needed after the read completes). Discovered CMP2/CHK2's layout shares MOVEM's exact "q1=other data, q2=EA descriptor" shape (baseline 2 ext words) — reused `peek_fi_full_movem`/`movem_bd_words`/`movem_od_words` from Phase 119 directly, adding only `is_cmp2chk2_idx_full`/`cmp2chk2_ext_count`, and used the same `q3_word`-based bd extraction as MOVEM (not the shared `fi_bd`, which needs `is_memind_full`'s own q1/q2 swap that CMP2/CHK2 doesn't get). **Found two real bugs in the shared `dyn_bit_get_Dn` mechanism itself**, neither ever exercised before since CMP2/CHK2 is the first consumer needing a *second* memory access after the swap: (a) `cmp2_addr2_r` (second bound read's address) sampled `ex_ea` at the exact cycle `dyn_bit_get_Dn` fires (same trigger: `mem_ack`), picking up the just-swapped Rn instead of Xn — a shadow-latch first attempt fixed the full BIU cosim path but broke 3 pre-existing unit tests whose simplified memory model acks on the very first possible cycle (no earlier "pending" cycle to have latched from); real fix gates `dyn_bit_get_Dn` to exclude CMP2/CHK2's own first-read ack, deferring the swap to the second read's ack (Rn isn't needed until then anyway) — zero effect on every other `dyn_bit_get_Dn` consumer since none have a second read. (b) Moving Rn's capture to the second-read ack still registered it (`cmp2_rn_r <= rd_b_data`), but the CCR computation fires the *same* cycle, reading the pre-update (stale) value — classic same-edge non-blocking-assignment race; fixed by removing the register and feeding `rd_b_data` live into the comparison logic instead. Verified via `tests/memind12.s` (CMP2.L brief + CHK2.L full-format, both in-bounds), wired into `make cosim_memind`. Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 6/6, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, matching the Phase 112 baseline exactly, zero regressions — a particularly meaningful gate since `dyn_bit_get_Dn`'s shared gating changed, touching CHK/ALU-mem-src/dynamic-bit-ops/MOVE-mem-to-mem too. CMP2/CHK2 has zero Harte coverage, so `memind12.s` is the only direct correctness check on the new decode itself. See `plan.md §Phase 120`.

121. Long (32-bit) bd support for every family already converted in Stages 1-3 — Item 2 of the post-Stage-4 follow-up plan. Turned out much smaller than originally framed ("needs a 4th/5th extension-word data path"): `fi_bd` only ever returned non-zero for word-size bd, silently returning 0 for long bd — but every Stage 1-3 site already reads `fi_bd` unconditionally via the shared template, so fixing `fi_bd`'s own definition fixes all ~25 sites at once. For the non-indirect case, a long bd only needs the descriptor's own second word (already available) plus one more word — and `q3_word` was already wired end-to-end and unused for these families (only MOVE/MOVEM abs.L and MOVEM's own bd use it). `memind_ext_count` already correctly counted 2 words for long bd (never had a value to go with it). One-line fix: `fi_bd = (bdsz==10) ? word_val : (bdsz==11) ? {ext_data[31:16], q3_word} : 0`. Genuine memory-indirect + long bd remains unsupported (documented, same boundary as plain indirect elsewhere); MOVEM's own long-bd would need a real 4th word, also out of scope. Verified via `tests/memind13.s` (ADD.L memory-source + OR.L memory-dest RMW, both long-bd, using a large negative displacement to keep the actual bus addresses within the project's 4KB cosim memory window) — CLR.L was tried first but hit an unrelated pre-existing quirk (extra read before write on indexed-EA writes, present even for brief CLR.L) so switched to OR.L. Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 7/7, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 121`.

122. MOVE mem-to-mem dst-side full-format EA (Sub-scope A) — the third and final item of the user-approved 3-item follow-up plan, closing the memory-indirect/full-format-EA rollout begun in Phase 115. `is_move_mm`'s indexed-dst decode has ~6 case arms by source shape; scope narrowed further during design (before writing code) based on each arm's own extension-word baseline: register src has a *fixed* 1-word baseline, folding straight into the existing `mode110_ea_src`/`is_memind_full`/`fi_bd` machinery unchanged. Abs.W src and `(d16,PC)` src have a *fixed* 2-word baseline, matching MOVEM/CMP2CHK2's "q1=other data, q2=EA descriptor" shape from Phases 119/120 — reused `peek_fi_full_movem`/`movem_bd_words`/`movem_od_words` directly, adding `is_move_mm_absw_idxdst_full`/`is_move_mm_pcrel_idxdst_full` + `move_mm_idxdst_ext_count`. Imm src and abs.L src (2-word baseline, dst brief already at `q3_word`) would need a genuine 4th word — same boundary Phase 121 drew around long bd/od, out of scope. Plain-memory src has a *variable* 0-or-1-word baseline per sub-mode, entangling with the same q3 slot — deferred as higher risk, not attempted. **Delivered: 3 of ~5 originally-targeted arms** (register/abs.W/PC-rel src). Verified via `tests/memind14.s` (abs.W+PC-rel src, both writes match Musashi exactly, hand-verified due to the same benign prefetch-interleave reordering documented since Phase 115) and `tests/memind15.s` (register src, write matches exactly, hand-verified — this arm's RMW mechanism has the same pre-existing extra-read quirk as CLR.L/MOVE SR,(ea), confirmed present even in brief form). Results: `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 7/7, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, zero regressions — meaningful gate since MOVE.b/w/l/q are among the most Harte-exercised families in the corpus. **This closes the user-approved 3-item follow-up plan and the memory-indirect/full-format-EA rollout as a whole** (Phases 115-122). Remaining, documented but out of scope: MOVE mem-to-mem's imm/abs.L/plain-memory-src arms (need a genuine 4th ext word or per-sub-mode wiring); genuine memory-indirect + long bd/od for any family; MOVEM's own long-bd; the MOVE SR,(ea)/CLR-to-indexed-EA extra-read quirks (pre-existing, no correctness impact). See `plan.md §Phase 122`.

123. BERR-mid-`<X>` test coverage for the new full-format EA paths — user follow-up after Phase 122, asking whether the pipeline-stall/BERR-abort rollout (closed for all ~19 `ex_mem_stall` sources as of Phase 113/114) had any gap opened by Phases 115-122's own new decode work. `mem_abort` (`mem_berr || exc_active`) is decode-content-agnostic and none of Phases 115-122 touched it or any abort-handling branch, so no new RTL gap was expected — but it hadn't been empirically exercised for the new paths specifically. Added 3 new tests to `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv` (reusing the existing `run_berr_mid_test()`/`claim_park()` machinery unchanged): **BERR-mid-CMP2-full** (full-format CMP2, `skip_cycles=0` faulting the second/bound read — exactly the phase Phase 120's `dyn_bit_get_Dn` gating fix touches), **BERR-mid-MOVEmm-idx-absw-full** (abs.W-src indexed-dst, full-format bd via the new `q3_word` extraction, fault on the write), **BERR-mid-MOVEmm-idx-reg-full** (register-src indexed-dst, full-format bd via ordinary `fi_bd`, fault on the write past the pre-existing extra-read quirk). All 3 passed on the first run — every check (injected, BIU fault detection, exception taken, correct vector, handler completion, EU recovery) succeeded, confirming `mem_abort`'s decode-agnosticism empirically. `make test` 34/34 (`stall_fsm` now 178 checks total), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — testbench-only, no Harte re-run needed. See `plan.md §Phase 123`.

124. Closed the last known stall-coverage gap: genuine memory-indirect EA (`([bd,An],Xn,od)`, the two-level 68020+ indirection mode). Left out of Category B's own 21-of-23-source sweep since Phase 104 ("genuine encoding ambiguity" at the time, later fixed by Phase 115's `dec_memind_is_post` bug but never revisited to add the test) and never given a dedicated BERR-mid test either (the existing "BERR-mid-MOVE-mem-mem" uses plain register-indirect, a different addressing mode despite the name). Added **B-22** (Category B: verifies decode holds off correctly for the instruction's full 2-phase duration — pointer read from A0+bd, then final read from pointer+Xn — and, going further than every other B-N check, that D2 actually receives the correct value read through both indirection levels, not just "did it unstick") and **BERR-mid-Memind** (same instruction, fault injected on the second/outer read) to `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`, reusing `tests/memind2.s`'s own Musashi-verified opcode encoding. **Found and fixed 3 real bugs, all in the test, not the RTL** — this instruction had never run through this harness before: (a) `MOVEA.L #imm,An` needs a full 32-bit (2-word) immediate — an early draft packed the opcode and the imm's low word into one `rom[]` entry as if it were 1 word, desyncing every following instruction by 2 bytes; this same bug was ALSO present in all three of Phase 123's own new BERR-mid tests, which "passed" anyway since `run_berr_mid_test` only checks recovery, never a data value — fixed all 5 sites; (b) chose fresh data addresses ($4000/$4010/$4200) entirely outside this testbench's own memory model bound (`MEM_WORDS=4096` = 16KB, valid up to `0x3FFC`) — the out-of-bounds `rom[]` index silently returned garbage rather than erroring, caught by B-22's own new D2-correctness check (the first check in this file to verify actual data flow through a memory-indirect FSM, not just a marker register) reading back `4e714e71` (two NOP opcodes); traced via temporary `$display` of the FSM's own internal state, which also confirmed the stall/decode-holdoff mechanism itself was correct the whole time — fixed by moving to `$3900` (in-bounds, confirmed unused). Results: `make test` 34/34 (`stall_fsm` now 187 checks), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — testbench-only, no Harte re-run needed. **Every Category B source and every BERR-abort-fixed source now has its own dedicated test exercising the real addressing mode or instruction form it claims to** — no known gaps remain in pipeline stall/hazard coverage. See `plan.md §Phase 124`.

125. Multi-source coverage for two generic pipeline mechanisms previously backed by exactly one data point each: interrupt-mid-FSM (CAS2 only, Phase 105/108) and DSACK wait-states composing with a real FSM's own multi-beat bus cycles (TAS only, Phase 107's T4b). Added a new shared task `run_int_mid_test` (mirrors `run_berr_mid_test`'s own factoring, but ends via a genuine `RTE` back into the main instruction stream rather than `claim_park`) plus **`INT-mid-MOVEM`** and **`INT-mid-Memind`** (reusing `tests/memind2.s`'s own encoding) — both passed cleanly first try, applying Phase 124's own lessons (2-word `MOVEA.L` immediate, in-bounds addresses, registers explicitly cleared before triggering). **`Wait-states-on-MOVEM-beats`** needed real debugging: `wait_states=3` (T4b's own value) gave bit-identical `elapsed0=223`/`elapsed3=223` across three different attempts, ruling out a sequencing bug. Temporary cycle-completion tracing on the testbench's own `ds_active_r`/`ws_cnt_r` confirmed the DSACK-stretch mechanism genuinely fires (3 extra ticks counted on both of instance 2's own reads) — the real explanation is that the S-state FSM doesn't sample DSACK until several `clk_4x` ticks into a bus cycle regardless of how early it's asserted, and MOVEM's own baseline per-beat latency has enough slack to fully absorb 3 extra ticks with zero visible effect on total elapsed time — a genuine, instruction-shape-dependent absorption effect (TAS's shorter baseline apparently has less slack, which is why the same value works for T4b), not a bug anywhere. Swept `wait_states=20` (`223`→`335`, clearly visible) then settled on `wait_states=10` (`223`→`279`) for comfortable margin. Results: `make test` 34/34 (`stall_fsm` now 205 checks, was 187), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — testbench-only (all temporary debug tracing removed before commit), no Harte re-run needed. Interrupt-mid-FSM: 3 sources now (CAS2/MOVEM/memory-indirect EA). Wait-states-on-FSM-beats: 2 sources now (TAS/MOVEM). Back-to-back FSM composition remains single-source (TAS→MOVEM), not picked up this phase. See `plan.md §Phase 125`.

126. Closed the breadth gaps `docs/stalls.md`'s own "What's left" section flagged: interrupt-mid-FSM (3→7 sources: added **`INT-mid-TAS`**, **`INT-mid-MOVEP`**, **`INT-mid-CAS`**, **`INT-mid-ADDX`**, covering the RMW-lock/byte-interleaved/dual-address-predecrement shapes not yet exercised by that mechanism), wait-states-on-FSM-beats (2→4 sources: added **`WS-CAS2`** and **`WS-Memind`**, both using `wait_states=10` directly and both showing a clearly measurable delta on the first attempt, no absorption surprise this time), and back-to-back FSM composition (1→3 pairs: added **`T4c`** MOVEP→CAS and **`T4d`** memory-indirect-EA→TAS, the latter with real cross-boundary data-flow checks mirroring B-22's own rigor). **One real bug found, in the test, not the RTL**: `T4c`'s first attempt used the bare `run_and_check` task with no `decode_pc` pre-wait directly after `INT-mid-ADDX`, and measured 11 data-space bus cycles instead of the expected 6 — root-caused via bracketed cycle-completion tracing to the *previous* interrupt handler's own trailing RTE stack reads still landing (`run_int_mid_test`'s own settle-wait can return with `decode_pc` having only just crossed its threshold while RTE is still retiring — the same "`decode_pc` can be ahead of what's actually completing in EX" lesson Phase 125 hit for `WS-MOVEM`); fixed with the identical explicit `decode_pc`-gating loop, applied defensively to `T4d` too. Results: `make test` 34/34 (`stall_fsm` now 245 checks, was 205), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — testbench-only, no Harte re-run needed. None of these are exhaustive sweeps of the full ~19-23 `ex_mem_stall` source list (unlike Category I's own BERR-abort rollout) — see `docs/stalls.md` for what remains. See `plan.md §Phase 126`.

127 (Step 1-2 of an 8-step plan, in progress). Investigated the caches for a user request to comprehensively test them and found a real architectural gap, not just a testing gap: `biu_cache_if.sv` is a genuine working 16-line direct-mapped I$+D$ controller, but the I-cache side was architecturally unreachable (IFU's bus port was wired "direct to cycle_gen — no cache") and the D-cache side, while reachable, had never once been exercised with caching enabled in 126 phases (CACR resets to 0, no test ever sets it otherwise). User chose, via `EnterPlanMode`, to wire the I-cache into the real IFU path first. **Step 1**: new `rtl/biu_icache_if.sv`, interposed between the IFU and `biu_cycle_gen`'s existing `ifu_*` port inside `m68030_biu.sv` (mirrors `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own EU-facing pattern) — read-only state machine (`IC_IDLE/IC_HIT/IC_FILL_0..3/IC_DONE`), a **pure combinational bypass when `icache_en=0`** (zero added latency vs. the pre-existing direct wiring — deliberately not reusing `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own registered-hop-even-when-disabled shape, which would have added a real regression for IFU specifically), and any miss while enabled always fills all 4 words and marks the line valid regardless of CACR's IBE bit (avoiding a subtle bug noticed while reading `biu_cache_if.sv` as a template, where IBE=0 there silently skips the I-cache array update entirely). Genuine SIZ=11 pin bursts deferred to Step 8. **Step 2**: full regression with CACR left at reset (0) — `make test` 34/34, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, full 124-suite Harte sweep PASS 702142/FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly)/SKIP 281221/TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to the pre-change baseline. Strongest evidence: `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s own `$finish` timestamp (829616) was byte-for-byte identical before and after, across 245 checks of real multi-instruction sequences. See `plan.md §Phase 127` and `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md` for the full 8-step plan (Steps 3-7 remaining: I-cache correctness/timing tests, first-ever D-cache-enabled tests, combined 4-config regression sweep, pipeline-stall interaction re-check).

128 (Step 3, first test, in progress). New `tb/cache_tb.sv` (mirrors `stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s/`cosim_grp_tb.sv`'s wiring) and the first real I-cache test: a tight `DBF D0,-2` self-loop re-fetching the same instruction address across 20 passes — the first test in this project to deliberately use a taken backward branch (every other `tb/*_tb.sv` avoids them; re-executing code to observe a cache hit is the whole point here). **Found and fixed a real, reproducible RTL hang while building it** — `biu_icache_if.sv`'s `cg_req`/`cg_addr` outputs, driven purely combinationally from `case(state)` (mirroring `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own style), hung the simulation dead on the very first real linefill miss: `biu_cycle_gen` reached `ST_READ_S6` and never advanced to `S7`, confirmed genuinely stuck (not slow) across 20s/90s/180s runs producing byte-identical output. Root-caused via bisection: an equivalent D-cache-enabled read through the **untouched** `biu_cache_if.sv`→`biu_sizing_fsm`→`biu_cycle_gen` path completed cleanly, narrowing the bug to my module's *direct* connection to `biu_cycle_gen`'s raw `ifu_*` port (bypassing `biu_sizing_fsm`, per the Step 1 design). `biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own header comment supplied the answer: it exists specifically to add "one cycle of latency (registered to break combinatorial loops with cycle_gen)" for the EU port — the old direct `ifu_*`-port wiring never needed this since the raw IFU always drove it from an already-registered signal (`fetch_pend_r`); my freshly-computed combinational output reintroduced exactly that hazard on the one port that had never needed protection from it before. **Fix**: register `cg_req_r`/`cg_addr_r` in the same `always_ff` as `state`, driving the module's outputs from these registers instead of combinationally — matching `biu_sizing_fsm`'s own pattern one clock edge later. Also found while investigating: the IFU's own prefetch queue touches more than one cache line per loop pass in general (an instruction's opcode and extension word can straddle a 16-byte line boundary), so the test's first-draft expectation ("exactly 1 bus cycle total") was wrong — redesigned around the actually-correct claim: real bus activity during warm-up, then *zero* further activity once every touched line is cached. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, full Harte sweep unchanged from Phase 127's own baseline (PASS 702142/FAIL 2/SKIP 281221/TIMEOUT 0), `stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s own `$finish` timestamp still byte-identical. See `plan.md §Phase 128`. Remaining: I-2 (aliasing/eviction), I-3 (CACR flush), I-4 (self-modifying code), I-5 (BERR-mid-linefill), and Steps 4-7.

129 (Step 3, I-2). Direct-mapped aliasing/eviction test: two lines sharing one cache index (A=0x1080, B=0x1180, same `idx=8`, differing only by tag), visited via JSR/RTS in a pattern that forces eviction both directions. **Found and fixed a second real RTL bug, more significant than Phase 128's — this one corrupts results silently instead of hanging.** Symptom: A's subroutine (`CLR.L D5; ADDI.L #601,D5; RTS`) never appeared to execute, D5 stayed 0 forever, while B's identically-shaped subroutine worked every time. Root cause: `biu_icache_if.sv` has zero awareness of `pc_wr_en` (the IFU's own flush signal) — `m68030_ifu.sv`'s own protocol explicitly abandons an in-flight fetch on a flush (`fetch_pend_r<=0`, "stale data is ignored" per its own header), which was airtight in the old direct wiring (a stale ack could only ever arrive *before* `fetch_pend_r` re-armed, by construction) but Phase 127's new intermediate module breaks that invariant — it keeps chasing whatever address it latched at dispatch to completion, oblivious to `ifu_req` dropping mid-fill, and because its own `ifu_ack` sits at 0 for the *entire* multi-cycle fill (not just a brief native-ack window), the IFU's re-arm condition fires almost immediately after the flush — so by the time the stale fill finally completes, `fetch_pend_r` has *already* re-armed for the new address, and the IFU consumes the stale ack as if it were the new delivery. Confirmed byte-for-byte via direct trace: the corrupted `q[0]` value (`0x207c`) was exactly `MOVEA.L #0x1180,A0`'s own opcode — data from an unrelated, already-retired earlier fetch, still in flight through the cache module at the moment A's JSR redirect fired and immediately re-armed. **Fix**: added `same_req` (checks the *live* `ifu_addr` still maps to the in-flight transaction's own latched `idx_r`/`woff_r`/`vtag_r`) and a sticky `abandoned_r` flag set the moment `same_req` goes false mid-fill; the fill is still allowed to complete and update the cache array (harmless, matches real hardware's own inability to abort an in-progress bus cycle), but `IC_DONE` now silently drops the ack/berr instead of handing it to a requester who's moved on — replicating what `fetch_pend_r`'s own guard achieved for free in the old design. `IC_HIT` gets the same protection checking `same_req` live. Also removed `addr_r`, a write-only dead register from Step 1 that had been a red herring mid-investigation. Separately found the "hit ⇒ zero bus activity" claim (rigorous for I-1's single-line loop) couldn't be cleanly re-asserted for I-2's own A#2/B#3 hits, since I-2's controller spans multiple lines and the IFU's own legitimate readahead can touch an unvisited line while decode is busy in a subroutine — removed those 2 assertions rather than chase a false failure; I-1 already covers the claim, and I-2's own 6 data-correctness checks independently confirm every hit/miss transition loaded the right value. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, full Harte sweep unchanged (PASS 702142/FAIL 2/SKIP 281221/TIMEOUT 0), `stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s `$finish` timestamp still byte-identical — the bug and fix are scoped entirely to the flush-mid-miss interaction this phase's test is the first to exercise. See `plan.md §Phase 129`. Remaining: I-3 (CACR flush), I-4 (self-modifying code), I-5 (BERR-mid-linefill), and Steps 4-7.

130 (Step 3, I-3). CACR cache-clear operations — two fresh lines at *different* indices, `C=0x1290` (idx 9) and `D=0x1390` (idx 10), letting CACR's `CI` (bit 3, global clear) and `CEI` (bit 2, clears one index via `CAAR[7:4]`) be told apart: `CI` must miss both C and D; `CEI` aimed at C's index must miss C alone while D stays a hit. Added `emit_set_caar` (mirrors I-1's own `emit_set_cacr` codegen helper) for the CAAR write CEI needs. Clear bits are level-sensitive while held, so one MOVEC write including the bit followed immediately by a second clearing it back to just `icache_en` is enough to fully invalidate. **One test-design issue found, same shape as I-2's own A#2/B#3 finding**: the bus-activity-delta proxy for "D stayed a hit" intermittently failed — not a new bug, the same documented I-2 phenomenon (multi-line controller code + the IFU's own legitimate speculative readahead touching a not-yet-visited line while decode is busy in C's subroutine). Rather than drop the assertion the way I-2 did, replaced it with something *more* rigorous: a direct internal-state check of `u_top.u_biu.u_icache.valid_i[10]`/`tag_i[10]`, read once C's own post-CEI miss has retired (program-order-after the CEI pulse) — settles the question immune to any later readahead noise, strictly better than the bus-activity proxy ever was. Results: `make test` 35/35 (`cache` now 23 checks, was 16), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — `biu_icache_if.sv`'s cache-clear logic was already correct since Step 1 and worked first-try once the test's own proxy issue was fixed; no Harte re-run needed (the RTL this phase exercises was already Harte-swept clean in Phase 129). See `plan.md §Phase 130`. Remaining: I-4 (self-modifying code), I-5 (BERR-mid-linefill), and Steps 4-7.

131 (Step 3, I-4 + I-5 — closes Step 3). **I-4**: self-modifying code. `E=0x14B0`, warmed via one cold-miss + one hit visit, then the *CPU itself* (not the testbench) executes a real `MOVE.W #702,(A0)` overwriting E's own cached immediate operand (701→702; added `MOVE_W_IMM_A0`=`0x30BC`, hand-derived and cross-checked against the existing `MOVE.L (A0),D0` encoding). Re-visiting E with no CACR flush must still read the stale, cached 701 (backing memory independently confirmed to already hold 702 — proving the staleness is the *cache's* doing, not a failed write); a `CACR.CI` flush then makes a further visit correctly see 702. Confirms the real-68030 no-automatic-I/D-coherency contract end-to-end. **I-5**: BERR mid-linefill — the last untested `ex_mem_stall`-shaped source this rollout introduces, applying the Phase 108-114 question ("does BERR mid multi-beat-FSM actually recover?") to `biu_icache_if.sv`'s own `IC_FILL_0..3` states for the first time. `F=0x15C0`; `berr_n` driven low on F's own first linefill beat (mirroring `stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s `skip_cycles=0` convention), released the instant `exc_active` is observed; vector 2 handler sets a marker then self-parks (`BRA_SELF`, no RTE — nothing to retry after a real unrecovered fault). **Both found the identical class of bug, testbench-only, not RTL**: an earlier draft of each wrote its own `rom[]` content interleaved with (I-4) or entirely after (I-5, at file scope) `@(posedge clk_4x)`-advancing waits from earlier tests — since this file's execution model is pure NOP-fall-through with nothing to stop the DUT, it had already fetched-and-executed the still-default-NOP-filled memory before the testbench's own late `rom[]` writes landed. Fixed both by writing all affected ROM content up front, before any time-advancing wait (I-4: restructured into a pure-setup block followed by a pure-execution-watching block; I-5: moved to the top of the `initial` block alongside the boot vector). I-5 hit a *second*, distinct bug even after that fix: a `while (decode_pc < target)` gate is the same "`decode_pc` can be ahead of actual EX retirement" hazard `docs/stalls.md` already catalogs — traced to 0 wait iterations while `CLR.L D5` (the very first instruction at that address) had genuinely not yet executed; fixed by waiting on `D5===0` directly (the instruction's own real effect) instead of an address-based proxy. `biu_icache_if.sv`'s own BERR-abort path (part of Phase 129's `abandoned_r` work) handled the real fault correctly on the first genuinely-correct attempt — no RTL bug found. Results: `make test` 35/35 (`cache` now 36 checks, was 23), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed (`tb/cache_tb.sv` only) — no Harte re-run needed. **Closes Step 3 of the cache-verification plan in full**: all five I-cache correctness tests (miss/hit, aliasing/eviction, CACR flush, self-modifying code, BERR-mid-linefill) are green. See `plan.md §Phase 131`.

132 (Step 4 — closes Step 4). I-cache timing. Before writing new tests, direct idx/vtag tracing (needed to pick a fresh index for the new tests) turned up a real bug in Phase 130's own already-committed I-3 test: its C=0x1290 and D=0x1390 were documented as "deliberately different indices" but actually **both map to real cache index 9** — the test still passed only because its "D survived CEI" check was unknowingly reading an *incidental* IFU-readahead line one past D's own code (0x13A0, filler NOPs sharing D's own tag by coincidence of address arithmetic), not D's real (colliding, evicted) line. Fixed by moving D to 0x13A0 (genuinely idx 10). **T-1/T-2** (exact bus-cycle-count checks): `G=0x0800`, a self-contained 7-word 2-pass-DBF sequence packed into one 16-byte-aligned line (entered via a new JMP added to I-4's tail, avoiding a NOP-desert miss-storm), plus a separate `JSR`-called `G2=0x1800` warmed-then-revisited. First attempt measured a *combined* delta of 8, not 4 — traced to the IFU's own prefetch queue spilling into the very next (fresh) line while decode was still inside G's short sequence, the same readahead-pollution class I-1/I-2/I-3 already catalogued, now against an exact-count claim. Fixed like I-3 did: a new `idx0_ds_count`, filtered on `biu_icache_if`'s own latched `idx_r`, attributing each bus cycle to the line that actually owns it — immune to spillover into a different index. Both now cleanly assert exactly 4 for a cold miss and exactly 0 for a hit. **T-3** (macro timing): two 40-pass `DBF` loops (`H1`/`H2`, disabled vs. enabled), comparing elapsed `clk_4x` ticks via a new `sim_ticks` counter. First attempt hung (hit the hard budget) — the `MOVEA.L #imm,A0` call to H1/H2 was missing its immediate's high word (2 longwords instead of the established 3), so JSR's own opcode got silently consumed as part of the immediate; fixed to match the proven `CLR;MOVEA(imm_hi,imm_lo);JSR` idiom used elsewhere in this file. Result: disabled=3016 ticks, enabled=902 ticks (~3.3x faster). Results: `make test` 35/35 (`cache` now 45 checks, was 36), `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed (`tb/cache_tb.sv` only, including the I-3 fix) — no Harte re-run needed. See `plan.md §Phase 132`.

133 (Step 5 — closes Step 5). D-cache first-ever enabled pass — the highest-value item in this plan, since `dcache_en` (CACR bit 9) had never been set anywhere in this project's 131 prior phases before a real memory access. D-1 (deliberately designed to probe this) found a real, previously-undetectable RTL bug: `biu_cache_if.sv`'s D-cache read-miss state only ever fetches and fills ONE word slot per miss (matching real 68030 hardware's own single-longword D-cache fill, correct by design) yet marked the *whole 16-byte line* valid with one bit — so reading a *different* word offset within an already-"valid" line, one that line's own miss never touched, returned uninitialized garbage reported as a cache HIT. Confirmed via internal-state tracing (`state=1`/CI_HIT with `rdata=xxxxxxxx`). **Fixed in `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv`**: `valid_d` changed from one bit per line (`[0:15]`) to one bit per word (`[0:15][0:3]`); `dhit`/`dhit_r` now gated on `valid_d[idx][woff]`; `CI_D_MISS` sets only the word it actually filled, additionally clearing the other 3 words' own valid bits when the miss's tag genuinely differs from what's currently cached (a real tag replacement, not just an unfilled offset within the same line) so stale data from the old tag can't falsely hit later; CD/CED clear logic and reset updated to match. Two test bugs found along the way, same shape as Phase 132's own I-3 fix: every D-cache test address shared a zero low byte, so they *all* aliased on real cache index 0 regardless of "different index" comments — fixed D-3's own S address (0x2700→0x2710, genuinely idx 1) and its CAAR target; and D-4a's own "write-through-on-hit costs 0" check measured the combined write+reread delta instead of isolating the reread, inflating the expected-zero result by the write's own mandatory bus cycle — fixed with an explicit "write has retired" checkpoint. A third finding, deliberately not chased further: D-5's first design (one shared handler + register-indirect `JMP (A1)`, redirected via a controller-set register between two chained BERR injections) corrupted state (`A1` never updated across the second visit; `D5`, untouched by anything in this path, read back `0xFFFF`) — a `JMP (An)` redirect immediately following exception dispatch, chained twice, a combination this project has never exercised before (both halves are separately proven correct). Switched to two independent fixed-target handlers using `JMP_ABS_L_OP` (already proven throughout this file) with the controller rewriting the vector-2 table between faults instead — arguably more representative of real handler code anyway. Results: `make test` 35/35 (`cache` now 68 checks, was 45; `biu`'s own pre-existing D-cache unit tests still pass too), `make cosim_grp` 8/8, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (expected: Harte never enables the D-cache). See `plan.md §Phase 133`. Open: the `JMP (An)`-after-exception-dispatch anomaly, undiagnosed.

134 (Step 6 — closes Step 6). Combined 4-config Harte sweep (baseline, I$-only `HARTE_CACR=0x1`, D$-only `0x200`, both `0x201`, via a new opt-in env-var override in `gen_harte_hex.py` that injects `MOVEC D7,CACR` into each test's init code). Found and fixed **three independent, previously-latent RTL pipeline/cache-timing hazards**, none of them cache-correctness bugs in the Steps 3-5 sense — all three are pipeline races the cache's altered fetch/access latency was simply the first thing in 133 phases to expose. **Hazard 1 (STOP-vs-CCR collision)**: `stop_sr_wr_en = ex_valid && ex_is_stop && !stop_r` fires unconditionally the instant STOP reaches EX, with nothing gating it against a still-in-flight instruction's own same-cycle CCR/SR commit; `sr_wr_data`'s priority mux then silently discards the real result. Old always-uncached fetch timing made this structurally impossible; an I-cache hit on STOP's own opcode fetch closes the gap for the first time. Fixed via new `stop_wb_hazard` in `eu_seq.sv`: `dec_is_stop && !need_ext && (sr_wr_en || (ex_valid && (ex_updates_ccr || ex_is_move_sr_w || ex_is_move_ccr_w)))` — three iterations (WB-only check insufficient → generalized to the comprehensive `sr_wr_en` aggregate, +12 other SR-writing families → a "keep EX frozen" attempt caused a genuine deadlock, reverted to plain bubble-insertion) plus the `!need_ext` guard fixing a second bug this hazard's own extra stall cycle exposed (STOP's 2-word opcode+operand extension-word mechanism desyncing, misdecoding STOP's own operand as `MOVE.L (A0),-(A3)`, A3 off by -4). **Hazard 2 (internal-exception-dispatch-vs-decode race)**: `eu_priv_req`/`eu_trap_req`/`eu_illegal_req`/etc. are all bare `ex_valid && ex_is_X` pulses with nothing blocking `dec_valid`'s continued advance behind them — `m68030_exc.sv`'s actual flush (`new_pc_wr`, at `EXC_LOAD`) doesn't fire until the *end* of the whole `EXC_PUSH`/`FETCH`/`LOAD` dispatch sequence, so decode kept fetching and committing side effects (e.g. an EA predecrement) from stale/garbage bytes for the entire window. Root-caused via MOVE Dn,SR clearing S immediately before the Harte runway's own STOP (a correct Privilege Violation, architecturally) followed by decode misdecoding STOP's own leftover operand — the identical A3-off-by-4 symptom as Hazard 1's second bug, via a completely unrelated mechanism. Fixed via new `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard = ex_will_except || exc_active` (aggregating all 8 internal-exception `_req`-style triggers) in `stall_base`'s unconditional top-level OR, using plain bubble semantics (not `ex_mem_stall`'s frozen-EX shape — `m68030_exc`'s own `EXC_IDLE` case only needs a single-cycle pulse, so nothing is lost bubbling `ex_is_priv` away immediately). A first version gated on `!exc_active` (dropping the instant `exc_active` first turns on) reproduced the same corruption one cycle later — `exc_active` means only "left `EXC_IDLE`," not "flush landed" — fixed by folding `exc_active` into the same OR term instead. **Hazard 3 (three D-cache byte-lane/alignment bugs, D$-only sweep: 14216 FAIL/1998 TIMEOUT)**: all three share one root cause — `biu_cache_if.sv`'s single-32-bit-slot-per-word cache model was only ever tested (Phase 133) against same-slot-same-size accesses, never against size/alignment mismatches. (3a) `CI_D_MISS` cached `sf_rdata` fetched at the CPU's own (possibly sub-longword) request size as if it were a fully-valid longword — fixed by forcing a genuine aligned-longword bus fetch (`sf_addr={addr_r[31:2],2'b00}`, `sf_siz=2'b00`) whenever actually populating the cache, then `extract_rd()`-ing the CPU's own sub-portion for the return value. (3b) `CI_WRITE`'s cache-update wrote `wdata_r` (TOP-justified, per `eu_seq.sv`'s `eu_lane()`) directly into the slot regardless of size/alignment, corrupting unrelated bytes whenever the write wasn't 4-byte-aligned — fixed with a new `merge_wr()` function repositioning the write into the correct raw-longword byte lane and preserving the rest. (3c) A genuinely misaligned longword access (e.g. RTR's own PC-pop from SP+2, an inherent RTR/RTE stack shape) spans TWO slots, which the single-slot model can't represent at all — fixed by excluding this shape from the cache entirely (new `d_size_ok`/`d_size_ok_r` gates on `dhit`/`dhit_r` and both cache-population paths), falling back to the already-correct disabled-cache passthrough fetch. Root-caused via RTR test #0 (worst-affected: 1 PASS/4037 fails+timeouts), traced through three false leads (an_wr_en, S/M bank routing) before finding the actual culprit: `eu_regfile.sv`'s separate `wr_en && wr_sel==15` "main port" firing from garbage-decoded code at a wild PC derived from the corrupted read. **Final 4-config results**: baseline PASS 702142/FAIL 2/SKIP 281221/TIMEOUT 0 (unchanged); I$-only/D$-only/both all PASS 702134/FAIL 2/SKIP 281229/TIMEOUT 0 (the 8-test PASS→SKIP delta is the already-understood `INIT_CODE_END` widening cost, identical across all three enabled configs, not cache-specific) — FAIL=2 is the same documented ASL.b anomaly in every config, zero TIMEOUT anywhere. `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8. See `plan.md §Phase 134` for the full derivation of all three hazards.

135 (Step 7 — closes Step 7). Pipeline-stall interaction re-check. Confirmed `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`/`tb/stall_hazard_tb.sv` are unaffected by Phase 134's RTL fixes (both already pass as part of `make test`) and neither has ever referenced CACR (both implicitly run at CACR=0, every cache disabled). Added one new cache-composition test, `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit` in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`: `MOVEC D7,CACR` (icache_en=1) followed by a `DBF D0,-10` self-loop (10 passes) whose body is a same-register RAW hazard (`ADDI.L #1,D1` immediately followed by `ADD.L D1,D2`, must stall on `hazard_ex`) — only the first pass can be a genuine bus miss, every later pass must be served from an I-cache HIT. Checks the exact accumulated sum (D1=10, D2=1+2+...+10=55), not just "the loop finished," since a stale-D1 read on any cache-hit-served pass would silently produce a wrong sum rather than an obviously-broken value or a hang. **`tb/cache_tb.sv` was tried first and abandoned**: its entire test sequence is one long NOP-fall-through address stream with no jump connecting the last test (I-5) to anything after it — I-5 deliberately parks its own handler in a permanent `BRA_SELF` self-loop once satisfied, so code appended after it is simply never reached (confirmed: the new test's own "reached my code" wait timed out, and D0 read a stale value left over from an earlier test before the new code ever got a chance to run). `tb/stall_hazard_tb.sv` was ruled out structurally: its own header states it's a deliberately BIU-less `m68030_ifu`+`m68030_seq`+`m68030_eu` harness with no cache to enable at all. `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv` already has the `claim_park()`/NOP-fall-through machinery this needed, plus a full `m68030_top`. Results: 4/4 new checks pass (`stall_fsm` now 249 checks, up from 245), `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8. Testbench-only — no RTL changed, no Harte re-run needed. See `plan.md §Phase 135`.

136 (Step 8 — closes Step 8, and the cache-verification plan in full). Genuine SIZ=11 pin-level burst linefill for the I-cache, replacing Steps 1-7's own deliberately-simple "4 separate ordinary reads" placeholder. The mechanism (`biu_burst_ctrl.sv`, `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s `ST_BURST_*` states, CBREQ#/CBACK# handshake) already existed fully built and unit-tested since Phase 7 via `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own direct `eu_burst_req` tests — but `m68030_top.sv` hardwired the external `eu_burst_req` input to 0 unconditionally, so nothing in the integrated chip had ever triggered it. `rtl/biu_icache_if.sv`: `IC_FILL_0..3` replaced with `IC_BURST0` (one `ic_burst_req` at the line's base address — a full 4-beat burst populates all 4 words in one shot) plus `IC_FILL_1B/2B/3B` (individual fallback re-requests for the degraded-CBACK# case, architecturally correct but never exercised in this project's own corpus since every testbench but `biu_tb.sv`'s own dedicated tests drives CBACK# permanently asserted). **Found and fixed two more real, previously-latent RTL bugs, both invisible for the same reason: nothing had ever made the burst mechanism a genuine, concurrent participant in the integrated chip before.** **Bug 1**: `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own `eu_burst_req` port sits *above* `grant_eu`/`grant_ifu` in its idle-priority chain, bypassing `biu_arbiter.sv` entirely — muxing the I-cache's own request into that port let a burst unconditionally win the bus over a *simultaneously pending, higher-priority* ordinary EU access. Found via direct `mem_req`/`mem_ack` tracing on `tb/cache_tb.sv`'s own I-4 test: JSR's own stack push reported success but a two-instruction-later RTS read back stale data, hanging in a permanent JSR/RTS loop. Fixed in `rtl/m68030_biu.sv`: `biu_arbiter.sv`'s own `ifu_req` input now sees `ic_cg_req | ic_burst_req`, and `ic_burst_req`'s own entry into `biu_cycle_gen`'s port is additionally gated on `grant_ifu`, routing it through the same arbiter-mediated priority the ordinary `ifu_req` path already used. **Bug 2**: with Bug 1 fixed, `tb/cache_tb.sv`'s D-2 test (D-cache aliasing/eviction) surfaced a second bug — `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s unified `SP_S7` completion `case` chain (shared across every cycle type) had no exclusion for `is_burst`; a burst's own S7, landing while the arbiter's own `grant_eu` was still stale-registered `1` from an unrelated, already-completed transaction, fell through into the *ordinary* `eu_ack` path and handed back `captured_rdata` still holding a completely different, earlier access's own data — silently corrupting a D-cache miss-fill with the wrong value. Fixed with `else if (is_burst) begin end` added to the `SP_S7` chain (a deliberate no-op — burst completion is already handled entirely separately via `biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s own `eu_burst_ack`/`eu_m16_ack`, unconditional every cycle). Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (was 3). `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8. Full 4-config Harte sweep — all four **bit-identical to Phase 134's own numbers** (baseline PASS 702142/FAIL 2/SKIP 281221/TIMEOUT 0; I$-only/D$-only/both all PASS 702134/FAIL 2/SKIP 281229/TIMEOUT 0), confirming genuine pin-level bursting preserves architectural correctness across the entire ~700k-test corpus. **This closes the cache-verification plan (Phase 127) in full — all 8 steps complete.** See `plan.md §Phase 136`.

137. JMP (An)-after-exception-dispatch anomaly (Phase 133) — investigated, not reproduced, closed. Reconstructed the original failing design in a standalone scratch repro (shared BERR handler doing `ADDQ.L #1,D5 ; JMP (A1)`, controller repointing A1 between two chained fault injections) and tested it against both current RTL and a checkout of RTL from immediately before Phase 134's `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard` fix (the theory: that fix might have resolved this as a side effect) — 0/10 checks failed on either revision, first with a plain single-beat read/read pair, then rebuilt to match D-5's own real shape exactly (CACR.dcache_en enabled, fault #1 a genuine cold D-cache read-miss, fault #2 a genuine write-no-allocate-on-miss) — still 0/10 failed both times. **Conclusion: the mechanism itself was never a real RTL race; the original "earlier draft"'s corruption was almost certainly a testbench-construction artifact** (most likely the same "ROM write issued after simulated time already passed that address" bug class this project has hit and fixed at least four times before: Phase 131's I-4/I-5, Phase 126's T4c/T4d, Phase 124's MOVEA.L 2-word-immediate bug). Converted into permanent regression coverage rather than left as an open question: added **D-6** to `tb/cache_tb.sv` (the exact reconstructed mechanism, inserted into the existing D-1..D-5 program-order chain), directly refuting the original anomaly's own two symptoms — A1 correctly reads the second continuation's address (not stuck at its first value), D5 settles at exactly 2 (not 0xFFFF or other garbage). **One genuine, separate finding surfaced building D-6's own "write never reached memory" check** (unrelated to JMP (An)): traced via a temporary write-commit `$display` that this testbench's memory model has no `berr_n` awareness at all — a write's own bus cycle keeps driving its pins to natural multi-tick completion regardless of the EU having already recognized the fault and dispatched internally, in parallel (architecturally correct 68030 behavior — a bus cycle in flight can't be un-asserted mid-course; a real peripheral would need its own BERR-awareness to refuse the latch, which this simplified model never implements). Whether a same-shaped "unchanged" check reads before or after that natural completion is a genuine pre-existing race against `berr_n`'s own 2-stage synchronizer delay — D-5b's own equivalent check happens to win it, D-6b's loses it (`JMP (A1)`'s extra register-file read shifts the relative EU-vs-bus timing just enough). Not a JMP (An) or RTL bug; resolved narrowly by dropping D-6b's own "T4 unchanged" assertion with a comment documenting the finding, leaving D-4b/D-5b's own equivalent checks untouched (out of scope). Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (46 checks, was 36). `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8. No RTL changed — testbench-only, no Harte re-run needed. See `plan.md §Phase 137`.

138 (Stage 1 of 7). First stage of a 7-stage, user-approved plan (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`) closing 4 previously-out-of-scope correctness edges: MOVE mem-to-mem's remaining full-format-EA arms, genuine memory-indirect + long bd/od, MOVEM's own long-bd, and two extra-read quirks (MOVE SR,(ea), CLR-to-indexed-EA). A dedicated investigation agent read the actual code for all four before planning; key grounding fact: the "4th extension word" several fixes need is not new hardware — `m68030_ifu.sv`'s 6-word queue already exposes it via `ext34_data[15:0]` (q4), already piped end-to-end, currently consumed only by `MOVE.L #imm32,abs.L`. (A genuine hard limit — a 5th ext word, needed for long-bd+long-od memory-indirect and MOVEM's own genuine memory-indirect — does exist since the IFU's drain-shift logic tops out at `dn==5`; explicitly out of scope for the whole series.) **This stage**: `eu_seq.sv:3739-3741`'s MOVEM mode110 arm only branched on `fi_bdsz==2'b10` (word bd, via q3_word); long bd (`2'b11`) fell through to the wrong brief fallback. `movem_ext_count` (`m68030_seq.sv:220-227`) already sized long bd correctly (4 ext words) — only the eu_seq.sv value extraction was missing. Fixed by extending the ternary to also handle `fi_bdsz==2'b11` via `{q3_word, ext34_data[15:0]}` (q3_word=high half, q4=low half). Single site, no `m68030_seq.sv` change. New `tests/memind16.s` (MOVEM.L store+load through a large-negative-displacement-forced full-format long bd, same technique as `tests/memind13.s`), wired into `make cosim_memind` as `buscmp-memind16` — compared cleanly first try. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 8/8, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 138`. Stage 2 (CLR non-indexed extra-read fix) is next.

139 (Stage 2 of 7). `eu_seq.sv`'s shared NEGX/CLR/NEG/NOT/TST memory block set `dec_is_mem_rd`/`dec_is_mem_rmw` uniformly for all four write-back ops, performing a real bus read before every write — correct for NEGX/NEG/NOT but architecturally wrong for CLR (real 68020/030 CLR-to-memory is a pure write, unlike the 68000). `tests/memind13.s`'s own header already documented this phantom read as present even for plain brief-form CLR.L. Split CLR into its own decode arm: non-indexed modes now use `dec_is_mem_wr` with `dec_unit=UNIT_MOVE`/`dec_use_imm=1`/`dec_imm=0` — deliberately reusing `MOVE #imm,mem`'s own already-Harte-proven CCR path (architecturally identical to "MOVE #0,ea": Z=1, N=V=C=0) rather than routing through `UNIT_ALU`/`ALU_CLR`. Indexed EA (`f_mode==110`) keeps the RMW path, deferred to Phase 144 alongside MOVE SR,(ea). Verified CLR.b/w/l Harte suites directly first (100% each) before the full sweep. Added a bus-cycle-count test (`CLR-non-indexed-no-extra-read` in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`) proving the read is gone — first attempt bracketed on decode_pc and got a spurious `delta=0` for the `(d16,An)` case (the same "decode_pc can be ahead of EX retirement" hazard `docs/stalls.md` catalogs, here from extension-word prefetch); fixed by bracketing on a marker register's own committed value instead, which cannot race since EX retires strictly in order. Both forms now show exactly 1 bus cycle (was 2). Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 8/8, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 139`. Stage 3 (memory-indirect long-bd + word-od fix) is next.

140 (Stage 3 of 7). `eu_seq.sv`'s `fi_od` (genuine memory-indirect outer-displacement extraction) had a real, previously undiscovered aliasing bug, not just a missing feature: its `else` branch fired identically for both null bd (`fi_bdsz==01`) and long bd (`11`), always reading `ext_data[31:16]` (q2, the word right after the descriptor) — correct for null bd, but for long bd that same slot is bd's OWN high half (already consumed by `fi_bd`), so od silently aliased onto bd's own data instead of reading its real value one word further out at q4 (`ext34_data[15:0]`). Fixed by splitting the branch explicitly on `fi_bdsz`: null→q2 (unchanged), long→q4 (new). No `m68030_seq.sv` change needed — `memind_ext_count` already sized long-bd+word-od correctly (4 ext words, already gated by the existing `ext_count>=4` path); only the value extraction was wrong. Genuine indirect + long od (needing a 5th ext word, q5) remains unsupported regardless of bd size — no q5 exists (Stage 8, out of scope). New `tests/memind17.s` (genuine post-indexed memory-indirect combining a long bd with a word od for the first time, via the same large-negative-displacement technique as memind13/16), compared cleanly first try, wired into `make cosim_memind`. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions (a meaningful gate despite Harte having zero coverage of this 68020+-only mode, since `fi_od`/`fi_bd` are shared signals touching every already-converted family in this rollout). See `plan.md §Phase 140`. Stage 4 (MOVE mem-to-mem imm-src full-format EA) is next.

141 (Stage 4 of 7). MOVE mem-to-mem's imm-src arm (`MOVE #imm,(d8,An,Xn)`), deferred by Phase 122 since it needs q4. Word-layout differs by size: MOVE.L consumes both `ext_data` words for its own imm32, pushing the descriptor to `q3_word` (needing a brand-new peek reading `ifu_q3_word`'s bits directly — only WORD bd achievable there, via q4; long bd would need a real q5, out of scope); MOVE.B/W consumes only `ext_data[31:16]`, leaving the descriptor at `ext_data[15:0]` — the same q1/q2 shape Phase 122's abs.W-src/PC-rel-src arms already use, so `fi_is_full`/`fi_bdsz`/`fi_iis` are directly reusable for the check (not `fi_bd` itself, which assumes the wrong slot for the value) — both word AND long bd achievable. Added `is_move_mm_immw_idxdst_full`/`is_move_mm_imml_idxdst_wordbd` to `m68030_seq.sv`'s `ext_count` priority chain. New `tests/memind18.s` (3 instructions covering all 3 achievable cases) — **not wired into `make cosim_memind`**: this arm shares the exact same pre-existing `dec_is_mem_rmw` phantom-read quirk as MOVE SR,(ea)/MOVE Dn,indexed (unrelated to this phase's `dec_ea_offset`-only change), confirmed by isolating `BUS W` lines directly — all three EA computations and written values matched Musashi exactly. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9 (unchanged), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions — the highest-value gate in this rollout so far (MOVE.b/w/l/q are among the most heavily Harte-exercised families). See `plan.md §Phase 141`. Stage 5 (MOVE mem-to-mem abs.L-src, same new q3-peek shape) is next.

142 (Stage 5 of 7). MOVE mem-to-mem's abs.L-src arm (`MOVE (xxx).L,(d8,An,Xn)`), deferred by Phase 122. Same word-layout shape as MOVE.L imm-src (Phase 141) — abs.L's own 2-word address pushes the dst descriptor to `q3_word` — directly reused Phase 141's new `peek_fi_full_q3`/`peek_fi_bdsz_q3`/`peek_fi_iis_q3` signals rather than duplicating them. Unlike the imm-src arm, this one uses the real `move_mm` FSM (genuine src-read-then-dst-write), not the RMW trick, so it doesn't share that arm's phantom-read quirk — confirmed directly. Added `is_move_mm_absl_idxdst_wordbd` to the `ext_count` chain. New `tests/memind19.s` — first automated-compare attempt failed, but from a *different*, already-catalogued benign quirk (the prefetch-interleave reordering shared by memind/memind4/memind6/memind9/memind14, not the RMW one): the actual write (`$304<-$deadbeef`) matches Musashi byte-for-byte, only the fetch-vs-read cycle order differs. Not wired into `make cosim_memind`, same convention as those five. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9 (unchanged), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 142`. Stage 6 (MOVE mem-to-mem plain-memory-src, the hardest of the q4-reuse tier) is next.

143 (Stage 6 of 7). MOVE mem-to-mem's plain-memory-src arm (`MOVE (An)/(An)+/-(An)/(d16,An),(d8,An,Xn)`), the hardest of the 3 deferred by Phase 122. **Found and fixed a real hang, not just a value bug**: a first attempt hung the simulator outright. Root cause is a previously-latent bug in `m68030_seq.sv`'s own `eu_ext_data` packing convention — for this arm's 1-word baseline (unlike every other MOVE mem-to-mem arm in this rollout, whose baseline was already ≥2 words), a real bd pushes `ext_count` to 2+, which flips which half of `ext_data` holds q1 (the descriptor), corrupting every `fi_is_full`/`fi_bd`-based read. `is_memind_full`'s own existing q1/q2 swap already solves exactly this — but it's keyed on `f_mode==110`, a field this arm's own `f_mode` (010/011/100, the source's mode) never matches, since the descriptor here lives in the separate `f_move_dst_mode_s` field instead — the first family in 8 phases of this rollout whose descriptor position depends on that field rather than `f_mode` directly. Fixed by folding a new `is_move_mm_plainsrc_idxdst_full` into the same swap condition — zero new eu_seq.sv extraction code needed once the swap was correct (`dec_dst_ea_offset` already used the plain template). `(d16,An)`-src (2-word baseline, needs its own q3_word extraction, same shape as Phase 138/141) was unaffected by the bug and got `is_move_mm_d16src_idxdst_wordbd` (word bd only). New `tests/memind20.s` (3 instructions) — isolated each `BUS W` line first given the earlier hang, all landed at the exact expected addresses/values; the full trace then showed the same benign prefetch-interleave quirk as memind19, not wired into `make cosim_memind`. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9 (unchanged), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions — an especially meaningful gate since the fix touches `eu_ext_data`'s own shared packing formula. See `plan.md §Phase 143`. Stage 7 (indexed-EA 2-register pure-write fix — highest risk, deliberately last) is next.

144 (Stage 7 of 7 — closes the correctness-edges plan). Indexed-EA 2-register pure-write fix: MOVE SR,(ea) + CLR-mode110, both previously using the RMW "2-port trick" purely to get 2 simultaneous register reads (An+Xn), performing a real unnecessary bus read before every write. Root cause: `ex_an_base`'s own mux (`ex_is_mem_wr ? rd_b_data : rd_a_data`) and `ex_xn_val` (always `rd_b_data`) collide on the same port for any indexed write. Fixed by decoupling `ex_an_base` from `ex_is_mem_wr` specifically for the indexed case (`(ex_is_mem_wr && !ex_is_idx) ? rd_b_data : rd_a_data`) — every non-indexed write is `ex_is_idx=0`, so the formula is unchanged for them. Verified the one other existing `dec_is_mem_wr && dec_is_idx` co-occurrence in the whole file (JSR `(d8,An,Xn)`) is unaffected — its own 3 `ex_an_base` consumers all bypass it via `ex_cur_sp`/`rd_a_data`-direct paths (same for PEA). Both instructions converted from RMW to `dec_is_mem_wr`, keeping the same register layout (An→rd_a, Xn→rd_b) — write data always comes from `dec_use_imm`, never `rd_a_data`, so no collision. MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn)'s own phantom read is a structurally different 3-register problem, left permanently out of scope (would reopen `port3.md`'s already-concluded 3rd-port question). Verified CLR.b/w/l + MOVEfromSR Harte suites directly first (100% each). Added `Indexed-EA-no-extra-read` to `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv` proving both forms cost exactly 1 bus cycle (was 2) — found and fixed a test-authoring bug along the way (extension-word encoding had the L-size bit in the wrong nibble, accidentally setting full-format and zeroing the displacement; caught because the bus-cycle-count checks passed while the memory-content checks failed, isolating it to the test not the RTL). Results: `make test` 35/35 (mandatory given the shared mux change — widest blast radius in this rollout), `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. **This closes the 7-stage correctness-edges plan (Phases 138-144) in full.** See `plan.md §Phase 144`.

145. Genuine q5 (5th extension word) IFU plumbing — first stage of a new plan tackling the two items the correctness-edges rollout deliberately left out of scope (MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn)'s 3-register phantom read; anything needing a 5th extension word). The IFU's 6-word queue already correctly fills `q[5]` today, just never exposed it — added a `q5_word` output, `ext6_valid` (`q_cnt>=6`), and the missing `dn==6` drain-shift case (mirroring the existing `q3_word`/`ext34_data`/`ext5_valid` pattern exactly), threaded through `m68030_seq.sv`/`m68030_top.sv`/`m68030_eu.sv`/`eu_seq.sv`. Found and fixed a real, previously-latent gap while extending `eu_ext_valid`'s own priority formula: it topped out at `ext_count>=4 → ifu_ext5_valid`, which would have under-gated any future `ext_count=5` instruction (not exploitable yet, since nothing used it before this phase, but a real bug once Phase 146/147 do) — added an explicit `ext_count>=5 → ifu_ext6_valid` tier. One testbench (`tb/seq_ctrl_tb.sv`, using wildcard `.*` port connection) needed new signal declarations for the added ports; every other touched testbench uses explicit named-port lists that already omit `q3_word`/`ext34_data`, so needed no changes. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 9/9 (unchanged, no consumer yet), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions (expected — pure additive plumbing). Confirmed scope: this unlocks exactly 3 sites (genuine memory-indirect long-bd+long-od; MOVE.L imm-src/abs.L-src long-bd) — MOVEM's own genuine memory-indirect worst case needs a 7th queue word and stays out of scope. See `plan.md §Phase 145`. Phase 146 (memory-indirect long-bd+long-od) is next.

146. Genuine memory-indirect long-bd + long-od fix — the first real consumer of Phase 145's q5 plumbing.
