Confirmed sizing was already correct before touching anything: `m68030_seq.sv`'s `memind_od_words` already returns 2 for long od, so `memind_ext_count` already computed 5 (opcode+5=6 total words) for long-bd+long-od, exactly matching Phase 145's own new `ext_count>=5 → ifu_ext6_valid` gate — zero `m68030_seq.sv` changes needed, purely a missing value-extraction gap in `eu_seq.sv`'s `fi_od`. `fi_od` previously returned 0 unconditionally whenever od was long-sized (`fi_iis[1:0]==11`) — that case was never implemented at all (distinct from the real aliasing bug Phase 140 fixed for word od). Restructured into an explicit 3×3 split on od-size × bd-size, all 9 positions derived from first principles (bd's words occupy the slot(s) right after the descriptor; od's word(s) start right after wherever bd's own words end) — the new case: long bd → od_hi/lo at q4/q5. `fi_bd` itself needed no change. New `tests/memind21.s` (long bd `-$10000` + long od `-$20000` together, forced via the usual technique) compared cleanly first try, wired into `make cosim_memind`. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 10/10, full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. See `plan.md §Phase 146`. Phase 147 (MOVE mem-to-mem imm-src/abs.L-src long-bd via q5) is next.

147. MOVE mem-to-mem imm-src/abs.L-src long-bd via q5 — third and last item of Phase 145's q5-unlock plan. Decode fix as planned: `m68030_seq.sv` widened `is_move_mm_imml_idxdst_full`/`is_move_mm_absl_idxdst_full` (renamed from `..._wordbd`) to also accept long bd (`peek_fi_bdsz_q3[1]` instead of `==2'b10`), added shared `q3bd_words` feeding both `..._ext_count = 3'd3 + q3bd_words`; `eu_seq.sv`'s two arms gained a long-bd branch reading `{ext34_data[15:0], q5_word}`. **Found a genuine, previously-latent IFU bug this phase's own test exposed, unrelated to decode**: the prefetch queue's fetch-trigger condition (`q_cnt_d<=4`, present since long before this rollout) combined with the fixed "always 2 words per fill" shape means queue parity, once set (via the one-time `skip_first_r` catch-up after an odd-aligned branch/flush), is permanent — every later fill preserves it (1→3→5→7-capped-at-6-never-reached), so from an odd start `q_cnt` can only ever reach 5, never 6, and the trigger's own `<=4` bound never re-arms from 5 — a permanent stall, invisible before this phase since nothing previously needed the 6th queue word (Phase 146's own test happened to land at even parity by chance). Root-caused via `$display` tracing of `q3_word`/`ext34_data`/`q5_word`/`ext_valid` every cycle, showing the correct fetched bytes sitting in place forever with `ext_valid=0`. **Fixed in `rtl/m68030_ifu.sv`**: widened the fetch trigger to `q_cnt_d<=5`, added a `held_word_r`/`held_valid_r` stash pair for the resulting 1-slot-only overflow (a fetch always returns 2 words; when `q_cnt_d==5` only `q[5]` is free — keep the first word there, stash the second, inject it with zero bus cost into the first free slot on a later drain, gate new fetches on `!held_valid_r` to avoid a second overflow before the first is placed). Also found a test bug ahead of the RTL bug: `tests/memind22.s`'s first draft used unsuffixed `($800)` for the abs.L-src operand, which fits in 16 bits so vasm silently assembled it as abs.W, routing through the *older* Phase 118 arm (no long-bd support at all) instead of this phase's new code — fixed by using `$10900` (>`$FFFF`, can't fold to abs.W). Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 10/10 (unchanged — `memind22.s` has the same benign prefetch-interleave reordering quirk as memind9/14/19/20, hand-verified: both writes, `$204`←`$13572468` and `$404`←`$2468ACE0`, match Musashi/WinUAE exactly). Full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions — the highest-value gate available, since the IFU fix touches shared fill/drain logic underneath every instruction in the corpus. **This closes Phases 145-147 (the q5-unlock plan) in full.** `port3.md`'s Item 1 (MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn), the 3rd register-file read port) is next. See `plan.md §Phase 147`.

148. Added the `rd_c` 3rd register-file read port — pure plumbing, no consumer yet (first stage of `port3.md`'s Item 1). `rtl/eu_regfile.sv`: new `rd_c_sel`/`rd_c_siz`/`rd_c_data`, mirroring `rd_a`'s own combinational read block exactly, purely additive. `rtl/eu_seq.sv`: matching output/input ports added, `rd_c_sel`/`rd_c_siz` tied to constant 0 for now (dead plumbing — Phase 149 wires them to MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn)'s real Dn source register). `rtl/m68030_eu.sv`: threaded `rd_c_*` straight through between the two, entirely internal to `m68030_eu` — testbenches instantiating `m68030_eu` itself (`eu_tb.sv`/`pipeline_tb.sv`/`stall_hazard_tb.sv`) needed zero changes; only `tb/eu_seq_tb.sv`/`tb/eu_regfile_tb.sv` (which instantiate `eu_seq`/`eu_regfile` directly) needed port-list updates. Added a real RF-8 test block to `tb/eu_regfile_tb.sv` (3 checks: 3 ports reading different registers at once, byte-sized port-C read independent of A/B sizing, all three ports agreeing when reading the same register) rather than just tying off — first draft's expected values were stale (assumed D0/A0 still held their RF-1/RF-2 values, but RF-7's own generate-loop coverage overwrites them earlier in the file), a test-ordering bug, not RTL. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 10/10 (unchanged), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, confirming the new port is genuinely inert everywhere unused. Phase 149 (rewrite MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn) to use `rd_c`) is next — the functional, higher-risk half of this item. See `plan.md §Phase 148`.

149. MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn) genuine single-phase write via `rd_c` — closes `port3.md`'s Item 1 and the whole Phase 145-149 rollout. Rewrote the `dec_is_move_reg_idx_dst` arm: `dec_is_mem_wr=1` (was RMW), `dec_updates_ccr=1` (reusing CLR-indexed's own Phase 144 CCR path), new `dec_c_reg={(f_mode==3'b001),f_reg)}`/`dec_reads_c=1` sourcing the write data from `rd_c` instead of a `dyn_bit_get_Dn` deferred swap of `rd_b` post-read-ack. `rd_a`(An)/`rd_b`(Xn) unchanged. `move_result_w`'s mux collapsed to a plain `ex_is_move_reg_idx_dst ? rd_c_data : ...` (no swap-timing gate needed); `mem_wdata` gained a matching `rd_c_data`-sourced case; `ex_mem_rmw_ccr` dropped its `|| ex_is_move_reg_idx_dst` special case. **Found a genuine, previously-latent hazard-detection gap while wiring this up**: `hazard_ex`/`hazard_wb` had no 3rd-operand equivalent at all — a real RAW hazard on the new rd_c source register would have gone completely undetected; extended both formulas' three OR-clauses with a matching `dec_reads_c`/`dec_c_reg` term. `tests/memind15.s` (Phase 122's original register-source test, previously needing a `--reads-only` workaround to hide the phantom read) now compares **cleanly in full** — updated its header, wired into `make cosim_memind` as a full (non-`--reads-only`) comparison; added `tests/memind24.s` (An-source sibling, exercising `dec_c_reg`'s own is-An bit) — also clean. Results: `make test` 35/35, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12 (was 10/10), full 124-suite Harte re-run — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions — MOVE.b/w/l/q's own suites (among the most heavily Harte-exercised) independently confirmed 100%, the highest-value gate available since this touches shared CCR/hazard logic used pipeline-wide. `port3.md` updated: this is the one genuine 3rd-register-file-read-port exception found in the whole project, correcting its own prior "concluded, nothing needs a 3rd port" framing. See `plan.md §Phase 149`.

150 (Stage 0 of a 6-stage plan). Wired real MMU address translation into the live IFU/EU datapath — previously the MMU had **zero effect on any real memory access**: `m68030_top.sv` hardwired `m68030_mmu`'s general translation-request port to dead constants, so only explicit PFLUSH/PTEST/PMOVE instructions ever drove the walker/ATC/TT0-TT1, even with `TC.E=1`. Found by reading the RTL before writing any new tests, in response to the user's request to test the MMU more thoroughly toward eventual Linux support. `rtl/biu_mmu_if.sv`: new `wp` (write-protect) output mirroring `ci`; new `fault_is_berr` output distinguishing a real walk-time bus error from a purely logical fault (invalid descriptor), preventing a new synthetic fault-capture pulse from double-capturing and corrupting an already-correct real-BERR capture in `biu_exc_capture.sv` (identified and fixed before it could cause a bug, via reasoning, not a failing test). `rtl/biu_mmu_arb.sv` (new): 3-way arbiter (EXT>D>I, BIU-097) in front of `biu_mmu_if`'s single-request walker, since ordinary D-cache/EU and I-cache/IFU miss paths are now real requesters alongside the pre-existing PTEST/PMOVE EXT port. `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv`/`rtl/biu_icache_if.sv`: new `CI_XLATE`/`IC_XLATE` states entered on a cache miss when `tc_e=1`, translating the logical address before falling into the existing miss-fill states unmodified; faults route into the existing `CI_BERR` path (already asserts `eu_berr`/`ifu_berr` correctly, no new abort plumbing needed); `TC.E=0` keeps the exact pre-existing state graph structurally untaken. `rtl/m68030_biu.sv`: instantiates the arbiter, threads `tc` into both cache-if modules, muxes a synthetic one-cycle `fault_valid` pulse for the no-real-bus-error logical-fault case into `biu_exc_capture` alongside the existing real-BERR path. **Found and fixed two genuine bootstrap-ordering hazards in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`** (not RTL bugs — real 68030 firmware has the identical constraint): B-20/B-21 and BERR-mid-PTEST both enabled `TC.E=1` before configuring the transparent TT0 window, so the very next fetch needed a real walk against an unconfigured CRP; fixed by reordering TT0-before-TC and narrowing TT0 to an exact top-byte match (instead of B-20's original "any VA") so ordinary code fetches keep bypassing translation while each test's own deliberately-different-top-byte target VA still reaches the real walker. New `tb/mmu_xlate_tb.sv` (Stage 0f): a dedicated full-chip integration test proving real translation for a real instruction — `MOVEA.L #0x20001004,A0`/`MOVE.L (A0),D0` through a genuine single-level walk (`IS=5,TIA=15,PS=12`) to a different physical page, asserting the translated PA's own data lands in D0, the table-walk's descriptor read hits the exact expected address with FC=101 (BIU-083), and the actual data read hits the translated PA on the external bus — plus a `TC.E=0` control case proving translation is genuinely inert exactly as before. All 6 checks passed first try; wired into `make test`. Results: `make test` 36/36 (was 35/35), `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline — the mandatory gate proving the `TC.E=0` bypass stays truly zero-cost across the whole corpus. **Closes Stage 0 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150` for the full writeup. Stage 1 (translation fault → real exception end-to-end, including RTE-driven re-execution per BIU-152 — the most Linux-relevant behavior in the whole plan) is next.

**Current state**: 36/36 regression tests pass (`make test`). All 8 opcode groups pass vs Musashi (`make cosim_grp`). 50/50 synthetic dat-replay vectors pass (`make dat-synth`). **All 124 Harte suites confirmed** as of the last full re-run (Phase 108, after fixing both dispatch-race and BERR-hang gaps): ADD/SUB/AND/OR/EOR/CMP b/w/l all 100%; MOVE.b/w/l/q all **100%** (Phase 85/89); BCHG/BCLR/BSET/BTST all **100%** (Phase 83/88); **CHK all 100%** (Phase 86); CLR/NEG/NOT/NEGX/TST all sizes **100%** (Phase 81/87); **ASL/ASR/LSL/LSR/ROL/ROR/ROXL/ROXR all sizes 100%** except ASL.b's 2 confirmed corpus anomalies (Phase 87); **ABCD/SBCD/NBCD all 100%** (Phases 91, 101); **MULS/MULU/DIVS/DIVU all 100%** (Phases 92-94); **JMP/JSR all 100%** (Phase 95); **Scc all 100%** (Phase 96); **LEA/PEA all 100%** (Phase 97); **MOVEM.l/MOVEtoSR all 100%** (Phase 98); **RTS/RTR/RTE/TRAPV all 100%** (Phase 99); **ADDX/SUBX/MOVEP all sizes 100%** (Phase 102). **Every Harte suite in the entire corpus is now either 100% pass or a documented, confirmed non-bug.** **The 3rd-register-file-port investigation (`port3.md`) is concluded — nothing in the codebase requires it.** **The odd-restored-PC Address Error investigation (Phase 100) is concluded** — the mechanism is confirmed correct; the remaining gap is the (permanent, unfixable) exception-frame-width divergence, not a missing feature. **No known non-100% Harte results of any kind remain** except two confirmed-non-bug items: ASL.b's 2 corpus data anomalies (Phase 87) and TRAP/TRAPV-taken's permanent 68000-vs-68030 exception-frame-width divergence (Phases 99/100) — neither fixable, both fully documented. **Pipeline stall/hazard coverage (Phases 103-108, closed out Phase 124, multi-source depth added Phases 125-126)**: bus arbitration contention, RAW/CCR/autoincrement hazards (with exact cycle counts), control-transfer stall depth, all 23 of 23 multi-cycle FSM decode-holdoff sources including genuine memory-indirect EA (with exact bus-cycle counts), DSACK wait-state composition (including on FSM beats — now 4 sources: TAS/MOVEM/CAS2/memory-indirect EA), interrupt-mid-FSM (dispatch race fixed; now 7 sources: CAS2/MOVEM/memory-indirect EA/TAS/MOVEP/CAS/ADDX), BERR-mid-FSM (every `ex_mem_stall` source, including memory-indirect EA and the Phase 115-122 full-format paths), and back-to-back FSM composition (now 3 pairs: TAS→MOVEM/MOVEP→CAS/memory-indirect-EA→TAS) are now tested — the first inter-instruction pipeline coverage in the project, fully complete for every known `ex_mem_stall` source with growing multi-source depth on the generic mechanisms. See `docs/stalls.md` for the full catalog and what breadth, if any, remains.

**Architectural gap — did not exist.** Every "needs a 3rd port" diagnosis investigated across Phases 81-84 (Bucket A, most of MOVE, Bucket C, Bucket D/CHK) turned out to be missing decode or a test-harness bug, not an RTL port-count limitation — Bucket B (AND/OR/EOR/SUB/CMP/ADDA/SUBA/CMPA) already worked with the existing 2-port time-multiplex trick, and even CHK's indexed form (the one case that looked architecturally justified on paper) closed the same way once actually tried. See `port3.md` for the full analysis, kept as a reference design in case a genuine 3-simultaneous-operand case ever turns up.

**The BERR-abort rollout is now fully closed, including test coverage.** Phase 108 fixed both RTL bugs found in Phases 105-106; Phase 109 extended Gap 2's fix from 4 to 16 of ~19 `ex_mem_stall` FSM sources; Phase 113 investigated the last two (PFLUSH/PTEST) and found them already correctly handled; Phase 114 added dedicated BERR-mid-`<instruction>` tests for the 12 sources Phase 109 fixed via `mem_abort`, in the process finding and fixing a real, previously-undiscovered RTL bug (`exc_frame_valid`'s sticky-forever design meant only the *first* EU-side bus error a session ever took was ever reported — every later, independent fault was silently dropped) plus three testbench-only bugs. The interrupt dispatch race (Gap 1) is fully closed. The BERR hang (Gap 2) is fixed/confirmed, with dedicated fault-injection test coverage, for every `ex_mem_stall` source: ordinary reads/writes, TAS, MOVEM, CAS2, MOVEP, MOVE16, ADDX/ABCD/SBCD/PACK predecrement forms, BFINS, CMP2/CHK2, MOVE mem-mem, single CAS, RTR/RTE, PMOVE64, PFLUSH, and PTEST — every one now has its own BERR-mid-`<X>` test in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`. Memory-indirect EA, the one remaining `ex_mem_stall` source without a dedicated BERR-mid test, got one in Phase 124 (`BERR-mid-Memind`) — **no `ex_mem_stall` source of any kind now lacks BERR-abort test coverage.**

**Two known, real RTL gaps root-caused this session — both now fully closed.** (1) An interrupt landing on the exact cycle a newly-ready instruction launches into EX right after an FSM retires, causing silent re-execution after RTE — **fixed in Phase 108** (Gap 1). (2) A sustained bus error (BERR) during an EU-initiated access hanging the CPU forever instead of raising a Bus Error exception — **fixed for 16 of ~19 `ex_mem_stall` sources** (Phases 108-109), the remaining PFLUSH/PTEST confirmed already-correct (Phase 113), and a *third*, independent RTL bug in the same area (only the first-ever fault per session was reported, Phase 114) also fixed — no gap remains.

**Step 6's 4-config Harte sweep found and fixed three independent, previously-latent pipeline/cache hazards this session.** (1) STOP-vs-CCR `sr_wr_data` collision — a still-in-flight instruction's own CCR/SR commit landing the exact same cycle STOP reaches EX could get silently discarded; **fixed** via `stop_wb_hazard` in `eu_seq.sv`. (2) Internal-exception dispatch (priv/trap/illegal/line-A/line-F/CHK/div-by-zero/RTE-format-error) racing against continued decode for the entire multi-cycle `EXC_PUSH`/`FETCH`/`LOAD` sequence, letting garbage-decoded instructions commit register side effects before the real flush landed; **fixed** via `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard` in `eu_seq.sv`. (3) Three D-cache byte-lane/alignment bugs in `biu_cache_if.sv` (sub-longword miss-fills cached as fully-valid; write-through cache-updates ignoring byte-lane position; misaligned longword accesses spanning two cache slots with no representation in the single-slot model) — **all three fixed**. See `plan.md §Phase 134` for full derivations; all three were root-caused via direct signal tracing, not guessed at.

**The cache-verification plan (Phase 127) is now complete in full — all 8 steps done, closed out in Phase 136.** Genuine SIZ=11 pin-level bursting is now live for the I-cache (Step 8), on top of Steps 1-7's own correctness/timing/pipeline-interaction verification for both caches. `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md` (the plan file) has no further open items from its own scope.

**The 7-stage correctness-edges plan (Phases 138-144) is now complete in full.** All four items from the original request are resolved: MOVEM's own long-bd support (Phase 138); the CLR-to-memory extra-read quirk, both non-indexed (Phase 139) and indexed (Phase 144); memory-indirect long-bd + word-od, including a real `fi_od` aliasing bug (Phase 140); MOVE mem-to-mem's three remaining full-format-EA arms — imm-src (Phase 141), abs.L-src (Phase 142), plain-memory-src (Phase 143, which also found and fixed a real simulator hang from a previously-latent `eu_ext_data` packing bug); and the MOVE SR,(ea) extra-read quirk (Phase 144, same structural fix as CLR-indexed). `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md` has no further open items from its own scope.

**The q5 (5th extension word) plan (Phases 145-147) is now complete in full.** The IFU's `q[5]` output/`ext6_valid` plumbing (Phase 145) unlocked all 3 sites it was scoped for: genuine memory-indirect long-bd+long-od (Phase 146) and MOVE mem-to-mem's imm-src/abs.L-src long-bd (Phase 147). Phase 147 also found and fixed a genuine, previously-latent IFU prefetch-queue parity-lock bug (`rtl/m68030_ifu.sv`'s fetch-trigger condition could never re-arm from `q_cnt==5`, permanently stalling any instruction needing the 6th queue word once the byte stream's queue parity went odd) — fixed via a widened trigger plus a `held_word_r` stash for the resulting 1-slot overflow. MOVEM's own genuine memory-indirect (needs a 7th queue word) remains explicitly out of scope, documented in Phase 145's own writeup.

**The `port3.md` Item 1 / rd_c rollout (Phases 148-149) is now complete in full — `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md` has no further open items from its own scope.** MOVE Dn,(d8,An,Xn)'s phantom-read quirk is eliminated; the arm is a genuine single-phase write, verified via a full (non-`--reads-only`) Musashi bus-trace comparison for both the Dn-source and An-source forms, with zero Harte regressions. `port3.md` has been corrected to record this as the one genuine 3rd-register-file-read-port exception found in the whole project.

151 (Stage 1 of 6). Translation fault → real exception, end to end, including RTE-driven retry per BIU-152 — the page-fault-then-retry cycle a real kernel's fault handler depends on. Investigated via a standalone scratch probe before touching any RTL: reasoned Stage 0 should already work end-to-end since it deliberately reused the existing, mature BERR-abort/exception-dispatch machinery rather than a parallel path — verified rather than assumed, and found **two real, previously-undiscovered RTL bugs**, both invisible until now since nothing had ever driven a genuine mid-instruction EU-side bus error immediately followed by a real, already-decoded next instruction (every prior BERR-mid-`<X>` test parks into a self-loop handler instead; Harte's own single-instruction-then-STOP structure can't reach this shape at all). **Bug 1 (dispatch race)**: `mem_berr`'s own same-cycle assertion drops `ex_mem_stall` the instant the fault is detected, freeing decode to dispatch an already-decoded next instruction into EX the very same cycle — but `exc_active` takes a further cycle to assert, leaving a window where the wrong instruction launches and fully commits before the exception ever blocks it. Same hazard *class* as Phase 108's `int_ready`/`int_pending` fix and Phase 134's `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard` fix, neither of which covered `mem_berr`. Fixed in `eu_seq.sv`: `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard` extended with `mem_berr` (combinational, covers the same cycle — a first attempt using only a one-cycle-delayed registered latch was traced and found still one cycle too late) plus a new `pending_mem_berr_r` sticky flag covering the gap until `exc_active` takes over. **Bug 2 (wrong captured fault_pc)**: even with Bug 1 fixed, the dispatched frame's own captured PC pointed at the *next* instruction, not the faulting one — `fault_pc` was fed from raw `ifu_decode_pc` (decode's current position, which legitimately races ahead of a still-executing multi-cycle EX-stage instruction) for every exception type. Fix: exported eu_seq.sv's already-existing-but-unused `ex_decode_pc` (latched at the decode→EX transfer, confirmed by reading the surrounding stall/freeze logic to never get re-latched while the instruction remains in EX) as a new port, threaded to m68030_top.sv. **Not** a blanket swap, though — an interrupt's own correct resume address is genuinely "the next instruction about to decode" (real 68030 semantics), so a first attempt that switched every exception source broke `INT-mid-MOVEM`; final fix is `fault_pc = bus_err_req ? eu_ex_decode_pc : ifu_decode_pc`, only touching the mid-instruction bus-error/translation-fault path. **RTE's own wide-frame (Format $9/12-word) support turned out to already exist** (`rte_frame_extra()`/`rte_fmt_skip_r`, built during Phase 99/100 but never exercised against anything beyond Format $0 before) — zero changes needed, worked first try. Extended `tb/mmu_xlate_tb.sv` with a Phase 3: a deliberately invalid descriptor at a fresh VA, a real vector-2 handler that fixes it and RTEs, asserting the retried MOVE.L completes with the *fixed* PA's own data. Two address-choice mistakes (page-frame aliasing onto the PC boot vector; a non-page-aligned frame silently truncated by page_mask) were caught by direct signal tracing before landing. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline — critical given both fixes touch shared dispatch/fault-capture logic used by every exception type. **Closes Stage 1 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 1` for the full writeup. Stage 2 (write-protect violations, needed for copy-on-write) is next.

152 (Stage 2 of 6). Write-protect violations — a write to a WP-marked page must fault; a read must not. Checked the existing RTL before writing anything new: Stage 0's own `biu_cache_if.sv` `CI_XLATE` state was already built defensively to cover this (`if (xl_fault || (xl_wp && !rw_r)) ... xlate_fault_r <= xl_fault ? !xl_fault_is_berr : 1'b1;` — a pure WP violation takes the unconditional-fault branch, routing into the exact same synthetic-fault-pulse/`CI_BERR`/`exc_active` path Stage 1 just proved end-to-end), and `biu_mmu_if.sv`'s own `wp`/`atc_wp`/`walk_wp_r` plumbing (Stage 0a) was already complete (descriptor bit 2 → `walk_wp_r` → cached into `atc_wp[]` → surfaced again on a later ATC hit). Nothing was half-built — Stage 0 already anticipated this stage. Extended `tb/mmu_xlate_tb.sv` with a Phase 4: a fresh VA with a permanently-WP descriptor, a new non-retrying vector-2 handler (WP has nothing to fix and retry, unlike Phase 3's invalid descriptor — marks completion and parks, matching the project's established BERR-mid-`<X>` convention), reads the WP page first (must succeed, incidentally priming the ATC), then writes to the same page (must fault — vector 2, format 9, handler completion marker) — exercising the WP check against a *cached* ATC entry, not just a fresh walk. All 17 checks (Phases 1-4) passed on the first real attempt; two ROM-addressing arithmetic mistakes in the test itself (each word 2 bytes off from its real address) were caught by hand-tracing before compiling, not by a failing run. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12. No RTL changed this stage — no Harte re-run needed, Stage 1's own full sweep still stands. **Closes Stage 2 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 2` for the full writeup. Stage 3 (U/M bit hardware write-back per BIU-086) is next.

153 (Stage 3 of 6). U/M bit hardware write-back (BIU-086) — on first access, write the descriptor's Accessed (U) bit back via a real FC=101 bus write; on first write, additionally set Modified (M) the same way. Neither existed before. Researched the real 68030 short-format page descriptor's exact bit layout first (Motorola manual + Linux m68k's own `motorola_pgtable.h`, which must match real hardware since it walks real page tables), both independently confirming: bit2=WP, **bit3=U**, **bit4=M**, **bit6=CI**. This exposed a real, previously-undiscovered pre-existing bug: every leaf-descriptor read in `biu_mmu_if.sv` read CI from `mmu_rdata[3]` (actually U, an unrelated field) instead of bit 6 — fixed alongside the U/M work since it touches the same bits; its live impact was limited (Stage 0's own notes already flagged `mmu_ci` as fed from a stale, non-live value), which is presumably why nothing caught it. `biu_mmu_if.sv`: state enum widened to 4 bits, new `MS_UPDATE` state issues a real write bus cycle to the leaf descriptor's own address whenever U/M need setting, then continues on exactly where it would have gone anyway. Two data paths: a fresh walk already has the raw descriptor to OR bits into directly; a cached ATC hit doesn't (only derived pa/ci/wp were ever stored), so reconstructs the descriptor from those plus guaranteed U=1 and the new M bit (a documented simplification — doesn't preserve unrelated reserved bits, out of scope). New ATC fields `atc_m[]`/`atc_desc_addr[]` (no `atc_u[]` needed — every cached entry already has U=1 guaranteed by construction). `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own MMU port was architecturally read-only before this stage (no wdata/rw at all) — added both, wired into the `grant_mmu` cycle-mux branch; the pre-existing read-cycle default is completely unchanged, so this is structurally zero-risk for every existing walk read. Extended `tb/mmu_xlate_tb.sv` with a Phase 5: a fresh page (U=0,M=0 from the start), a read (must set U) then a write (must set M, data still lands correctly at the translated PA too) — checked at three levels: retried-instruction register result, exact pin-level bus-cycle shape of each write-back, and the descriptor's own backing memory read back directly. All 8 new checks (26 total) passed on the first real attempt. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline — the mandatory gate given this touches `biu_cycle_gen.sv`, the single most sensitive module in the project, even though Harte itself never reaches `MS_UPDATE`. **Closes Stage 3 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 3` for the full writeup. Stage 4 (MMUSR correctness per BIU-087) is next.

154 (Stage 4 of 6 — closes the MMUSR/is_ptest half of the MMU-hardening plan's original scope). MMUSR correctness (BIU-087) — replaced `biu_mmu_if.sv`'s dead `mmusr=16'h0` placeholder and `m68030_mmu.sv`'s ad hoc, spec-incorrect approximation (CI squatting on bit 7, which the spec defines as S) with the real bit layout: new `build_mmusr()` assembling T(13)/WP(12)/I(11)/M(10)/U(8)/ATC(2) from named fields (Level/N/S left 0 — diagnostic-only, lower-confidence real-hardware edge cases that don't gate any OS page-fault decision), computed per-state (`MS_ATC_HIT`/`MS_WALK_DONE`/`MS_UPDATE`'s ATC-hit-M-only path/`MS_FAULT`'s `16'h8000`-or-T+I split); `biu_mmu_arb.sv` broadcasts it to the PTEST/`EXT` port same as the pre-existing `pa`/`ci`/`wp`; `m68030_mmu.sv` simplified from two hand-rolled approximations to a single `mmusr_r <= biu_mmusr;` relay. **Found and fixed a real, previously-latent RTL timing bug via the new test** (not caught by Stage 0-3's own extensive verification, since none of it checked MMUSR bit-for-bit): `MS_WALK_A/B/C`'s own transition-into-`MS_FAULT` branches asserted `fault_r<=1'b1` *at the transition itself*, one cycle before `MS_FAULT`'s own body computes the matching `mmusr_r` classification — `m68030_mmu.sv`'s relay fires the instant it first sees `biu_fault=1` and so captured a stale MMUSR left over from the *previous* translation, exactly the class of bug the file's own `hit_r`/`walk_done_r` don't have (both are set inside their own target state's body, same cycle as their own `mmusr_r`). Root-caused via a temporary per-cycle `$display` trace showing `mmusr_out` reading one whole PTEST call behind `ptest_ack`. Fixed by removing the premature `fault_r<=1'b1` from all 7 transition-in sites (`fault_is_berr_r`'s own early assertion is untouched — it's correctly read back a cycle later inside `MS_FAULT`), leaving `MS_FAULT`'s own pre-existing assignment as the sole source, synchronizing `fault_r` with `mmusr_r` the same way `hit_r`/`walk_done_r` already were — adds exactly one cycle of latency to every fault path, invisible to every existing poll-loop-based consumer. Extended `tb/mmu_tb.sv` with MMU-9 through MMU-14 (all bit-for-bit `mmusr_out` checks against `build_mmusr()`'s own computation, replacing the old coarse "B=0" check): fresh WP-page walk, the same VA re-queried as an ATC hit (ATC bit set — confirming PTEST does load the ATC even though it must skip U/M write-back), an invalid-descriptor fault, an injected bus-error fault, and — closing the "also verify PTEST doesn't write U/M" item this session's own earlier work had left unverified — a new sticky write-cycle monitor on the walk bus proving a fresh PTEST walk issues zero write cycles, paired with a real (non-PTEST) read of a different fresh page as a positive control proving the monitor genuinely catches a real write-back when one does happen. One test-authoring arithmetic slip (not RTL) found along the way: an expected value initially confused bit 12 with bit 8 while eyeballing nibble boundaries — `build_mmusr()`'s `Level` field spans bits 6:3, straddling a nibble, so bit-by-bit derivation is required. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline — the mandatory gate given the `fault_r` fix touches `biu_mmu_if.sv`'s core FSM again, even though Harte never sets `TC.E=1` and so never reaches any of Stage 0-4's translation machinery. **Closes Stage 4 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 4` for the full writeup. Stage 5 (PLOAD, currently entirely unimplemented) is next; Stage 6 (long-format descriptors) remains flagged as possibly out of scope, to confirm with the user before Stage 5 closes.

155 (Stage 5 of 6 — closes the MMU-hardening plan's original scope, with Stage 6 still to confirm). PLOAD — the one MMU instruction with zero RTL before this stage. New `dec_is_pload`/`pload_start_r`/`pload_run_r` FSM in `eu_seq.sv` mirrors PTEST's own exactly, with one difference: `biu_is_ptest=1'b0` (a REAL walk, so U/M write-back and ATC installation happen like an ordinary access, using the caller's own `rw` argument rather than PTEST's hardcoded read) and a `pload_req && !tc_e` immediate no-op branch PTEST's own dispatch notably lacks. New `pload_req`/`va`/`fc`/`rw`/`ack` ports threaded `eu_seq.sv`→`m68030_eu.sv`→`m68030_top.sv`→`m68030_mmu.sv`, mirroring PTEST's own port set throughout. **Encoding collision caught before it shipped**: real Motorola silicon overlaps PLOAD's bit pattern with PFLUSH's own `ext_data[15:13]==001` prefix (reverse-derived from Musashi's `m68kdasm.c` disassembler, the only available reference — Harte has zero PLOAD coverage and Musashi doesn't functionally implement it either); a first attempt using that literal encoding broke `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s own already-passing B-19 (PFLUSHA) — its real `PFLUSHA_EXT=0x2000` collided with the new recognition mask exactly. Fixed by giving PLOAD its own unused `mmu_op_type` value (`011`; only `001`/`010`/`100` are used) instead of subdividing PFLUSH's own `001`, matching this codebase's own established non-overlapping-reinterpretation convention rather than real silicon's genuinely context-dependent bit scheme — flagged as lower-confidence than PFLUSH/PTEST/PMOVE's own encodings, honest given there's no external oracle to verify a literal encoding against here at all. No `m68030_seq.sv` change needed — PFLUSH/PTEST/PMOVE have no dedicated `ext_count` entry either; their shared single-extension-word baseline is already served by the same default `ifu_ext_valid` gating, which PLOAD inherits correctly for free. `tb/mmu_tb.sv`: new `do_pload()` task; MMU-15 (PLOAD on a fresh VA confirms exactly 2 walk requests then exactly 0 further requests on a subsequent access — a genuine ATC hit, plus correct PA; U pre-set on the descriptor to isolate this count from the write-back mechanism, after a first attempt without it saw 3 requests, having forgotten PLOAD's own walk *also* triggers U write-back on a truly fresh page); MMU-16 (PLOAD with `rw=0` drives a real write-back with the exact expected U+M wdata pattern, unlike PTEST which must suppress this). A real end-to-end test (a genuine PLOAD opcode fetched through `m68030_top`'s actual IFU, extending `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s B-19/20/21-style tests) was attempted to close the one gap `tb/mmu_tb.sv` can't reach (it drives `m68030_mmu.sv`'s ports directly, bypassing `eu_seq.sv`'s own decode) — it DID catch the encoding collision above, then a second placement attempt collided with a different pre-existing hazard (`0x2E10`/`0x2E20` turned out to be scratch **data** for two much-earlier BERR-mid-ABCD/PACK tests, not free code space — traced directly: `rom[0x2E1C/4]` held `0x4EE1`, not the default NOP fill). Moved to a genuinely unused address (`0x3FA0`) via an explicit JMP redirect (avoiding a multi-KB NOP walk through other tests' own data); `dec_is_pload` was confirmed firing correctly on the real fetched opcode at that point (`instr_word=0xF010`, `ext_data=0x6200`, exactly as encoded) — but instruction fetch then stalled indefinitely retrieving the extension word (I-cache `IC_BURST0` never completing, `CACR` unexpectedly reading disabled despite an earlier test enabling it and nothing disabling it again), a hang whose signature doesn't obviously implicate PLOAD's own new code and would need its own dedicated investigation. **Reverted** rather than land a half-diagnosed change — `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv` is byte-for-byte unchanged; the decode-correctness finding stands from the trace alone. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 5 of the MMU-hardening plan.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 5` for the full writeup. Stage 6 (long-format descriptors) is the only item left — flagged as possibly out of scope, confirm with the user rather than assume. The `IC_BURST0`/`CACR` hang found while building the (reverted) real-IFU PLOAD test is a candidate follow-up investigation, not a confirmed bug.

156 (Stage 6 of 6 — closes the 6-stage MMU-hardening plan in full). Long-format (8-byte) MMU descriptors — the walker's own header comment admitted the gap outright (`11=long-table(treat as table)`, silently misreading real long-format data as short-format). Asked the user directly rather than assume, since the plan flagged this as possibly out of scope (real Linux/m68k kernels typically use short-format only); user chose to implement it. **This stage carried a real risk the rest of the plan didn't**: a wrong bit-layout guess wouldn't just break one instruction (like PLOAD's own encoding uncertainty) — it would make the walker silently misinterpret real page-table data while passing this project's own self-referential tests, incompatible with actual silicon. Multiple WebSearch/WebFetch attempts against archive.org's OCR'd 68851 manual (two editions), the MC68030 manual via NXP, and a Motorola patent either 403'd or returned only table-of-contents matter (large scanned manuals' bit-diagram figures don't survive OCR well enough for a small summarizer to locate) — one patent did confirm DT's position is format-invariant and address fields are 28/24 bits, enough for a hypothesis but not enough to implement. The user then supplied additional detail that itself contained a real, load-bearing self-contradiction (same DT value listed for both short- and long-format table descriptors, with nothing telling a walker how many bytes to read) — flagged directly rather than implemented around, since guessing at exactly the ambiguous part would have defeated the whole point. The user then supplied a local copy of the actual MC68030UM.pdf (3rd edition), read directly via this session's PDF tool — Figures 9-8 through 9-19, resolving everything with high confidence: **DT=2/3 ("valid 4/8 byte") mean "the NEXT level's descriptors are short/long format," set by the PARENT descriptor (or CRP's own DT for level A) — DT=1 (page) can appear in either format, since format was already fixed by whichever ancestor pointed here.** A long descriptor's first longword mirrors the short-format status BYTE exactly (bit6=CI,bit4=M,bit3=U,bit2=WP,bits1:0=DT) shifted up 32 bits (L/U+LIMIT+S above it are unchecked, matching this project's own pre-existing short-format simplifications); the second longword is a pure address field (table: bits[31:4], page: bits[31:8], same widths as short-format). Practical consequence: DT/WP/U/M/CI are ALWAYS read from `mmu_rdata` at the same bit positions regardless of format — only the address source differs, which made the actual RTL change far smaller than the investigation preceding it. `rtl/biu_mmu_if.sv`: new `MS_WALK_LONG2` state (fetch the 2nd longword) plus `walk_long_r`/`walk_level_r`/`walk_word1_r`/`walk_word1_addr_r`/`walk_long_is_page_r`; `MS_WALK_A/B/C`'s existing page/table case arms each gained one `if (walk_long_r)` branch into `MS_WALK_LONG2`, short-format branches otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged; the existing U/M write-back mechanism (Stage 3) is reused unmodified for long-format pages, just fed from `walk_word1_r` instead of `mmu_rdata`. Genuine MMU-level indirect descriptors (DT=2/3 at the final configured level) remain explicitly out of scope, same as they already were for short-format — the existing "no next level, treat as leaf" shortcut is preserved unchanged for long format too, not bundling a second new feature into this stage. New `tb/mmu_tb.sv` MMU-18: a full long-table→long-page 2-level walk, checking the resulting PA (`0xCAFE5777`), an exact 5-bus-cycle count (2+2 reads + 1 U write-back, deliberately not pre-setting U this time so the same call also proves the write-back fires through the genuinely new `walk_word1_r`-based path), and the exact write-back wdata — all 5 checks passed on the first real attempt, directly validating the bit-layout derivation. One test-authoring mistake (not RTL): `int before, after;` collided with reserved Icarus/SVA keywords — renamed to `cnt_before`/`cnt_after`. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (long-format never activates unless `crp[33:32]==3`, which nothing in the existing corpus — captured on real 68000 hardware with no PMMU concept at all — ever sets). **Closes Stage 6 — the 6-stage MMU-hardening plan (Phase 150) is now complete in full.** See `plan.md §Phase 150 Stage 6` for the full writeup.

157 (Stage 1 of a new 5-stage gap-closure plan). Documentation fixes, no RTL. User asked for a comparison against an external web guide to find implementation gaps — that guide turned out unreliable (conflates x86 paging terminology like "CR0"/PTE-fields-P-RW-U-S-A-D with 68030 architecture, and 68000-era UDS/LDS/DTACK with the 68030's real single-DS/DSACK signals, both of which this project's own CLAUDE.md already documents as wrong for the 68030) so it wasn't used as a source — instead the real MC68030UM.pdf (already used for Phase 150 Stage 6) was read directly. **Found a real, pre-existing error in this project's own `biu_spec.md`**: BIU-058 described CPUSH/CINVA/CINVL as MC68030 cache-maintenance instructions — checking the complete alphabetized instruction table (Table 3-14, every entry ABCD→UNPK) found no such entries anywhere; they're MC68040-only (the '040 has larger, separately-managed caches needing explicit push/invalidate opcodes). The exact same chip-generation-conflation mistake as the bad web guide, just already baked into our own docs. Real MC68030 cache maintenance is entirely CACR-bit-based, already correctly implemented (Phase 130) — zero RTL gap, pure doc fix (also flagged a related Section-14-errata mention of CINVA as likely spurious for the same reason). Separately **confirmed PLOAD is real** (Table 3-14: `PLOADR`/`PLOADW (fc),(ea)`, "supervisor state then entry→ATC else TRAP" — Table 3-10's own MMU summary had just omitted it by oversight), validating Phase 150 Stage 5's work targeted a genuine instruction. Also corrected CLAUDE.md's own coprocessor-bus description: "A[15:13] encoding the primitive type" was wrong — A[15:13] is CpID (which of up to 7 coprocessors, confirmed against Figure 10-3/10-1); the response primitive code is a data value read back from the Response CIR, never encoded in the address. Three genuine gaps confirmed for the remaining stages: SRP (Supervisor Root Pointer) selection (`srp` threaded as a port but never read in `biu_mmu_if.sv`'s walker), BKPT instruction (zero decode, real opcode 0x4848-0x484F), and cpSAVE/cpRESTORE (zero decode, real privileged instructions per Table 3-14/3-9). No test/Harte re-run needed (no RTL touched). **Closes Stage 1.** See `plan.md §Phase 157 Stage 1` for the full writeup and `~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md` for the full 5-stage plan. Stage 2 (SRP selection) is next.

158 (Stage 2 of 5 — gap-closure plan). SRP (Supervisor Root Pointer) selection. `srp` was already threaded end-to-end as a port (`m68030_top.sv`→`m68030_biu.sv`→`rtl/biu_mmu_if.sv`) but the table walker never read it — every walk unconditionally used `crp` regardless of `TC.SRE`/FC2. Confirmed against the real manual (Section 9.5.2): "The translation tree with its root defined by the SRP register is selected only when SRE and FC2 are both set. Otherwise, ... CRP ... is selected." Fixed in `rtl/biu_mmu_if.sv` with an "active root" mux (`use_srp = tc[30] && fc[2]; active_root = use_srp ? srp : crp;`), applied to both the level-A base-address computation and the Phase 150 Stage 6 long/short-format check (`active_root[33:32]`, was `crp[33:32]`) — same bit position for either root per Figure 9-9. New MMU-19 in `tb/mmu_tb.sv`: same VA reachable via two distinct root pointers, each with its own valid, distinguishable page descriptor, exercising the full SRE×FC2 truth table (SRE=1/FC2=1→SRP; SRE=1/FC2=0→CRP; SRE=0/FC2=1→CRP, with an explicit PFLUSH between the first and third sub-case since both reuse FC=101 and would otherwise hit the first's own now-cached ATC entry) — all 3 sub-cases (7 checks) passed first try, a wrong-root selection would have produced an observably wrong PA rather than a coincidental match. Results: `make test` 36/36, `sim/mmu` standalone clean, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_mmu_if.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (expected: Harte never sets `TC.SRE`). **Closes Stage 2.** See `plan.md §Phase 157 Stage 2` for the full writeup. Stage 3 (BKPT) is next.

159 (Stage 3 of 5 — gap-closure plan). BKPT instruction. Real opcode `0100100001001nnn` (0x4848-0x484F, breakpoint number in bits[2:0]) had zero RTL before this stage. Read manual Section 7.4.2 directly before implementing (this rollout's own established discipline): BKPT issues a CPU Space read (FC=111, breakpoint type field A[19:16]=0, breakpoint number on A[4:2], word transfer, per Figure 7-42); DSACK'd/STERM'd returns a replacement opcode spliced into the pipeline in place of BKPT; BERR'd takes an illegal instruction exception (vector 4). A dedicated investigation compared IACK's own dedicated-8-state-block implementation against the FPU coprocessor stub's lighter `cyc_is_X_r`-discriminator pattern riding ordinary `ST_READ_S0` states — the coprocessor shape is the much closer structural template (decode-driven, caller-computed address, same DSACK-vs-BERR two-outcome shape) — and confirmed via direct trace that `instr_word` is a plain combinational IFU-prefetch-queue alias with **no override mux anywhere in the codebase**, meaning genuine live replacement-opcode substitution (correctly interacting with the IFU's own ext_count/drain logic for whatever that word turns out to need) is a separate, substantial undertaking on the scale of the memory-indirect-EA rollout (Phases 115-122) — not folded into this stage. **Scoped like the FPU coprocessor stub itself** (Phase 55: "one CPI cycle; full protocol later"): implemented the pin-accurate bus cycle and the BERR'd illegal-instruction outcome in full; the DSACK'd outcome correctly captures the replacement opcode word (proving the bus protocol/data-lane extraction) but doesn't attempt live substitution — PC just advances past BKPT normally, documented explicitly. New `dec_is_bkpt` decode (`(instr_word & 16'hFFF8) == 16'h4848`) placed after the existing ILLEGAL check — **verified safe against the recurring "earlier if-else branch swallows a narrower opcode" bug class** before relying on it: BKPT shares its `f_dn/f_dir/f_ss` fingerprint with the pre-existing PEA decode block one branch earlier, but PEA's own `f_mode>=3'b010` guard correctly excludes BKPT's `f_mode=001` (a reserved EA mode for PEA — exactly the hole real silicon uses for BKPT), confirmed via direct bit-level derivation, not trial and error. New `bkpt_start_r`/`bkpt_run_r` FSM mirrors the FPU dispatch FSM exactly; new `eu_bkpt_req/rw/siz/fc/addr/wdata`/`eu_bkpt_rdata/ack/berr` ports mirror `eu_coproc_*` end-to-end through `m68030_eu.sv`→`m68030_top.sv`→`m68030_biu.sv`→`biu_cycle_gen.sv` (new `cyc_is_bkpt_r` discriminator alongside `cyc_is_coproc_r`). The BERR'd outcome is a *late* decision (only known once `eu_bkpt_berr` arrives mid-FSM, same shape as `chk_trap`/`div_trap` — not decode-time `ex_is_illegal`) — new `bkpt_trap_w` folded into `ex_will_except`'s existing OR-list and exported as `eu_bkpt_illegal_req`, OR'd into `illegal_req` at `m68030_top.sv`, reusing the existing hazard-protected illegal-instruction dispatch path unchanged. New `tb/biu_tb.sv` tests (mirroring the coprocessor cycle's own test pair): DSACK'd (full bus-protocol check at S2/S3 + captured replacement-opcode word) and BERR'd (injected at ST_READ_S4, same pattern as the existing P4-2 ordinary-read BERR test) — 14/14 new checks passed first try. Results: `make test` 36/36, `tb/biu_tb.sv` 0 failures, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `eu_seq.sv`/`biu_cycle_gen.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (expected: BKPT is 68020+-only, zero Harte coverage). **Closes Stage 3.** See `plan.md §Phase 157 Stage 3` for the full writeup. Stage 4 (cpSAVE/cpRESTORE) is next.

160 (Stage 4 of 5 — gap-closure plan, closes the 4 required stages). cpSAVE/cpRESTORE instructions. Zero RTL before this stage. Read manual Section 10.2.3 directly before implementing: both F-line, privileged, CpID=1 (this project's only modeled coprocessor), distinguished from FPU/MOVE16's own shared `f_group`/`f_dn` fingerprint by TYPE field (`{f_dir,f_ss}`) — cpSAVE=100 (Figure 10-15), cpRESTORE=101 (Figure 10-17) — with an EA field at bits[5:0] naming the state-frame location. The real protocol (format-word CIR read, branch on not-ready/invalid/valid-with-length, transfer N bytes via a separate operand CIR loop) is genuinely complex; matched the plan's own explicit scope and the FPU stub's precedent with a one-CIR-read stub: issues a single read to the Save CIR (0x04) or Restore CIR (0x06, Figure 10-5) and completes, capturing the format word without acting on it. New `dec_is_cpsave`/`dec_is_cprestore` inserted ahead of the existing generic FPU catch-all in the F-line decode block (which would otherwise swallow both, since it matches any `{f_dir,f_ss}` for CpID=1) — both follow the existing MOVE.W#,SR privilege-check pattern (supervisor-only). New `cpsr_start_r`/`cpsr_run_r`/`cpsr_is_restore_r` FSM mirrors the FPU dispatch FSM, **sharing `eu_coproc_req`/`ack`/`berr` with the FPU stub** (architecturally correct — both are coprocessor CPU-space cycles, mutual exclusion free since only one instruction occupies EX at a time) — `eu_coproc_addr` becomes a mux, with cpSAVE/cpRESTORE using a freshly-computed, **manual-Figure-10-3-correct** address layout (`A[15:13]=CpID`, `A[4:0]=CIR select`), since this is new code. **Found, documented, did not fix**: the pre-existing FPU stub's own address layout (`A[15:13]=ppp`) doesn't actually match Figure 10-3 either — a Phase-55-era inconsistency, unrelated to this stage, flagged inline rather than silently fixed (nothing tests it against a real coprocessor). `rtl/m68030_seq.sv`: new `is_cpsave`/`is_cprestore` ext_count classifiers added to the existing 1-word/2-word OR-chains, mirroring PEA's own table style — pure word-count classification, no register/scale decode needed. New `tb/eu_seq_tb.sv` test (only a harness instantiating `eu_seq` directly can observe the new decode+FSM+address-mux, since the bus mechanism itself is shared/reused, not new BIU plumbing) drives `cpSAVE (A0)`/`cpRESTORE (A0)` directly and checks request/address/is_restore_r correctness. **Found and fixed a real, previously-latent testbench bug** while debugging the first failing run: `tb/eu_seq_tb.sv`'s own `eu_seq` instantiation never connected `mem_berr` at all — X-poisoning `ex_exc_dispatch_hazard`'s unconditional `|| mem_berr` term and therefore `stall`/`instr_ack` for **every** instruction in the file, invisible for its entire prior history since all existing tests use fixed-cycle-count waits rather than reading `instr_ack`; fixed by tying `.mem_berr(1'b0)`, matching the existing `.exc_active(1'b0)` convention. Results: `make test` 36/36 (confirms the tie-off didn't perturb this file's ~700 other lines of checks), `tb/eu_seq_tb.sv` 0 failures (6/6 new checks pass), `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `eu_seq.sv`/`m68030_seq.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (expected: zero Harte coverage for either instruction). **Closes Stage 4 — the 4 required stages of the gap-closure plan are now all complete.** See `plan.md §Phase 157 Stage 4` for the full writeup. Stage 5 (optional — MMU LIMIT/S bit + genuine indirect descriptors) remains, confirm before starting.

161 (Stage 1 of a new 8-stage cache-correctness plan). CACR bit-position fix — the foundational fix. User asked for a comparison of the cache implementation against MC68030UM.pdf Section 6; a research fork found 6 confirmed gaps, independently re-verified against the manual (Figures 6-2/6-3/6-4/6-14, §6.1.2/6.1.2.1/6.1.2.2/6.1.3/6.1.3.2/6.2/6.3.1.x) and the RTL before planning; a 7th item (CIIN/CIOUT) turned out bigger than scoped ("not yet verified" → "doesn't exist in the RTL at all") and the user chose to implement it as a full stage. This stage: Figure 6-14 (confirmed by direct read) gives CACR bits 13=WA,12=DBE,11=CD,10=CED,9=FD,8=ED,4=IBE,3=CI,2=CEI,1=FI,0=EI. `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv`'s I-cache bits were already correct; **every D-cache bit was wrong** — `dcache_en=cacr[9]` read FD (freeze) instead of ED (enable, cacr[8]); the CD/CED clear-triggers read cacr[12]/cacr[11] (really DBE/CD) instead of cacr[11]/cacr[10]. Consequence: software setting ED the textbook-correct way got a D-cache that silently never activated — invisible since Phase 133 because `tb/cache_tb.sv` encoded the identical wrong-bit assumption, so every D-cache test was self-consistently validating a fiction. Fixed both the RTL (3 bit positions) and the 5 `emit_set_cacr()` call sites in the test file that shared the bug. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (all ~68 D-cache checks still pass with the corrected bits, confirming the underlying D-cache logic itself was already right — only the CACR gating was wrong), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 1 — the foundation every later stage in this plan builds on.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 1` for the full writeup. Stage 2 (function-code bits in both cache tags) is next.

162 (Stage 2 of 8 — cache-correctness plan). Function-code bits in both cache tags. Manual §6.1.2 (p.6-6, confirmed by direct read): D-cache tag = A31-A8+FC0-2, I-cache tag = A31-A8+FC2. Both `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv`'s D-cache tag and `rtl/biu_icache_if.sv`'s I-cache tag were pure `addr[31:8]` with zero FC bits — a supervisor and user access to the same logical address could alias onto the same line and hit each other's data. **Found a deeper, chip-wide finding while threading FC into the I-cache side (documented, not fixed this stage)**: `rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own ordinary instruction-fetch path hardcodes `cyc_fc=3'b110` (Supervisor Program) for literally every instruction fetch in the chip, regardless of the CPU's real current mode — real 68030 silicon uses FC=010 (user) vs 110 (supervisor) depending on the S-bit, and nothing in this project's instruction-fetch pipeline has ever threaded that distinction through. This means the D-cache half of this fix is fully live (ordinary EU data accesses already derive FC dynamically from the S-bit via `eu_seq.sv`'s `mem_fc={sr_live[13],1'b0,1'b1}`, confirmed directly) but the I-cache half, while structurally correct and forward-compatible, can't yet discriminate real user-vs-supervisor fetches until that separate, substantial, chip-wide gap is fixed (genuinely threading S-bit-awareness through the whole instruction-fetch address/FC path — zero such input exists anywhere in it today). Widened D-cache tag to 27 bits (`{eu_fc,addr[31:8]}`), I-cache tag to 25 bits (`{ifu_fc[2],addr[31:8]}`); added a new `ifu_fc` input to `biu_icache_if.sv`, consolidating what were two independent hardcoded-FC sites (this file's own `xl_fc` literal plus `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own) into one clearly-flagged constant. Updated two existing test literals for the new tag widths (I-3's own direct `tag_i[10]` check); added a new low-risk direct internal-state check to D-1 confirming `tag_d[0]` genuinely captures FC. **A full MOVES-based instruction-level aliasing test was built and every one of its own assertions passed, but inserting it between the existing D-4b and D-5 tests caused an unrelated, unexplained timing sensitivity in the D-5a→D-5b transition further down the file** (fault counter reached a wild garbage value instead of 2) — chased for a while (ruled out leftover SFC/DFC/register state) without finding root cause; reverted rather than land something fragile for marginal extra coverage beyond the direct internal-state check, which already proves the core fix. Flagged as an open, undetermined follow-up (genuine RTL race vs. testbench artifact) rather than guessed at. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv`/`biu_icache_if.sv`/`m68030_biu.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 2.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 2` for the full writeup. Stage 3 (RMW read forced-miss for D-cache) is next.

163 (Stage 3 of 8 — cache-correctness plan). RMW read forced-miss for D-cache. Manual §6.1.2.2 (p.6-9, confirmed by direct read): "The read portion of a read-modify-write cycle is always forced to miss in the data cache" (the read's own data still populates the cache afterward — only the lookup is forced off-cache). `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv` had zero RMW-awareness in `dhit`. **Scope clarification**: this project's own `dec_is_mem_rmw` decode flag is much broader than the manual's own concept — it covers every software RMW-shaped instruction (BCHG/NEG/ADD-to-memory etc, all two ordinary unlocked bus cycles); the manual's own narrower, genuinely-bus-locked RMW concept only applies to TAS/CAS/CAS2. **Found, documented, not fixed**: `bus_lock` (`m68030_top.sv`) is declared but never driven anywhere, and the existing `mem_rmw` port only ever fires for TAS — CAS/CAS2 currently issue their own read/write as two ordinary unlocked bus cycles, not the hardware-locked RMW cycle real silicon uses for them too; a separate, deeper, pin-level-timing gap from this stage's own cache-behavior scope. New `mem_rmw_lookup` output on `eu_seq.sv` (TAS's own existing `mem_rmw` condition OR CAS's own read-phase condition OR CAS2's own two read phases, using `cas2_rd2_r` for the second), deliberately separate from `mem_rmw` itself so the existing TAS bus-lock mechanism is untouched — threaded through `m68030_eu.sv`→`m68030_top.sv`→`m68030_biu.sv`→new `biu_cache_if.sv` input, ANDed into the combinational `dhit` (sampled at dispatch) as `!mem_rmw_lookup`; `dhit_r` (used for the write-phase cache update) deliberately untouched. New `tb/cache_tb.sv` check (D-8, inserted between the existing D-1/D-2 tests, avoiding the exact insertion point that caused Stage 2's own unexplained sensitivity): an ordinary read caches an address, then `TAS (A0)` targets that same cached address — verified via exact bus-cycle count (2, not 1) that the read is genuinely forced to miss. Passed first try. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory, and meaningful here since TAS/CAS/CAS2 are Harte-covered) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 3.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 3` for the full writeup. Stage 4 (IBE gating fix + WA + DBE D-cache burst) is next.

164 (Stage 4a of 8 — cache-correctness plan). I-cache burst-enable (IBE) gating fix. Manual §6.1.3.2/Figure 6-13 (confirmed by direct re-read): CBREQ# must not be asserted when "burst filling for the cache is not enabled." A prior version of `rtl/biu_icache_if.sv`'s own header comment claimed IBE only meant "use burst pin protocol," contradicting the manual — found during this plan's own re-verification, not the original research fork. Fixed: `iburst_en=cacr[4]` gates `IC_IDLE`/`IC_XLATE`'s miss-dispatch decision between the existing `IC_BURST0` (CBREQ# asserted) and a **reinstated** `IC_SINGLE_0..3` sequence (4 genuinely separate ordinary reads, CBREQ# never asserted — distinct from the hardware-degraded case `IC_FILL_1B/2B/3B`, which still asserts CBREQ# even though CBACK# never arrives). Building this hit **two real, previously-latent bugs**: (1) a combinational-loop hazard — driving `cg_req`/`cg_addr` from `case(state)` reproduced the exact Phase 128 hazard already documented on `ic_burst_req_r`'s own declaration comment, hanging the simulator outright on the very first I-cache miss (IBE resets to 0, so every test's first miss now hits this path); fixed with new registered `cg_single_req_r`/`cg_single_addr_r`, mirroring `ic_burst_req_r`/`ic_burst_addr_r`. (2) a stale-ack race, found after fixing (1) — all 4 reads completed but returned wrong/stale data; root-caused via cycle-counted tracing to `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own `state<=state_nxt` being gated `phase_r==2'd3` (its FSM only advances 1-in-4 `clk_4x` edges, the documented "4 ticks per external bus cycle" design), so a combinational `ifu_ack` tied to `state==ST_READ_S7` genuinely reads high for 4 consecutive edges — reacting to raw `cg_ack` re-triggered on every one, racing through all 4 words in one real S7 window with 3 of 4 "acks" reading stale data. Fix: `cg_ack_rise` (already declared in the file, with a comment noting it "mirrors `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own `sf_ack_rise` technique," just not yet wired into `IC_SINGLE_0..3`) — zero gap states needed, same continuous-hold-req-and-advance-address shape `IC_FILL_1B/2B/3B` already uses successfully (which never hit this since `ic_burst_ack` is already a registered one-tick pulse from `biu_burst_ctrl.sv`, unlike raw `cg_ack`). New sticky `ic_burst_req_seen_r` monitor in `tb/cache_tb.sv`, checked at the end of I-1's own existing cold-miss warm-up (which already runs at IBE=0), proves CBREQ# is genuinely never asserted. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (69 checks, +1), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_icache_if.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (Harte's own boot sequence never sets IBE=1, so it was already exercising the — now finally correct — IBE=0 path throughout). **Closes Stage 4a.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 4a` for the full writeup. Stage 4b (write-allocate, WA) is next.

165 (Stage 4b of 8 — cache-correctness plan). Write-allocate (WA) for the D-cache. Manual §6.1.2.1/Figure 6-4 (5 worked examples, confirmed by direct re-read): WA=0 write-miss never touches the cache; WA=1 write-miss on an *aligned* long-word allocates (replace tag, write data, validate only that word slot, invalidate the other 3 — Example 4); WA=1 write-miss on a *misaligned or sub-long-word* write never writes data, only clears that word slot's own valid bit (Example 5). `cacr[13]` (WA) was a pure no-op before this stage. Fixed: new `wa_en=cacr[13]`; `rtl/biu_cache_if.sv`'s `CI_WRITE` gained an `else if (wa_en)` branch alongside the existing hit-update path, splitting on `siz_r==2'b00 && addr_r[1:0]==2'b00` (aligned long-word → full Example-4 allocate) vs. everything else (Example-5 invalidate-only). Deliberately reuses Phase 134's own `d_size_ok`/`d_size_ok_r` boundary (a misaligned long-word write spanning two word slots already bypasses the cache entirely) rather than replicating Example 2's own dual-entry cross-slot behavior — documented, not a gap needing a fix. New "D-9" test in `tb/cache_tb.sv` (aligned-write-allocate + sub-long-word-no-allocate) found and fixed **four real testbench bugs**, all confirmed via direct internal-state tracing showing `dhit=1` (genuine zero-cost hits) at every point the *measured* delta disagreed — the RTL fix itself worked correctly on the first attempt: (1) missing `rom[0x2E08/4]` pre-population, defaulting to `X` and silently exhausting the poll budget; (2) a checkpoint-omission bug measuring straight from before the write to after the re-read, folding the write's own mandatory bus cycle into the "hit costs 0" delta; (3) an unexplained cross-test timing sensitivity — inserted between D-4b and D-5 (matching this test's own original design), it desynced D-6's own later chained-fault counter (D5 read 0x321 instead of 2) despite every one of its own checks passing, the *exact* same symptom already documented and left unresolved in Stage 2's own postmortem (a different, abandoned test hit the identical downstream corruption) — resolved not by re-investigating that open question but by relocating this test to a fixed ROM address (0x0C00) reached via an explicit jump from D-6's own tail, sidestepping the fragile insertion point entirely; (4) discovered only after relocating — D-6's own coincidental leftover state left D6 already reading 0 by the time this test's own check code started polling (hardware hadn't yet reached this test's own ROM), so a naive "wait for D6==0" checkpoint matched immediately on that stale value; fixed with a genuinely 3-phase wait, first synchronizing on a 0xFFFFFFFF placeholder only this test's own code ever writes before trusting a later "D6==0" as this test's own `CLR_L_D6`. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (73 checks, +4), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 4b.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 4b` for the full writeup. Stage 4c (DBE-gated D-cache burst fill) is next — likely the largest remaining implementation stage in this plan.

166 (Stage 4c of 8 — cache-correctness plan, closes Stage 4 in full). DBE-gated D-cache burst fill. Manual §6.1.3.2 (confirmed by direct re-read): a burst-capable read-miss, with DBE set and the D-cache enabled, fills and validates the *entire* line via CBREQ#/CBACK#, not just the requested word. `cacr[12]` (DBE) was a pure no-op before this stage; every D-cache miss only ever fetched/validated the single requested word (`CI_D_MISS`, correct for the non-burst single-entry-mode case, but the burst path itself never existed). Fixed: new `dburst_en=cacr[12]`; new `dc_burst_req`/`dc_burst_addr`/`dc_burst_fc` outputs + `dc_burst_rdata0..3`/`dc_burst_beat`/`dc_burst_ack`/`dc_burst_berr` inputs on `biu_cache_if.sv`, mirroring `biu_icache_if.sv`'s own already-proven `ic_burst_*` shape exactly (Phase 127 Step 8/Phase 136) — including building the request/address as *registered* outputs from the start, reusing Stage 4a's own Phase-128-hazard lesson rather than rediscovering it. Four new states (`CI_D_BURST0`/`CI_D_FILL_1B/2B/3B`) mirror `IC_BURST0`/`IC_FILL_1B/2B/3B`: full 4-beat burst validates all four `data_d`/`valid_d[idx_r]` slots + replaces `tag_d[idx_r]` unconditionally; degraded fallback (CBACK# never asserted) individually re-requests the rest but still ends up validating the whole line. `CI_IDLE`/`CI_XLATE` dispatch gained a new `dcache_en&&dburst_en&&!mmu_ci&&d_size_ok[_r]` branch selecting `CI_D_BURST0` over `CI_D_MISS`, reusing the exact same 3-way gate (and therefore the same misaligned-longword exclusion) `CI_D_MISS`'s own cache-populate branch already uses. `rtl/m68030_biu.sv`: `dc_burst_req` joins the existing `cg_burst_req_mux` as a third tier (`eu_burst_req` > `dc_burst_req`-gated-on-`grant_eu` > `ic_burst_req`-gated-on-`grant_ifu`, matching documented EU>IFU priority); `u_arb`'s own `eu_req` widened to `sf_cyc_req|dc_burst_req`, mirroring exactly why `ifu_req` already needed the same treatment for `ic_burst_req` (Phase 127's own real starvation bug). Found and fixed a genuine `dc_burst_fc` gap while wiring the mux (unlike the I-side's own documented hardcoded-FC limitation, the D-side already threads real FC everywhere else — added `dc_burst_fc=fc_r` to avoid a *new* regression). `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own direct instantiation needed the new ports tied off; found and fixed a **pre-existing, unrelated gap** while touching it — Stage 3's own `mem_rmw_lookup` input had never been connected there at all (floating, a real X-propagation risk apparently never manifesting since this testbench's RMW tests don't exercise the D-cache lookup path). New "D-10" test (`tb/cache_tb.sv`): a fresh 16-byte line, burst-miss read, then a read at a *different*, never-independently-fetched word offset in the *same* line — must also HIT (0 bus cycles), the decisive proof only a genuine whole-line fill can produce; a new sticky `dc_burst_req_seen_r` monitor confirms the real burst port was used. Found and fixed one real testbench bug: D-10's own D6/D7 placeholder-preload was initially placed *before* `emit_set_cacr`, whose own internal scratch-register use (`MOVE.L #value,D7`) immediately clobbers a D7 placeholder written ahead of it — the check then hung its full budget waiting for a value that would never reappear; fixed by moving the placeholder writes to *after* `emit_set_cacr` (D-9's own D6 placeholder never hit this since `emit_set_cacr` never touches D6). The degraded-fallback burst path isn't reachable in this testbench (`cback_n` hardwired asserted, same pre-existing limitation the I-cache's own tests inherited) — documented, not fixed, since it mirrors the I-cache's own already-validated shape. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (78 checks, +5), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv`/`m68030_biu.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 4c — Stage 4 (IBE/WA/DBE) is now complete in full.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 4c` for the full writeup. Stage 5 (Freeze, FD/FI) is next.

167 (Stage 5 of 8 — cache-correctness plan). Freeze (FD/FI) for both caches. Manual §6.3.1.5 (D-cache, FD) + §6.3.1.10 (I-cache, FI), both confirmed by direct re-read: FD=1 means a miss (read or write) does not replace the indexed entry, but a write that *hits* still updates the entry even while frozen (D-cache's own explicit exception); FI=1 means a miss simply doesn't replace the entry (I-cache is read-only, no exception to state). `cacr[9]`/`cacr[1]` were previously a no-op/misused. **D-cache** (`biu_cache_if.sv`): new `dfreeze_en=cacr[9]`; added `&& !dfreeze_en` onto existing conditions rather than new checks — `CI_IDLE`/`CI_XLATE`'s `CI_D_BURST0` dispatch, `CI_D_MISS`'s cache-populate branch (sequential + matching output-block `sf_addr`/`sf_siz` forcing), `CI_WRITE`'s WA-driven write-miss-allocate branch; `CI_WRITE`'s write-*hit* update (`if(dhit_r)`) deliberately left completely ungated, matching the manual's exception verbatim. **I-cache** (`biu_icache_if.sv`): new `ifreeze_en=cacr[1]`; needed a genuinely new state (`IC_FROZEN_MISS`, unlike the D-side) since a frozen miss still needs to fetch data for the IFU, just without touching `data_i`/`tag_i`/`valid_i` — reuses the registered `cg_single_req_r`/`cg_single_addr_r` pair Stage 4a already established, single fetch via `cg_ack_rise` straight to `IC_DONE`. `IC_IDLE`/`IC_XLATE` dispatch gained `else if (ifreeze_en)` checked before the burst/single fallback but after the `tc_e`/`IC_XLATE` translation check; addressed via the specific requested word (`{ifu_addr[31:2],2'b00}`/`{xl_pa[31:2],2'b00}`), not the line base (no line-fill happening here). New "D-11" test (`tb/cache_tb.sv`): W6 primed unfrozen then write-hit-updates-while-frozen (value + 0-cost re-read confirm it stayed cached); W7 fresh, write-miss under FD=1|WA=1 confirms FD overrides WA (write-through lands, but re-read still costs a real bus cycle). New "I-6" test: G=0x1700 visited twice via JSR under FI=1 — both visits show real bus activity, directly contrasting with I-1's own cache-HIT-on-second-visit test. Both new tests passed cleanly on the first attempt (the placeholder-synchronization lesson from D-9/D-10 was applied proactively, and I-6's own D5 marker was confirmed safe to wait on directly without one, since it reliably holds a stale "2" from D-6's earlier test, never coincidentally 0 or 701). Confirmed by reading I-5's own ROM setup (not assumed) that I-5 never sets CACR itself, inheriting whatever the tests before it last left it at — I-6's own final restore matches D-11's, avoiding any disturbance to I-5's already-passing behavior. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (117 checks total at runtime, +10 this stage), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — both cache-if modules changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 5.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 5` for the full writeup. Stage 6 (CACR self-clearing bit readback masking) is next — expected to be the smallest remaining stage.

168 (Stage 6 of 8 — cache-correctness plan). CACR self-clearing bit readback masking. Manual §6.3.1.3/6.3.1.4/6.3.1.8/6.3.1.9 (confirmed by direct re-read): "The CD bit is always read as a zero" (identically for CED/CI/CEI). §6.3.1 itself, also re-read: bits 31-14 and 7-5 are reserved, "currently read as zeros" — a broader requirement than the plan's own original CD/CED/CI/CEI-only scope, found while re-confirming Figure 6-14's layout and folded in since it's the same masking mechanism. `rtl/eu_regfile.sv`'s `cacr_out` returned the raw last-written value unconditionally. **Deliberately not fixed at `cacr_out` itself** — that same signal is also `biu_cache_if.sv`/`biu_icache_if.sv`'s own live `cacr` input, which needs to observe the real momentary `1` software writes into CD/CED/CI/CEI to fire their own clear-trigger logic (confirmed via `grep` before touching anything — masking `cacr_out` would have permanently broken cache-clearing, a real regression caught before it shipped). Fixed instead at the *one* consumption site of the MOVEC-readback value: `eu_seq.sv`'s `ctrl_reg_rd_val` mux (`12'h002` case for `MOVEC CACR,Dn`, confirmed via `grep` to be `cacr_in`'s sole use) — `{18'h0, cacr_in[13:12], 2'b00, cacr_in[9:8], 3'h0, cacr_in[4], 2'b00, cacr_in[1:0]}` passes through only WA/DBE/FD/ED/IBE/FI/EI, forcing bits 31-14/11/10/7-5/3/2 to 0. New "D-12" test (`tb/cache_tb.sv`): writes CACR=`0x3FFF` (every bit 13:0 set), reads back via a new `MOVEC CACR,D6` (read-direction form of `emit_set_cacr()`'s own write opcode, `0x4E7A` vs `0x4E7B`) — expects `0x3313`, hand-derived and cross-checked with a standalone Python one-liner before writing the test rather than trusted by eye; passed cleanly on the first attempt. Results: `tb/cache_tb.sv` 0 failures (118 checks, +1), `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `eu_seq.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 6.** See `plan.md §Phase 158 Stage 6` for the full writeup. Stage 7 (CIIN/CIOUT pins) is next.

169 (Stage 7 of 8 — cache-correctness plan). CIIN/CIOUT pins. Confirmed via `grep` at planning time: neither pin existed anywhere in the RTL. Manual §6.1.3.1/§6.1.3.2 (confirmed by direct re-read) + p.6-9's own Figure 6-3 context (read during Stage 4b): CIIN (peripheral input) prevents a fetched entry from loading in single-entry mode, and can be asserted per-beat during burst mode; CIIN is ignored on writes. CIOUT (CPU output) tells the peripheral an access is definitely non-cacheable, and when asserted "the data cache is completely ignored, even on write cycles operating in write-allocation mode." New `ciin_n` input on `biu_config.sv`, 2-stage-synchronized exactly like every other async pin, output `ciin_s` — threaded through `m68030_top.sv`/`m68030_biu.sv` into both cache-if modules; new `ciout_n` output computed in `biu_cache_if.sv` from `mmu_ci||mem_rmw_lookup||(fc_r==3'b111)||!dcache_en` (the manual's own listed trigger conditions). **Found and deliberately did not fix a related, already-documented gap**: `mmu_ci` (used for CIOUT here) is fed from the EXT/PTEST port, not `xl_ci`/`ca_xl_ci` (the real, live, per-access MMU translation CI result) — already flagged in this project's own Phase 150-era comments as "threaded through but not yet acted on"; wiring it into real cache-inhibit behavior would be a separate, deeper MMU-integration fix, out of this stage's scope. New `ciin` input on both cache-if modules, gating whether a completed fill also *updates the cache array* — never gating the fetched *value* itself (always correctly extracted and returned regardless), since the bus request itself is already committed, at a forced size, before CIIN's own value is knowable. This needed restructuring `CI_D_MISS`'s own two-way if/else into extract-always-but-populate-conditionally — getting this wrong would have silently returned wrong-sized data to the CPU on a CIIN-blocked byte/word read, caught by reasoning through the existing structure before writing the fix. **Scope boundary, documented not fixed**: the manual's own CIIN-during-burst text is per-beat granularity; this project's burst mechanism captures a full 4-beat burst via one combined ack, so CIIN is checked once, for the whole line, at final completion — replicating true per-beat CIIN would need reworking the beat-tracking mechanism itself. I-cache gets `ciin` only (no CIOUT — the manual's own CIOUT description is D-cache-specific); checking once at `IC_SINGLE_3`'s/burst's own final completion is not a simplification for the I-side specifically, since `valid_i` is one bit per whole line, not per word. **Blast radius**: adding pins to `m68030_top.sv`'s port list touched all 12 testbenches that instantiate it directly (confirmed via `grep`) — by far the largest blast radius of any stage in this plan — applied mechanically since every one already ties off `cback_n` in the identical shape. Also found and fixed two related pre-existing gaps while touching `tb/biu_tb.sv` (instantiates `biu_config`/`biu_cache_if` directly): both `biu_config` instantiations needed `ciin_n` tied inactive, and `biu_cache_if`'s own instantiation needed `ciin`/`ciout` tied off — the same "found floating, matching Stage 4c's `mem_rmw_lookup` gap" pattern; `tb/biu_int_tb.sv` (instantiates `m68030_biu` directly) needed the same treatment. No dedicated CIIN-asserted test this stage — every testbench ties `ciin_n` permanently inactive, so the unchanged regression suite itself is the correctness gate for "the new pins don't disturb anything when inactive," the primary risk this stage's own large blast radius carried; a dedicated CIIN-mid-fill test is a reasonable follow-up.
