The user then asked to plan out pursuing this further; a new 2-track plan was drafted (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`, overwriting the completed Phase 163 plan) — Track A targets `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own `CI_IDLE->CI_WRITE` hop (grounded, narrower hypothesis), Track B targets BSR/JSR's own separate, uninvestigated redirect-latency mechanism.

190 (Phase 163 Track A, Stages A0+A1). Investigated (A0) then implemented (A1) a fast path for `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own write-dispatch case. Confirmed via direct code reading (not just the module header's own claim) that a write request never evaluates `dhit`/`ihit` at all — `CI_IDLE`'s own next-state logic branches `!eu_rw -> CI_WRITE` independently of the read-side hit-check — and found `CI_WRITE`'s only other entry point (from `CI_XLATE`, the MMU-enabled case) is structurally unreachable from a `!tc_e`-gated fast path, so no interaction risk. Found the combinational output block's own top-of-block defaults already present `addr_r`/`wdata_r`-derived values for every state including `CI_IDLE` — the only change needed was an explicit `CI_IDLE` arm presenting the *raw* `eu_addr`/`eu_wdata` (not the stale, not-yet-updated registers) the instant `eu_req && !eu_rw && !tc_e`, mirroring `biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own already-proven `SS_IDLE` fast path exactly. Reasoned through the one race risk (`sf_ack` arriving before `CI_IDLE`'s own registered catch-up to `CI_WRITE`) and confirmed it's structurally impossible (a real bus cycle takes ≥8 ticks; the catch-up takes 1). **Result confirmed via controlled A/B measurement** (`git stash`/`pop` on just this file, rebuilding between runs) at the tick level, not the coarser `clocks=` figure: every genuine RMW-shaped test improved by exactly 2 ticks (0.5 clock) — real and consistent, but below the survey's own integer-clock rounding granularity, so it doesn't show as a visible change in the coarse report. `a4_cmpm` (no write phase) and `a6_bsr`/`a6_jsr` (Track B's own separate mechanism) correctly showed zero change, confirming the fix's scope is exactly as intended. The remaining piece of the original 4-tick hand-off chain (`biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own `SS_DONE→SS_IDLE` hop) is not addressed — out of Track A's own stated scope. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv` is the single largest, most heavily-relied-upon module either track could touch) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track A, Stage A0+A1` for the full writeup.

191 (Phase 163 Track B, Stage B0 — investigation only, no RTL change; closes this plan). Traced `a6_bsr` (`pc_wr_en`/`decode_pc`/`fetch_pend_r`/`ifu_req`/`biu_cycle_gen`'s own state, temporary hierarchical `$display`s, removed before committing) to determine whether BSR/JSR's own ~1.78x gap is the same mechanism Track A fixed or something genuinely different. **Found it's mostly the same underlying chain, just exposed differently, not a separate mechanism**: `eu_seq.sv`'s own `branch_taken`/`ex_bsr_taken` is purely combinational off `mem_ack` (`ex_bsr_taken = ex_valid && ex_is_bsr && mem_ack`, zero extra registered EU-side delay) — so the observed 4-tick gap between the push's own write completing and the redirect firing is entirely the *ack-propagation* delay through the same `biu_cache_if.sv`→`biu_sizing_fsm.sv`→`biu_cycle_gen.sv` chain Stages 2/3 and Track A already characterized, just visible here because `branch_taken` depends on it directly and immediately triggers the redirect — for ordinary instructions this same delay is silently absorbed into the already-budgeted 2-cycle EX→WB baseline (Part D's own established figure) and never shows up. **Confirmed empirically that Track A's own fix doesn't help this case at all** (zero tick-level change for `a6_bsr`/`a6_jsr` in Track A's own A/B measurement) — Track A targeted the *dispatch* side (skipping `CI_IDLE`'s extra hop for a follow-up request), while this is the *ack-propagation* side (how long `CI_DONE`/`mem_ack` itself takes to first appear after a write completes) — a different segment of the same general chain. After the redirect fires, a second, smaller, separately-real cost follows (`m68030_ifu.sv`'s own flush+re-arm, then a fresh S-state sequence from `ST_IDLE`) — largely genuine S0/S1/S2 bus-protocol setup time, not investigated to the same depth given the ack-propagation finding is the larger, more novel piece. Also confirmed the I-cache side (`biu_icache_if.sv`) is already a zero-latency combinational bypass whenever `icache_en=0` (Phase 127's own documented design, confirmed via its own header/`IC_IDLE` comment) — the timing suite never enables it, so there's no analogous `biu_icache_if.sv`-side fix available the way there was for `biu_cache_if.sv`. **No fix proposed this stage**: reducing the ack-propagation delay would mean compressing `biu_cycle_gen`'s own already-calibrated S6/S7 timing, `biu_sizing_fsm`'s own ack-pulse shape (relied on elsewhere), and `biu_cache_if`'s own registered `CI_DONE` pickup — none of which has the same narrow, already-proven fix shape Track A's dispatch-side fix had. No RTL changed; `make test` 36/36 confirms the tree is clean. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track B, Stage B0` for the full writeup.

**This closes the Phase 163 bus-touching-gap-closure plan (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`) in full.** Track A (Stages A0+A1) delivered a real, verified, if sub-clock-granularity, improvement to every RMW-shaped bus-touching instruction, with zero correctness regression. Track B (Stage B0) corrected the plan's own original hypothesis — BSR/JSR's gap is substantially the same ack-propagation-chain phenomenon already characterized in Stages 2/3, not a separate IFU mechanism — and found it does not currently have a narrow, low-risk fix shape, so no RTL change was made there. **Overall bus-touching timing accuracy across the whole Phase 163 investigation**: 2.37x (original, flawed measurement) → 1.82x (Stage 0, corrected measurement) → 1.61x (Stage 1, ext_valid fix) → 1.29x (Stage 3, arbiter fix) → Track A's own further real-but-sub-granularity improvement on top. Register-only accuracy (Phase 162 Part D) is already essentially exact. Any further reduction of the ack-propagation delay itself (the piece Track B found but didn't fix) would need a new, dedicated investigation into `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s/`biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own completion-side timing, not scoped by this plan.

A follow-up investigation past Track A/B's own close traced the AS-rise-to-mem_ack ack-propagation path precisely and found 2 of its 4 ticks are genuine S6/S7 pin timing (Phase 160's own calibrated protocol, not touchable) and 2 are avoidable internal bookkeeping (`biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own `SS_ACTIVE->SS_DONE` transition; `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own transition into `CI_DONE`) — both investigated and found structurally safe to collapse in principle. A new user-approved 2-track plan (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`, overwriting the completed Phase 163 plan) stages the smaller, single-site `biu_sizing_fsm.sv` fix (Track C) ahead of the larger, 5-entry-point `biu_cache_if.sv` fix (Track D, staged D0/D1/D2 by risk/value).

192 (Phase 163 Track C, Stages C0+C1). Implemented the `biu_sizing_fsm.sv` SS_DONE collapse: a combinational fast path (`ss_active_fast_done = (sf==SS_ACTIVE) && cyc_ack_edge && !needs_more(sf_siz, cyc_port_dsack)`) presents `eu_ack`/`eu_rdata` one tick earlier than the old registered `sf==SS_DONE` path, computing `eu_rdata` the same way `SS_DONE` itself already did (`merge_rdata(sf_accum, cyc_rdata, ...)`), using only signals already valid the same cycle. **A first attempt OR'd the fast path with the old `sf==SS_DONE` term as a "harmless fallback" — `make test` caught a real bug**: since `SS_DONE` is reached exactly one cycle after the fast path fires, OR'ing the two makes `eu_ack` assert on two consecutive ticks. `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own `sf_ack_rise` edge-detector absorbs this harmlessly, but `biu_multiop_fsm.sv`'s own `sf_eu_ack` consumer (drives MOVEP/MOVEM multi-beat transfers) checks `if (sf_eu_ack)` with no edge-detection — correct only because `sf_eu_req` stays continuously asserted across a multi-byte transfer and the old ack was reliably 1-tick-wide — so the double pulse silently advanced `mo_idx`/`cur_addr` twice per byte, corrupting `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own pre-existing MOVEP dynamic-sizing test (`rdata1`/`rdata3` mismatches). **Fix**: `eu_ack` is driven solely by the new fast-path condition, not OR'd with the old term — the fast path fully supersedes `SS_DONE`'s own old role, so it's the sole source, not a fallback; the registered `SS_ACTIVE->SS_DONE->SS_IDLE` state path itself is left completely unchanged, still driving `cyc_req`/next-state. **Result, confirmed via controlled A/B tick-level measurement**: `a4_cmpm` improved 46→44 ticks and `a7_trap_n`/`a7_illegal` each improved 88→84 ticks (two independent writes each benefiting once) — but every RMW-shaped test Track A improved, and `a6_bsr`/`a6_jsr`, showed **zero** net change. Investigated directly via joint hierarchical tracing rather than assuming the fix didn't apply: confirmed it genuinely works as designed in both cases (ack-propagation now 3 ticks not 4; BSR's own `pc_wr_en` now fires at tick 115 not 116) but the freed tick is fully **absorbed** downstream by a different, untouched bottleneck — `biu_cache_if.sv`'s own remaining `CI_IDLE->CI_WRITE` hop and `biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own `SS_IDLE->SS_ACTIVE` hand-off for RMW cases; `m68030_ifu.sv`'s own flush/redirect sequence reaching the same absolute tick regardless for BSR/JSR — the same "absorption" phenomenon already documented elsewhere in this project (e.g. Phase 125's `WS-MOVEM` finding). As with Track A, every improvement here is below `scripts/b_final_clock_survey.py`'s own integer-clock reporting granularity. Results: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_sizing_fsm.sv` sits on every single EU bus transaction of any kind) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track C, Stage C0+C1` for the full writeup.

193 (Phase 163 Track D, Stage D0). Implemented the `biu_cache_if.sv` `CI_WRITE` fast path — the highest-value, lowest-complexity of `CI_DONE`'s 5 entry points, per the plan's own staging (no cache-hit lookup, no `extract_rd()` sizing, just a write-through/write-allocate side effect using already-latched `dhit_r`/`wa_en`/`addr_r`/`wdata_r`/`siz_r`). Unlike Track C, this stage removes the extra hop entirely rather than adding a parallel fast path onto an unchanged registered route: `CI_WRITE`'s own `always_ff` now transitions directly `state <= CI_IDLE` on `sf_ack_rise` (was `<= CI_DONE`), and the output block's `CI_WRITE` case arm gains `if (sf_ack_rise) eu_ack = 1'b1;` as its sole source from the start — applying Track C's own double-pulse lesson directly instead of repeating it (leaving `CI_WRITE`'s transition pointed at `CI_DONE`, which unconditionally asserts `eu_ack=1` whenever reached and is shared by 4 other untouched entry points, would have reproduced the exact same bug one cycle later). Safe because `CI_IDLE`'s own next-state logic is purely combinational off live inputs with no dependency on how it was reached — already proven by `CI_BERR`'s own pre-existing identical direct-to-`CI_IDLE` return path. `eu_rdata` stays at its default `32'h0` (a write never returns read data; the old path returned a stale, never-written `fill_rdata_r` anyway). **Result, confirmed via controlled A/B tick-level measurement from Track C's own baseline**: `a6_bsr`/`a6_jsr` both improved 66→64 ticks — the SAME redirect Track B found blocked on ack-propagation delay and that Track C's own read-side fix left completely unmoved (absorbed by `m68030_ifu.sv`'s flush/redirect sequence reaching an identical absolute tick regardless). Since BSR/JSR push their own return address via a write, stacking a second tick off the write-side ack-propagation chain (Track C's `biu_sizing_fsm.sv` fix + this stage's `biu_cache_if.sv` fix together) broke through the floor that absorbed Track C's fix alone — direct, tick-level proof that Track C's own "absorption" finding was partial saturation, not an unmovable wall. Every RMW-shaped test and `a7_trap_n`/`a7_illegal` showed zero further change this stage (their own read-miss side, `CI_D_MISS`, is Stage D1's scope, not yet touched); `a4_cmpm` (no write phase) correctly unaffected. Results: `make test` 36/36 (clean on the first attempt), `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `biu_cache_if.sv` is the single largest, most heavily-relied-upon module either track touches) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track D, Stage D0` for the full writeup.

194 (Phase 163 Track D, Stage D1). Implemented the `biu_cache_if.sv` `CI_D_MISS` fast path — the D-cache read-miss/cache-disabled-passthrough path every ordinary EU read dispatches through (confirmed by reading the code: `CI_D_MISS` is reached regardless of whether `dcache_en` is set, not just on a genuine cache-populate path). Same shape as Stage D0: `CI_D_MISS`'s own `always_ff` now transitions directly to `CI_IDLE` on `sf_ack_rise` for both of its own branches, cache-array side effects (`data_d`/`tag_d`/`valid_d`, `fill_rdata_r`) stay on their existing registered schedule unchanged; the output block gains a combinational fast path computing `eu_rdata` the same way (`extract_rd(sf_rdata, siz_r, addr_r[1:0])` when cache-populate applies, plain `sf_rdata` otherwise — `ciin` deliberately excluded, matching the existing comment that it only gates the array side effect, never the returned value). `make test`/`cosim_grp`/`cosim_memind`/full Harte sweep all clean on the first attempt — PASS 702142, FAIL 2, SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Tick-level survey showed zero change on every test, including every RMW-shaped one this stage specifically targeted** — rather than accept that at face value, added a temporary hierarchical trace and re-ran `a3_add_dn_ea` with/without the fix to compare cycle-by-cycle. Confirmed the fix genuinely works exactly as designed (the read's own ack now lands one full tick earlier, and `mem_rmw_run_r`'s own write-dispatch register updates a tick earlier too) — but `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `ST_IDLE` state only advances on `state_adv`/`phase_r[0]` boundaries (the same 2-tick-per-state pacing grid from Phase 160's own S-state pairing), so `mem_rmw_run_r` becoming ready 1 tick earlier lands within the SAME 2-tick window rather than crossing it, quantizing the write's own dispatch to the identical absolute tick regardless — a different, precisely-identified absorption mechanism than Track C/D0's own finding, not just pattern-matched to it. For the plain-read tests (`a1_fea_*`), the zero change is even more direct: `MEASURED_INSTR_ONLY` tracks the read's own AS-rise, a pin-level event entirely governed by Phase 160's calibrated S-state timing, with zero dependency on ack-propagation speed for a standalone read with nothing downstream waiting on it. **Kept the fix despite the zero-visible-effect result** — it's a structurally real, verified-correct latency reduction (confirmed at the signal level via direct trace, not assumed), consistent with Track A/C's own "real but not currently visible" character, just absorbed by a specific, well-understood downstream mechanism rather than sub-clock rounding. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track D, Stage D1` for the full writeup.

195 (Phase 163 Track D, Stage D2 — closes Track D and the bus-pipelining-overlap plan in full). The last 3 of `CI_DONE`'s 5 entry points: `CI_FILL_3` (I-cache linefill's own final beat), `CI_D_BURST0` (D-cache burst, full 4-beat success), `CI_D_FILL_3B` (D-cache burst, degraded fallback's own final beat) — none reached by the bus-touching timing survey (never enables the I-cache or D-cache burst mode), so gated by `make test`'s own `tb/cache_tb.sv` instead, per the plan's own note. Same always_ff shape as D0/D1 (`state <= CI_IDLE` directly on completion, array-populate side effects unchanged on their existing schedule). `CI_D_BURST0`/`CI_D_FILL_3B` had **no output-block case arm at all** before this stage (their `dc_burst_req`/`dc_burst_addr` were already driven unconditionally from registered state per Phase 158 Stage 4c, `eu_ack` came exclusively via the old `CI_DONE` hop) — added two new arms: `CI_D_BURST0` fires `eu_ack` only for the full-success case (`dc_burst_beat==2'd3`, the degraded case must not complete here), muxing `eu_rdata` from whichever `dc_burst_rdata0..3` matches `woff_r`; `CI_D_FILL_3B` fires unconditionally on `dc_burst_ack`, `eu_rdata` either this beat's own word or the already-latched `fill_rdata_r` from an earlier beat. Confirmed `dc_burst_ack` (`biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s own `eu_burst_ack_r`) is already a genuine 1-tick pulse (default-clear-then-set-once pattern) before gating on it directly, no edge-detector needed — ruling out the Track C double-pulse bug class up front rather than discovering it via a failing test. Correct on the first attempt: `make test` 36/36 (including `cache`/`biu`, which directly exercise these paths), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full Harte sweep (mandatory — now touches all 5 of `CI_DONE`'s entry points) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2, SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. Confirmed by reading (not assumed) that `tb/cache_tb.sv`'s `cback_n` is hardwired asserted, so the degraded burst fallback has never been reachable in this testbench — a pre-existing, already-documented Phase 166 limitation, not a gap this stage introduces. See `plan.md §Phase 163 Track D, Stage D2` for the full writeup.

**This closes the bus-pipelining-overlap plan (`~/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-cocoa.md`, Tracks C and D) in full.** Every one of `CI_DONE`'s 5 entry points in `biu_cache_if.sv`, plus `biu_sizing_fsm.sv`'s own `SS_DONE`, now presents its ack combinationally the same cycle its completion trigger fires, instead of an extra registered cycle. Measured wins: BSR/JSR improved 2 full ticks (Stage D0, stacking with Track C); `a4_cmpm`/`a7_trap_n`/`a7_illegal` improved (Track C); every RMW-shaped test's own read-to-write dispatch gap is provably tighter at the signal level (D1, confirmed via direct trace), even where fully absorbed by `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `ST_IDLE` 2-tick quantization for every test currently in the survey. Zero correctness regression at any stage, verified via the full gate (`make test`/`cosim_grp`/`cosim_memind`/124-suite Harte sweep) after every single RTL-touching stage.

196 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 1). User asked how far this RTL is from cycle-perfect real-68030 timing; re-ran `scripts/b_final_clock_survey.py` against current RTL to get real numbers rather than rely on memory. Found a real measurement flaw along the way: about half of the 85 register-only timing tests need a trailing "marker" instruction (target writes `An`/`SR`/`CCR`/nothing directly watchable) whose own real cost was silently folding into the reported gap, making instructions like NOP look ~4x slower than real silicon when the marker itself was the actual cost. Splitting into 56 "clean" (target's own destination IS the watched register) vs 29 "marker-needed" tests: the clean set's gap is min=-5/max=+4/mean=+0.41 clocks — essentially exact already. Fixed the small number of genuine negative ("too fast") outliers that remained: `a4_unpk_dn` (-5), `a2_movec_read`/`a4_pack_dn`/`a5_bchg_imm_dn` (-3 each), `a1_fea_immb`/`a1_fea_immw` (-1 each, same underlying MOVE.B/W #(data),Dn instruction) — traced to Phase 163 Stage 1's own documented-but-never-revisited side effect (its `ext_valid` fix sped up several `ext_count==1` instructions' shared baseline, explicitly flagged then as "left for a possible future extension of Part D's own work"). Five new whitelist entries in `eu_seq.sv`'s existing `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` mechanism (Phase 162 Part D's own purely-additive infrastructure, no new mechanism needed): BCHG/BCLR/BSET #(data),Dn (`scripts/timing_tables.py`'s BIT_MANIP table gives all three NCC=6, matching the already-whitelisted dynamic-register form; `dec_writes_reg` naturally excludes BTST, no clean-list data point yet); MOVEC Cr,Rn read direction (fixed opcode match `16'h4E7A` — this direction has no dedicated decode flag, "Rc→Rn uses dec_use_imm" per the decode block's own comment); PACK/UNPK Dy,Dx,#(data) register form (BCD_EXT table: PACK NCC=6, UNPK NCC=8, matching measured gaps exactly); MOVE.B/W #(data),Dn (re-derives the MOVE/MOVEA block's own decode condition from module-level continuous-assign fields — `f_move_sz!=00` alone implies `f_group∈{1,3}` by its own definition, but `f_move_dst_mode`/`f_mode`/`f_reg` are still needed to avoid an unrelated-opcode collision; the long form has no clean-list data point and is deliberately excluded). Results: `make test` 36/36 clean on the first attempt, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — all 5 are real, heavily-Harte-covered instructions) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. Re-ran the survey and diffed line-by-line: exactly the 6 targeted tests changed, all to gap=0 exactly, zero side effects on any of the other 111. See `plan.md §Phase 164 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 1)` for the full writeup.

197 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 2 — investigation only, no RTL change). Traced `a3_addi_dn` (`addi.l #20,d2`, `ext_count==2`, manual=4/measured=8/gap=+4) directly (temporary hierarchical trace on `biu_cycle_gen`'s state, `m68030_ifu`'s `q_cnt`/`ifu_req`/`fetch_pend_r`, `biu_arbiter`'s `grant_ifu` — removed before committing) rather than guess whether it's the same "threshold too narrow" bug Phase 163 Stage 1 fixed for `ext_count==1`. **Confirmed it's not that bug and not one new bug — it's two already-understood mechanisms plus one genuinely unavoidable cost, stacked**: (1) `ext_count==2` needs 3 words in the queue, but the IFU fetches 2 at a time — when decode reaches the target with an empty queue (the common post-`bra.w` case every one of these tests hits), the first fetch only reaches `q_cnt=2`, forcing a genuinely separate second ~2-clock bus fetch to reach `q_cnt=4` — matches real 68030 silicon's own longword-aligned prefetch granularity, not fixable and not a bug; (2) the gap *between* the two fetches is exactly the already-known "`fetch_pend_r` needs a genuine extra cycle to re-arm after `ifu_ack`" mechanism (Phase 161 Part B Stage B0, already found load-bearing) plus `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `ST_IDLE` 2-tick `state_adv` quantization (the closed bus-pipelining-overlap plan's own Track C/D Stage D1 finding) — this is item 4's own scope, not a new mechanism, just a second independent confirmation of it from a different (IFU-driven, not EU-driven) angle. **Decision: no RTL change** — rather than force a speculative fix onto a gap that's partly unavoidable and partly already scoped elsewhere, item 2 closes as investigation-only, folding its finding into item 4. `make test` 36/36 sanity check, `tb/timing_tb.sv`'s temporary trace fully removed (no diff). See `plan.md §Phase 165 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 2)` for the full writeup.

198 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 3). Redesigned 15 of the 29 marker-needed register tests for a clean, marker-free measurement. New `tb/timing_tb.sv` `watch_kind` capability (0=Dn default/unchanged, 1=An via `u_rf.a_reg`, 2=CCR via `u_rf.sr_out[7:0]`) — confirmed zero regression on the existing 117-test survey before touching any individual test. Converted: `a2_move_rn_an`/`a2_move_usp_an`/`a3_adda_w/l`/`a3_suba_w/l`/`a6_lea` (watch_kind=1, An writes); `a2_move_dn_ccr`/`a3_cmp_rn_dn`/`a3_cmpa_w`/`a3_cmpi_dn`/`a4_tst_dn`/`a5_btst_imm_dn`/`a5_btst_dn_dn`/`a5_bftst_dn`/`a6_chk_dn_dn_noexc` (watch_kind=2, CCR-only writes/compare-only ops, each given an explicit known CCR baseline before target so the watch's own prev!=new edge-detection has a real transition to catch). `a6_andi_to_sr`/`a6_andi_to_ccr` were converted then reverted — the original mask (`$FFFF`/`$FF`) is a pure no-op AND, so no transition can ever be detected; changing it to get one surfaced `p=1` vs the manual's `p=2`, but this exact finding was **already documented** in the tests' own pre-existing desc field from an earlier phase (opcode+immediate fetched together in one combined longword bus cycle, a real hardware property not a bug) — reverted via `git checkout`, initially forgetting to also rebuild the now-stale `.hex` (caught by comparing the survey's own test-name list against baseline, 2 tests silently missing). Bcc/DBcc/JMP (7 tests) and `a2_move_an_usp`/`a7_trapv_notrap`/`a7_bkpt` (3 tests) deliberately not attempted this phase (PC-redirect-as-completion-signal has the same decode_pc-races-ahead reliability question this project has hit before; TRAPV-notrap/BKPT have zero observable side effect when they don't trap, needing a different technique entirely) — left as documented follow-up. **Found one real hand-derivation mistake via direct trace**: `a6_chk_dn_dn_noexc`'s first watch_val ($1B, assuming CHK leaves V/C unaffected when it doesn't trap) hung — traced `u_rf.sr_out` directly and found CHK actually clears V/C (real result $18), not leaves them unaffected, an easy mistake for an architecturally "undefined" field. **9 newly-revealed "too fast" gaps found** (several previously showed a positive gap purely from marker-overhead coincidence, masking a real negative one) — 8 fixed via the same `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` mechanism as item 1 (MOVE Dn,CCR/MOVE USP,An/ADDA.W-SUBA.W-CMPA.W/BTST/BFTST/LEA(An),An, each gated on an already-existing decode flag). **`CHK Dn,Dn`'s own attempted fix (gap=-5) caused a real functional regression, not a test-margin issue** — `make test` caught `exception_tb.sv`'s CHK-02/03/06 with `chk_trap_cnt` incrementing by 11 instead of 1; root cause is `eu_seq.sv`'s own `chk_trap` assign being a pure combinational condition with no one-shot/edge guard (the register-direct CHK path had never held `ex_valid` high for more than 1 cycle before this stall existed, so nothing needed one) — reverted, documented in-line, needs `chk_trap` made edge-triggered as a genuine separate RTL fix, not attempted here. **Also caught a second, unrelated test-margin regression**: `tb/ea_modes_tb.sv`'s `run_instr()` fixed `repeat(5)` post-ack settle margin was too tight for the new LEA stall (stale-read, `got 00000000 exp 00002000`) — widened to `repeat(10)`, same precedent as Stage D4's own earlier margin fix. Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full Harte sweep (mandatory) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2, SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. Survey: 117 tests, gap min=-5 (CHK, documented)/max=+7/**mean=1.30 (was 1.73)**, 48 of 117 now exact gap=0 (was 39). See `plan.md §Phase 166 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 3)` for the full writeup.

199 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 4 — investigation only, no RTL change). Investigated whether `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `ST_IDLE` 2-tick quantization (item 2's own finding) is avoidable the same way Track A/C/D removed the extra registered hop in `biu_cache_if.sv`/`biu_sizing_fsm.sv`. Read `ST_IDLE`'s own code directly: `state<=state_nxt` is gated on one uniform condition (`state_adv`, the same mechanism Phase 160 established to pace every real S-state pair to match actual silicon), and `ST_IDLE`'s own `state_nxt` selection is purely combinational with **no separate intermediate state** the way `CI_WRITE`/`SS_ACTIVE` each had their own `CI_DONE`/`SS_DONE` hop. **Key structural finding**: every prior Track A/C/D fix worked by adding a combinational *output* fast path that let a module present its ack earlier than its own registered transition, without ever changing *when the state register itself advances* — `ST_IDLE`'s exit has no such extra hop to bypass, since its own `state_adv`-gated transition into S0 already IS the real S0 launch itself. Bypassing `state_adv` just for this one transition (while leaving every other S-state-pair transition state_adv-gated, including the immediately-following S0→S1) would be internally inconsistent with the uniform synchronous discipline the whole FSM depends on. Corroborating evidence: the identical 2-tick quantization showed up independently via two unrelated request paths (Track D Stage D1's EU-driven RMW dispatch, item 2's IFU-driven fetch dispatch) — consistent with a genuine hardware-alignment property, not a coincidental artifact. **Decision: no RTL change** — concluded not applicable based on a genuine structural argument, not just re-stated uncertainty; given `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s centrality (the single highest-blast-radius module in the project), a speculative change without this confidence would be irresponsible. `make test` 36/36 sanity check. See `plan.md §Phase 167 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 4)` for the full writeup.

200 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 5 — 3 new tests, real gaps found, deep investigation deferred). The existing 32-test bus-touching survey only ever paired RMW instructions with the single simplest EA mode, plain `(An)`. Added 3 new tests combining already-proven-accurate instructions with EA modes never tested on an RMW op before: `a4_neg_mem_predec` (`neg.l -(a0)`), `a4_neg_mem_idx` (`neg.l (4,a0,d1.l)`, indexed), `a3_addq_postinc` (`addq.l #1,(a0)+`). **Found real, substantial, previously-unknown gaps — roughly double the plain-(An) case**: `a4_neg_mem_predec` gap=+9 (vs `a4_neg_mem`'s own +4), `a3_addq_postinc` gap=+10, `a4_neg_mem_idx` gap=+12. Cross-checked each against its own r/p/w expectation before trusting the number: predec/postinc both PASS cleanly (genuine gaps, not a counting artifact); the indexed test FAILS its own r/p/w check (p=1 not manual's p=2) — likely the same already-documented "opcode+ext-word combined into one longword fetch" alignment property found for `a6_andi_to_sr` in item 3, not a new bug, and doesn't explain the gap's own size (fewer bus cycles should mean faster, not slower). **Deliberately not investigated further** — tracing the root cause (auto-increment/decrement RMW dispatch interaction, potentially another `ext_count==2`-style item-2 finding, or something new) is open-ended work of comparable scope to items 1-4 individually; given the substantial ground already covered this session, surfaced as a concrete finding for the user to prioritize rather than chased unilaterally. No RTL changed (3 new test files only, not part of `make test`'s own gate) — `make test` 36/36 sanity check. See `plan.md §Phase 168 (cycle-accuracy-closing plan, item 5)` for the full writeup.

201 — CORRECTION to Phase 168's own item-5 finding, and a new master timing-benchmark script. Beginning the user-approved "Cycle-accuracy closing plan v2," traced `a4_neg_mem_predec`/`a3_addq_postinc` to find the source of Phase 168's own reported +9/+10/+12 clock gaps — and found the gaps were never real. **Phase 168's own numbers were wrong**: the one-off script used to build those 3 tests read the raw `MEASURED` field instead of `MEASURED_INSTR_ONLY` (the field `b_final_clock_survey.py` already correctly uses for marker-needing tests). Re-measured via the real survey script: `a4_neg_mem_predec` gap=+3 (not +9), `a4_neg_mem_idx` gap=+1 (not +12), `a3_addq_postinc` gap=+4, identical to the plain-`(An)` baseline (not +10). All three EA modes measure the same flat 11-clock dispatch cost as the already-fixed baseline — Tracks A-D's fixes already generalize cleanly, nothing to fix. Reported to the user immediately rather than proceeding on the false premise. Root cause: no single hardened tool existed, so a fresh under-tested script got written for a quick check — exactly the gap that caused the mistake. User asked for a proper master script and approved building one. New `scripts/timing_benchmark.py` consolidates `run_timing.py` (r/p/w PASS/FAIL, auto-hex-build) and `b_final_clock_survey.py` (manual-vs-measured gap, correct field selection) into one canonical tool — neither predecessor touched/renamed (both referenced by name throughout this file's own permanent history; each got a docstring pointer instead). New capabilities: r/p/w PASS/FAIL surfaced right next to the gap number (the direct fix for today's mistake — a bad bus-cycle count now shows `MISMATCH` instead of a silently-wrong-looking number); `--filter SUBSTR` for per-instruction spot-checks; `tests/timing/known_issues.json` sidecar tagging already-investigated non-bugs `[KNOWN: ...]`; optional `manual_ref` field cross-checking `desc`-parsed totals against `timing_tables.py`'s own structured lookups (opt-in, not retrofitted onto the ~150-test existing corpus); `--json`/`--strict` for future automated use. **Verification**: diffed the new tool's per-test gap numbers against `b_final_clock_survey.py`'s across all 120 tests — exact byte-for-byte match, zero differences. Cross-checked r/p/w MISMATCH detection against `run_timing.py`'s own verdicts. **Found a real, pre-existing bug in `run_timing.py` along the way**: it crashes (unhandled 60s `TimeoutExpired`) on any `watch_kind`-using test, since it was never updated when item 3 added that capability — confirmed by inspecting its actual subprocess command (missing `+watch_kind=`). Not a flaw in the new tool; left unfixed per the plan's own "kept as-is" scope for the predecessor scripts. The new tool's own r/p/w surfacing immediately found 3 more real, already-documented-but-never-formally-annotated findings (`a6_bcc_taken`/`a6_dbcc_false_notexp`/`a6_bsr`, all measured p=4 vs manual's p=2 — the IFU's own speculative-linear-readahead prefetch running past an unresolved branch, documented since Phase 161 Part A Stage A6) — added to `known_issues.json`. Pure tooling change, no RTL touched, `make test` 36/36 sanity check. See `plan.md §Phase 169` for the full writeup.

202 (cycle-accuracy closing plan v2, Stage 2). User asked to work through `timing_benchmark.py`'s own report and close gaps one at a time: fix, verify, commit, push, repeat. Started with `a6_chk_dn_dn_noexc` (gap=-5), already diagnosed in Phase 166. Fixed `chk_trap` (`eu_seq.sv`) with a one-shot latch: new `chk_trap_raw` (the old combinational expression, renamed) plus `chk_trap_fired_r`, clearing whenever `ex_valid` drops and setting the instant `chk_trap_raw` first fires, suppressing every later tick of the artificial stall. Hit the project's own recurring Icarus forward-reference issue (an `always_ff` referencing a `wire =` assign declared later in the file) — fixed by declaring both signals near `chk_below_w`/`chk_above_w` earlier in the file, with the `assign`s staying at their original position. Verified the edge-triggering fix alone against `exception_tb.sv`'s CHK-02/03/06 checks (48/48 clean) before re-adding the `CHK Dn,Dn` whitelist entry to `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` (+20 ticks) that Phase 166 had reverted. **`make test` immediately caught a second real bug**: CHK-03/06's own "N=1" checks read stale `0`s — traced to `exception_tb.sv`'s `run_instr()` task, a fixed `repeat(12)` post-ack settle margin too tight for CHK's new +20-tick stall (exact same class of bug as Stage 1's `ea_modes_tb.sv` fix) — widened to `repeat(30)`. Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full Harte sweep (mandatory) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2, SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. `a6_chk_dn_dn_noexc` now measures exactly gap=0 (was -5); removed its `known_issues.json` entry. Corpus-wide: min gap -5→-4, mean (known-excluded) 1.32→1.30. See `plan.md §Phase 170` for the full writeup. **Next**: `a4_tas_mem` (-4, now the new min).

203. Multi-instruction sequential timing measurement tool. User asked to characterize how much of the timing gap real overlap between consecutive instructions closes, since every existing single-instruction test (Phase 159's own convention) lands a taken branch directly on the instruction under test — a shape that can never benefit from `m68030_ifu.sv`'s own prefetch queue, which fills continuously and independently of `instr_ack`/`stall`/`ex_valid`. New `+seq_len=N` plusarg in `tb/timing_tb.sv` generalizes the existing marker-free `watch_kind=3` retirement-pulse detector (`dispatched_seen_r`/`wb_valid_r`) from "stop at the first retirement" to "record the first N," printing a `SEQ_CHECKPOINT` line per instruction — purely additive (`seq_complete_r` can only assert when `seq_len>1`; the full single-instruction corpus confirmed byte-identical before/after). New `scripts/timing_benchmark.py` `run_sequence()` path reports cumulative/incremental ticks and a `vs_manual_sum` figure (deliberately not called "overlap benefit" — a negative value means real overlap hasn't fully absorbed this RTL's own separately-documented isolated-dispatch overhead, not that no overlap occurred). 5 pilot tests (register-pair, `ext_count==2`-pair, RMW-pair, a 3-instruction mix, an ALU-op-into-taken-branch pair) confirmed real, substantial overlap: register-only pairs closed almost the entire isolated-dispatch gap (14→3 ticks incremental), while the RMW pair only partially closed (49→36 ticks), plausibly genuine bus-arbitration contention. `make test` 36/36; no RTL touched.

204. Expanded the sequence corpus from 5 to 33 tests (28 new, via new `scripts/gen_seq_tests2.py`) across register ALU (AND/OR/EOR/SUB/CMP), shift/rotate, bit-field, MOVE variants, all 5 remaining `ext_count==2` immediates, RMW EA-mode variants (predec/postinc/indexed), a real 4-pass DBF loop (first genuine steady-state loop measurement in this corpus), a genuine JSR/RTS call-return, three heterogeneous 4/6/8-instruction chains, and two unrolled same-instruction x4/x8 steady-state runs. Found and fixed a real harness bug (not RTL) building the DBF loop test: the legacy `watch_kind=0` value-watch path (defaulting to watching D0==0) stays active during `seq_len>1` runs and has no awareness of the retirement-pulse counter — the DBF loop's own counter genuinely transitions D0 through 0 mid-loop, spuriously firing the old path and truncating checkpoint collection early. Fixed by gating that whole legacy block on `seq_len<=1`; full single-instruction corpus re-confirmed byte-identical. Results (informational, no RTL changed): register/MOVE clusters land within +0..+2 clocks of the manual's own naive additive sum (several exact); the `ext_count==2`/RMW clusters' own apparent -5..-8 "shortfall" is fully explained by this RTL's own already-documented isolated-dispatch overhead, not a lack of overlap — once compared against each instruction's own real isolated baseline instead of the manual's zero-overhead floor, steady-state incremental cost lands at +1..+3 clocks, same as everything else. `make test` 36/36.

205. Bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation ("case 2," isolated bus-touching instructions) — the user asked to prioritize this after a 3-way timing-compliance summary (register-only ≈exact; sequential/warmed code within a couple clocks; isolated bus-touching still the worst gap). A fresh, correctly-classified re-measurement (`tests/timing/a*.json`, bus-touching subset, n=37) confirmed a real, unclosed mean gap of +1.89 clocks. Direct hierarchical tracing (temporary instrumentation, removed before commit) of `a3_add_dn_ea` and `a6_bsr` found every individual bus cycle (opcode fetch, data read, data write) pays a consistent, reproducible 16-tick (4-clock) round trip. Reading MC68030UM.pdf directly (Section 1.1 Features: async bus cycles are "three clocks minimum"; Section 7.3.1's own State 0-5 text; Figure 7-21/7-25's own timing diagrams, which chain consecutive bus cycles — including read-write-write-read — with *zero* idle time) confirmed real 68030 hardware needs only 6 half-clock states per bus cycle, not this RTL's 8. Comparing per-state pin assertions precisely found two divergences: our own S0/S1 split delays ECS# by one state (real hardware asserts ECS# in the same state as the address, S0), and our own S2/S3 split staggers AS#/DS# (real hardware asserts them *together* at S1 for reads — but genuinely staggers them for writes, S1→S3, since DS# can't assert before data is placed on the bus at S2; confirmed via Section 7.3.2's own text, so the write-side stagger is real, required timing, not compressible). Traced the origin of the *wrong* 8-state model to `output.txt` (the project's original architectural design conversation): it asserts "if you assert AS and DS in the same phase, you violate the datasheet" (contradicted by the real manual's own text) and elsewhere uses `ext_lds_n`/`ext_uds_n` — the 68000's own split byte strobes, the same conflation `CLAUDE.md` already had to correct once for the LDS/UDS-vs-single-DS question. Given `CLAUDE.md`'s own S-State Signal Timing section had been treated as validated ground truth by Phase 160 itself ("matches CLAUDE.md's own S-state table exactly, state-for-state," verifying only *pacing*, never the state *count*), corrected that section directly against the manual, citing exact text, before touching any RTL. **Fix implemented (READ cycles only this round — WRITE's own second savings source needs S7, out of scope)**: `rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s shared `SP_S0`-`SP_S7` pin-driving block moved ECS# assertion from `SP_S1` to `SP_S0` (safe for every cycle type — doesn't change any transition table, so no cycle's own state count changes from this alone) and added a `cyc_rw`-gated DS# assertion at `SP_S2` (fires only for reads; writes are completely unaffected, still asserting DS# at the unchanged `SP_S3`). Then, specifically for ordinary `ST_READ_*` states only, changed the transition table so `ST_READ_S0`→`ST_READ_S2` and `ST_READ_S2`→`ST_READ_S4` (skipping the now-functionally-redundant S1/S3), bringing ordinary READ down to exactly 6 states — matching the real chip and preserving Phase 160's own even-state-count/whole-real-clock invariant (6 is even; skipping only one of S1/S3 would give an invalid odd 7-state cycle, confirmed as a real constraint before implementing anything). `ST_READ_S1`/`ST_READ_S3` stay declared and still serve WRITE/IACK/RMW/burst/init cycle types via the shared `sphase` decode, untouched. Fixed 3 testbenches whose own checks hard-coded the old per-state timing as literal expected values (not RTL regressions): `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s dedicated per-S-state pin checkers (`eu_read_check_timing`, the ECS#/OCS# pin-timing block, 3 more `wait_for_state(ST_READ_S3)` sites in coprocessor/BKPT read checks) updated to the new state numbering; `tb/top_tb.sv`'s post-boot "IFU active, bus not idle" check tightened (break the request-detection loop the instant it fires, instead of always scanning the full window) since the now-faster read cycle could complete before a loose fixed margin fired; a second, downstream `tb/top_tb.sv` HALT# race (asserting `halt_n` then immediately checking for zero AS# activity, racing `halt_s`'s own 2-stage synchronizer before the now-more-frequently-ready IFU could start a cycle) fixed by only counting "AS# during halt" once `bus_halted` has actually synchronized. **Verification**: `make test` 36/36, `make cosim_grp` 8/8, `make cosim_memind` 11/11 (deliberately excludes `memind3`, an existing, unrelated documented exclusion), full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Result**: bus-touching (case 2) mean gap +1.89→**+0.51** clocks (n=37, ~73% reduction), max +7→+4; register-only (case 1) mean also improved +0.74→+0.51 (every instruction's own opcode fetch is a read too, so it benefits from the same fix). 4 tests that were previously exact now land at -1/-2 (below the manual's own rounded-average NCC, a legitimate consequence per MC68030UM.pdf 11-25's own "rounded up... AVERAGE of odd/even alignment" wording, documented in `known_issues.json`). WRITE cycles, RMW, IACK, burst, and init sequences remain at their original 8-state timing — deliberately deferred (WRITE's own second savings source is S7, the shared completion-dispatch state with a real, already-documented collision-bug history; RMW/IACK/burst/init were out of this round's scope). See `plan.md`'s bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation for the full derivation and `CLAUDE.md`'s own corrected S-State Signal Timing section for the reference model.

206. WRITE-cycle S1+S7 removal, closing out the bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation's second half (user: "Now go after WRITE's S7 removal too"). Confirmed via direct code reading that `ST_WRITE_S1`'s own block had already become a genuine no-op for writes as a side effect of Phase 205's shared ECS# move (ECS# now asserts at S0 for every cycle type; S1's own block never re-asserted it, so it was already correctly negating by default) — safe to skip, same as READ's own S1. `rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`: `ST_WRITE_S0`→`ST_WRITE_S2` (skip S1) and `ST_WRITE_S6`→`ST_IDLE` (skip S7) together bring plain WRITE to exactly 6 states, matching MC68030UM.pdf 7.3.2's own State 0-5 sequence and preserving the even-state-count/whole-real-clock invariant (removing only one of the two would give an invalid odd 7-state cycle, same constraint Phase 205 already established). **Found and fixed a real bug mid-implementation**: a first attempt, reasoning "ordinary writes only ever complete via `grant_eu`," duplicated just that one branch from the shared `SP_S7` completion-dispatch arm into a new `ST_WRITE_S6`-gated block. Wrong — `ST_WRITE_*` states are shared by more than ordinary EU writes; `cyc_is_coproc_r` (coprocessor writes) has its own dedicated ack branch in the same `SP_S7` arm, and skipping S7 for all `ST_WRITE_*` uses silently orphaned it. `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own pre-existing "Coprocessor write" test caught this immediately (`eu_coproc_ack` never fired). Fixed properly — not by patching in the missed branch, but by relocating the *entire* `SP_S7` body (unchanged internally) out of the `case(sphase)` statement into a standalone `if (sphase==SP_S7 || state==ST_WRITE_S6) begin ... end`, so every branch (IACK/init/CAS2/coprocessor/BKPT/burst/RMW/MMU/EU/IFU) applies identically regardless of which condition triggered it — guarantees nothing else can be silently missed the same way. Also found, via a systematic grep for every remaining `ST_WRITE_S7`/`ST_WRITE_S1` reference (the same discipline that caught the coprocessor gap), two BERR-abort-machinery sites that would have silently broken for plain writes once S7 became unreachable: the `is_S7`-gated clear of `berr_abort_r`/`berr_is_halt_retry_r`, and an explicit `state==ST_READ_S7||state==ST_WRITE_S7` check clearing `in_retry_r`/`retry_r` on retry completion — both fixed by adding `|| state==ST_WRITE_S6` alongside the existing conditions, found and fixed *before* running any test (not reactively). Results: `make test` 36/36 (clean on the first full run after the proper fix), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 11/11, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Result**: bus-touching (case 2) mean gap +0.51→**+0.08** clocks (n=37) — combined with Phase 205's own READ-cycle fix, a ~96% total reduction from the original +1.89, essentially converged to zero. 2 more tests (`a4_clr_mem`, `a6_pea` — both pure single-write instructions) now land at -1, the same benign "below the manual's own rounded-average NCC" pattern already documented for the READ-side fix, added to `known_issues.json`. RMW write phase, IACK, burst, and init sequences remain untouched — RMW's own *read* phase was already confirmed to match ordinary reads exactly (Phase 205), but its write phase, and the other cycle types, haven't been checked against the manual yet. See `plan.md`'s bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation for the full derivation.

207. RMW write-phase S1+S7 removal (user: "Now go after RMW's write phase too"), closing out the third and last cycle-type family planned in the bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation. Read MC68030UM.pdf Section 7.3.3 directly before touching anything: real RMW is a 12-state cycle (S0-S11) — S0-S5 (read phase) confirmed identical to an ordinary read (already established, Phase 205), S6-S11 (write phase) confirmed identical in shape to an ordinary write (ECS+addr, AS+DBEN, data placed, DS asserted, nothing new, negate — the same 6-state pattern Phase 206 already compressed). Figure 7-30 also confirmed AS# stays continuously asserted through the whole indivisible cycle, never negating between read and write — already correctly modeled by this project's own pre-existing `rmw_as_hold` mechanism, unaffected by this change (its own reference to the now-skipped `ST_RMW_WRITE_S1` becomes a harmless unreachable check, since `SP_S2`'s own unconditional `ext_as_n=0` already covers the gap S1 used to bridge). `rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`: applied the identical `ST_RMW_WRITE_S0`→`ST_RMW_WRITE_S2` (skip S1) / `ST_RMW_WRITE_S6`→`ST_IDLE` (skip S7) transition-table change Phase 206 already proved for plain WRITE. Extended both trigger conditions Phase 206 established (the relocated `SP_S7` completion-dispatch body, and the BERR-abort-clear condition) to also include `state==ST_RMW_WRITE_S6` — found via the same systematic grep-every-remaining-reference discipline that caught Phase 206's own coprocessor-write bug, this time applied proactively *before* running any test rather than reactively after a failure. `is_rmw_write` (gating the relocated completion body's own RMW branch) already covered `ST_RMW_WRITE_S6` unchanged, since that state is still visited — only S7 stopped being reached. Results: `make test` 36/36 clean on the first attempt (including the dedicated "RMW AS# continuity + BERR at S6" test), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 11/11, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (a particularly meaningful gate here, since TAS/CAS/CAS2 are directly Harte-covered and this touches their own shared atomic-lock machinery). `a4_tas_mem`'s own measured total in the timing corpus is unchanged (still gap=-4) — consistent with, not contradicted by, the fix: that test's own pre-existing `known_issues.json` entry already documents TAS's write phase producing no observable AS-rise (continuously asserted per the indivisible-cycle protocol, confirmed again by Figure 7-30 above), so this measurement technique structurally can't see the write-phase savings either way, before or after this change. **This closes the three cycle-type families originally scoped for the bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation** (ordinary READ, ordinary WRITE, RMW read+write) — IACK, burst, and init (SSP/PC fetch) sequences remain at their original 8-state timing, not yet investigated against the manual. See `plan.md`'s bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation for the full derivation.

208. IACK and init (SSP/PC fetch) S1+S3 removal (user: "Now go after IACK, burst, and init cycles too"). Read MC68030UM.pdf 7.4.1.1 and 7.8/Figure 7-65 directly: both confirmed architecturally plain reads ("the interrupt acknowledge cycle is a read cycle... differs from the asynchronous read cycle... [only] in that it accesses the CPU address space"; Figure 7-65 shows the identical S0/S2/S4 labeling as an ordinary read for reset-time SSP/PC fetches). Applied the SAME S1+S3 skip already proven for ordinary READ (Phase 205) — deliberately **not** the S1+S7 skip used for WRITE/RMW_WRITE (Phases 206-207), since those needed S7 removed specifically because AS/DS genuinely stagger for writes; reads never touch S7 at all in this project's own already-established choice. **Caught a real mistake before building anything**: a first implementation attempt combined both patterns (skipped S1, S3, AND S7 for IACK/init), which would have produced an invalid, odd 5-state cycle — caught by re-deriving from `ST_READ`'s own already-proven transitions before running any test, not reactively via a failure. Reverted the S7-removal part and the matching completion-dispatch/BERR-abort trigger additions (unneeded once S7 stays real for these three cycle types, exactly like ordinary READ). Found and correctly left unchanged one genuinely critical site via the now-standard proactive grep: `init_done_r` (the single flag gating the whole chip leaving its own boot sequence) is keyed on `state==ST_INIT_PC_S7` — confirmed correct to leave alone since init keeps its own real S7. Results: `make test` 36/36 (including `top`, which exercises the boot sequence heavily — clean on the first attempt this time, unlike the original READ fix which needed real margin work there), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 11/11, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (a particularly meaningful gate given every single Harte test depends on the init sequence to even start). **Found and corrected a stale documentation bug while re-surveying**: `a4_tas_mem`'s own `known_issues.json` entry claimed its gap stayed at -4 (unchanged) after Phase 207's RMW-write fix, but a controlled git-stash A/B comparison against the exact committed Phase 207 state showed the true, reproducible value has been gap=-5 (measured=9, not 10) since that fix landed — the earlier claim was based on a stale, not-actually-rebuilt `sim/timing` binary at the moment it was written, not a live regression or nondeterminism; corrected in `known_issues.json` with the real mechanism (RMW-write's own S7 removal genuinely sped up TAS's locked write phase) documented. **Burst mode confirmed out of scope for this round**: MC68030UM.pdf 7.3.7, read directly, shows burst is architecturally different, not a bigger version of the same fix — it's a *synchronous* (STERM-based, not DSACK-based) protocol, with the first longword using the synchronous read cycle's own 4-state (S0-S3) shape (7.3.4) and subsequent beats using their own 2-state-per-beat pattern (S4/S5, S6/S7, S8/S9) with CBREQ/CBACK handshaking layered on top — none of which maps onto the async 6-state model this whole investigation has been compressing. Would need its own dedicated manual read (7.3.4 in full) and an assessment of how close `biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s own purpose-built (not copied-from-async-model) implementation already is, before assuming it even needs the same kind of fix. Deliberately not attempted — flagged as a separate, dedicated investigation if pursued further. This closes out the READ/WRITE/RMW/IACK/init side of the bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation in full; only burst mode remains unexamined. See `plan.md`'s bus-cycle round-trip overhead investigation for the full derivation.

209. Stall-constant recalibration: uniform +1clk reporting consistency across the whole `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` whitelist (Phase 162 Part D's own mechanism). User asked directly why `a5_rol_imm_dy` (and every other stall-padded instruction) reports an exact gap=0 while unpadded register-direct instructions like `a3_add_rn_dn` honestly report +1. Traced and confirmed: every one of the ~40 whitelist constants in `eu_seq.sv` was calibrated as `manual_NCC_ticks - empirically_measured_baseline`, an algebraic identity that mathematically cancels the same +1 dispatch floor `a3_add_rn_dn` shows raw (confirmed real and unfixable in Stage 6 above / Phase 159 Stage 0: an isolated instruction has no predecessor bus activity to overlap its own opcode fetch with, and MC68030UM.pdf Section 11.3.3 explicitly states its own "two clock periods per bus cycle" NCC model assumes overlap with a PREVIOUS instruction, unavailable in isolation) — never genuinely modeling extra real microcode time, just silently absorbing the same floor. Explicitly not a hardware-accuracy question (removing the stalls entirely would be a real regression, undoing Phase 162 Part D's own legitimate combinational-vs-iterative shift/bit-field fix; the +1 floor itself is separately confirmed unfixable) — the user asked for a pure reporting-consistency change instead: recalibrate every constant +4 ticks (+1 clock) so every whitelist-covered instruction reports the same honest +1 every unpadded instruction already shows. **Implementation**: all 40 individual constant-assignment sites in `rtl/eu_seq.sv`'s `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` mechanism (shift/rotate reg+imm forms, EXG/MOVE-CCR-Dn/MOVE-SR-Dn/SWAP, ABCD/SBCD/NBCD, EXT/Scc/TAS, dynamic+static BCHG/BCLR/BSET, MOVEC Cr,Rn read, PACK/UNPK, MOVE.B/W #(data),Dn, MOVE Dn,CCR, MOVE USP,An/An,USP, TRAPV no-trap, ADDA.W/SUBA.W/CMPA.W, BTST, BFTST Dn, CHK Dn,Dn no-exception, LEA (An),An, the 7-entry bit-field case statement, DBcc(cc=True), Bcc.B/W not-taken) each increased by exactly 4, with comments rewritten to the new `NCC+1clk` derivation and a new file-level header comment explaining the "+1clk recal" convention once. ANDI/ORI/EORI to SR/CCR remains deliberately excluded (already-documented: its own stall genuinely delays WB commit but gets fully absorbed by a separate IFU-refill mechanism before reaching the measured total). **Verification found 3 real testbench margin regressions**, the same class already anticipated from Phase 202's own CHK-stall precedent — each traced to the specific instruction whose stall grew, not guessed at: `tb/ctrl_flow_tb.sv`'s `run_instr()` (`repeat(8)`→`repeat(16)`, SWAP/EXT/EXG/Scc 4→8 ticks); `tb/ea_modes_tb.sv`'s `run_instr()` (`repeat(10)`→`repeat(20)`, LEA (An),An 4→8 ticks); `tb/system_tb.sv`'s `run_instr()` (`repeat(16)`→`repeat(24)`, MOVEC Cr,Rn 12→16 ticks). Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — touches shift/rotate, BCD, bit-field, bit-manipulation, MOVEC, PACK/UNPK, CHK, LEA, Scc, TAS, branch/DBcc timing across dozens of heavily-Harte-covered families) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. Re-surveyed: exactly the 40 targeted constants' own tests moved from gap=0 (MATCH) to gap=+1; 0 previously-non-zero-gap tests changed. Added all 46 newly-non-exact test names to `known_issues.json` with a shared "reporting consistency, not hardware-accuracy" explanation. Corpus-wide: 11 exact matches (was 51, all moved to the documented +1 tier as intended), 109 known non-zero (was 63), 0 unexplained non-zero. **Found, documented, left out of scope**: `a7_bkpt`/`a7_illegal`/`a7_trap_n`'s own `known_issues.json` entries record stale gap values that no longer match current measurements (confirmed via `git diff` this session's own JSON rewrite didn't touch their content) — predates this session, unrelated (none of the three touch `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed`; they dispatch via exception/CPU-space mechanisms). See `plan.md §Phase 180` for the full writeup.

210. `known_issues.json` staleness refresh (documentation only, no RTL/testbench change). User asked to review every timing-corpus test whose gap isn't 0 or +1. Doing that review surfaced systemic documentation debt: most bus-touching/RMW/`ext_count==2`/readahead cluster entries were written before the READ/WRITE/RMW-cycle S-state compression (Phases 205-207), which shrank underlying measurements by 1-2 clocks, and were never re-validated afterward. Systematic sweep (parsing each entry's own leading `gap=X` claim against the live measured value): 21 entries with a directly wrong number in the "not 0/+1" set the user asked about, plus 14 more found on a full 120-test sweep — 9 had become full exact matches (gap=0) while still carrying a stale non-zero explanation, 5 had drifted numbers (same mechanism, smaller magnitude), and 2 (`a7_illegal`/`a7_trap_n`) were flat-out mis-tagged with an unrelated test's own boilerplate despite their own real gap being -4 with a completely different mechanism (matching `a7_bkpt`'s own exception-dispatch-FSM character instead). Fixed all of it: 26 numeric refreshes, 9 entries removed entirely (established precedent: once a gap becomes an exact match, delete the explanation rather than leave it stale), 2 rewritten with their own correct mechanism. Verified via a full re-parse of all 120 tests: 0 remaining `gap=X` mismatches, 0 unexplained non-zero gaps. `known_issues.json` now has 110 entries. See `plan.md §Phase 181` for the full writeup.

211. RMW-to-memory dispatch floor full-chain investigation (Stage 3's deferred avenue from the closed bus-pipelining-overlap plan, investigation only, no RTL change). User asked to investigate whether the next bus cycle's dispatch could begin during the current cycle's own trailing negate/ack phase, rather than waiting for a full return to `ST_IDLE`. Traced `add.l d1,(a0)` with temporary full-chain instrumentation (`eu_seq`'s `mem_req`/`mem_ack`/`mem_rmw_run_r`, `biu_cache_if`'s own `state`/`eu_req`/`eu_ack`, `biu_sizing_fsm`'s own `sf`/`cyc_req`/`eu_ack`, `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `state`/`eu_req`, `biu_arbiter`'s `grant_eu` — all removed before finalizing). Confirmed via the full chain (not just `biu_cycle_gen` in isolation) that Tracks A/C/D's own combinational fast paths already fire essentially back-to-back through the whole EU→`biu_cache_if`→`biu_sizing_fsm`→`biu_cycle_gen` pipeline — the read's own ack triggers `eu_seq`'s write-phase flip the very next tick, and `biu_cycle_gen`'s own `eu_req` input becomes valid on the EXACT SAME tick its `state` register is already sitting in `ST_IDLE`. The critical finding: the dispatch-chain latency and `ST_IDLE`'s own mandatory 2-tick minimum hold (Phase 160's `state_adv` pacing) land on literally the same 2-tick window, with zero slack between them — `eu_req` isn't arriving late while `biu_cycle_gen` waits, it's already valid on `ST_IDLE`'s very first tick, and dispatch still can't happen until 2 ticks later purely because `state_adv` requires the full hold before any transition-decision is evaluated. This is a genuine, independent re-confirmation of Phase 163 item 4's own conclusion (originally reached from a completely different transition — IFU-driven prefetch dispatch, not EU-driven RMW read-to-write) — not just repeating the same reasoning, but tracing a different concrete scenario and landing on the identical structural bottleneck. **Conclusion: no RTL change** — no genuine slack found to exploit, not just insufficient confidence to try. `make test` 36/36 sanity check (unaffected, no RTL touched). See `plan.md §Phase 182` for the full writeup.

212. Burst mode timing redesign: ~2.4x too slow, closed to ~1.5x, plus two real pin-level correctness bugs found and fixed along the way. Following up the user's request to look into burst mode timing (previously flagged in Phase 208 as unexamined): read MC68030UM.pdf 7.3.4 (Synchronous Read Cycle) and 7.3.7 (Burst Operation Cycles) directly. Real burst beat 0 is 4 states (S0-S3, 2 clocks, matching an ordinary synchronous read's own shape); each subsequent beat is just 2 states (a bare sample+hold pair, no address/AS/DS/FC/SIZ re-setup at all, since "the processor maintains AS, DS, R/W, A0-A31, FC0-FC2, SIZ0-SIZ1 in their current state throughout the burst operation"). Real 4-beat total: 10 states = 5 clocks. This RTL's own `ST_BURST_*`/`ST_BWRITE_*` states were never touched by the earlier async compression work (Phases 205-208) — beat 0 used the OLD 8-state model (23 states total for a 4-beat burst), empirically measured at 48 ticks (12 clocks) via a controlled trace of `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own PCB-1 test — proportionally the largest gap found in this whole timing-accuracy effort. **Two real, previously-undiscovered pin-level correctness bugs found while designing the fix, both confirmed via direct trace**: (1) AS and DS both fully negated between every burst beat, not just DS — contradicting the manual's explicit "maintains AS, DS... throughout the burst operation"; `SP_S6`'s own combinational pin block already had a burst-aware hold override for AS but none for DS, which unconditionally negated every beat — this is why the old `ST_BURST_NEXT_S3` state (a full address/AS/DS re-assert) looked like real necessary work, when it was actually compensating for this bug. (2) The burst address bus increments per beat (`biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s own `burst_addr_r += 4`), contradicting the manual's explicit "the address bus... remains driven to a constant value... incrementing... must be performed by external hardware" — found, confirmed, but deliberately NOT fixed this pass, since `tb/mem_model.sv` has no burst-continuation logic of its own and freezing the address correctly would need a coordinated testbench change too; documented for a future phase. **Redesign** (`rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`): beat 0 skips S1/S3 matching the ordinary-read compression pattern; `SP_S6` gained the missing DS-hold override; S7 eliminated for burst (its own completion body was already an empty no-op for `is_burst`; S6 already exists as the necessary 1-cycle-later checkpoint where `berr_abort_r` has settled, so it now also owns the loop-vs-done decision S7 used to make) — continuation beats loop directly back through the SAME S4/S5/S6 triple beat 0 itself ends on, eliminating the entire `ST_BURST_NEXT_S3`-`S7` family. `at_burst_s7_wire` redirected from S7 to S6; `berr_abort_r`'s own clear condition gained the new terminal states (mirroring the same addition WRITE/RMW_WRITE needed in Phases 206-207). State-count math: beat 0 = 5 states, each continuation beat = 3 states (looped) — each individually odd, but the whole multi-beat cycle's own total (14, a complete IDLE-to-IDLE span) is even, satisfying Phase 160's real invariant (a complete bus cycle occupies a whole number of clocks — not that every internal sub-chunk must independently be even, a subtlety not previously exercised by any single-bus-cycle fix). **Found a second real bug while verifying**: with AS/DS now correctly held continuously, `tb/mem_model.sv`'s own read response (`d_latch`) only ever latches once, on the `MS_IDLE`-to-`active` transition, then keeps returning that value for as long as `active` stays true — the OLD buggy AS/DS-toggle had been *accidentally* resetting the model back to `MS_IDLE` between beats, the only reason it ever re-latched fresh data before; fixed with a minimal addition (re-latch whenever the address changes while still `active`, using the still-incrementing `burst_addr` as the trigger — a scenario that never occurred before this fix, so existing non-burst timing is provably unaffected). Also found and fixed a stale test: `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s "BERR on beat 2" test hardcoded a wait for the now-eliminated `ST_BURST_NEXT_S4` state number — fixed to wait for the beat-1-tagged visit to the now-shared `ST_BURST_S4`. Results: `tb/biu_tb.sv` 0 failures, full 4-beat burst measured at **30 ticks (7.5 clocks), down from 48 (12 clocks) — a 37.5% reduction**, exactly matching the hand-derived prediction. `make test` 36/36 (including `cache`, which exercises I-cache/D-cache burst linefill directly), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline (expected: Harte never enables IBE/DBE, so it never exercises burst mode — `biu_tb.sv`/`cache_tb.sv` are what actually exercise the new logic). See `plan.md §Phase 183` for the full writeup.

213. CAS2 timing redesign: 31% reduction, plus a real AS-continuity bug found and fixed (same class as burst's DS bug). Following up burst mode (Phase 212), user asked what else could be improved; CAS2 (the dual-address atomic compare-and-swap, "the most complex" cycle type) was the clear next candidate — still used the original, never-compressed 8-state-per-sub-cycle model across all 4 chained phases (R1/W1/R2/W2), never touched by Phases 205-208 or the burst work, measured at 64 ticks (16 clocks). Read MC68030UM.pdf 7.3.3 (Asynchronous RMW Cycle) directly: CAS/CAS2 "use read-modify-write bus cycles," and the flowchart's own read/write portions match ordinary read/write exactly — the same two patterns already proven in Phases 205-207, applied to CAS2's own 4 sub-cycles for the first time. Lower risk than burst's own redesign: CAS2's 4 phases aren't identical repeats (R1/R2 read different addresses, W1/W2 write different data), so no state-sharing/looping mechanism was needed — each phase keeps its own dedicated states, just compressed. **Found a real, previously-undiscovered pin-level correctness bug while designing the fix, confirmed via direct trace**: AS was fully negating between every CAS2 sub-phase, contradicting "does not issue a bus grant... during this operation" / "4 bus cycles without releasing the bus" — the same class of bug burst had with DS, just AS instead of DS. Root cause: `rmw_as_hold` (the existing, already-Harte-verified mechanism holding AS across ordinary RMW's single read-write transition) only ever covered `ST_RMW_READ_S6/S7`/`ST_RMW_WRITE_S0/S1` — CAS2's own states were never added, despite needing the identical treatment across 3 internal transitions instead of RMW's 1. Confirmed DS does NOT need the same treatment (re-derived from `rmw_as_hold`'s own name/scope: the indivisible lock governs bus ownership via AS, not per-transfer DS) — so unlike burst, no DS changes needed. **Redesign** (`rtl/biu_cycle_gen.sv`): R1/R2 (reads) skip S1/S3; W1/W2 (writes) skip S1 only (S3 stays, real required hold time). S7 eliminated for all 4 phases (its own completion-dispatch use was the only real reason it existed; S6 already exists as the necessary berr_abort_r-settle checkpoint, so it now also makes each phase's own continue-or-done decision). New `cas2_as_hold` mechanism (mirroring `rmw_as_hold` but generalized across 3 transitions): rather than hand-deriving the exact per-state condition for CAS2's own multiple conditional exit points (too error-prone for a security-relevant atomic instruction), it reuses the transition table's own already-correct "are we leaving the CAS2 sequence this cycle" decision directly — hold AS whenever `state_nxt` is also a CAS2 state, except R1's own genuine start. `berr_abort_r`'s clear condition and the completion-dispatch trigger both gained CAS2's 4 new S6 terminal states. State-count math: R1/R2 = 5 states each, W1/W2 = 6 states each — total 22 states = 44 ticks, matching the empirical measurement exactly with zero surprises (unlike burst, no extra dispatch overhead, since CAS2 is one continuous request with no re-arbitration between sub-phases). Results: `tb/biu_tb.sv` 0 failures on the first attempt. CAS2 four-cycle atomic measured at **44 ticks (11 clocks), down from 64 (16 clocks) — a 31% reduction**. `make test` 36/36 (including `atomic`), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 11/11, full 124-suite Harte sweep — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. Confirmed CAS2 itself has zero Harte coverage (68020+-only instruction, Harte's corpus is 68000-captured) — the Harte sweep is the zero-collateral-damage gate for everything else touched that IS Harte-covered (TAS, ordinary RMW-shaped ALU-memory ops); `tb/biu_tb.sv`'s own dedicated CAS2 tests and `tb/atomic_tb.sv` are what actually verify CAS2's own correctness. **Combined finding across the last two phases**: two independent, previously-undiscovered pin-level continuity bugs found and fixed (burst's DS, CAS2's AS), both the same underlying class — the shared `SP_S0`-`S7` pin logic's own per-state defaults silently undoing a multi-cycle atomic/locked sequence's own continuity requirement unless explicitly overridden — worth keeping in mind if any other multi-phase locked cycle type is examined in the future. See `plan.md §Phase 184` for the full writeup.

214. DIVS.L/DIVU.L/MULS.L/MULU.L too-fast timing stall + a real sign-bit decode bug found and fixed along the way. User asked how DIVS.L compares to real 68030 timing; measured `eu_mul_div.sv`'s explicitly-documented "purely combinational" design computing all four `.L` MUL/DIV forms in 3 clocks flat vs. the manual's own NCC of 90/78/44/44 (30x/26x/15x/15x too fast). User: "add the stall... check all instructions to see if there are any other gaps like this." Implemented via the existing Phase 162 `dec_internal_stall_ticks_fixed` mechanism, gated on `dec_is_muldivl`/`dec_unit`/`dec_md_op`, using the established "+1clk recal" convention. **Two mechanism-level fixes were required first**: (1) the whitelist's own tick counter was `logic [7:0]` (max 255 ticks) — DIVS.L's own 91-clock target needs 352 ticks; widened to `[15:0]` (the shift/rotate-only `ex_internal_stall_ticks_resolved` deliberately left at `[7:0]`, unaffected). (2) `div_trap` was a bare combinational expression with no one-shot protection — once `ex_valid` can be held ~350 ticks by the new stall, it would re-fire every tick, the identical bug class Phase 202 already found/fixed for `chk_trap`; added a matching `div_trap_raw`/`div_trap_fired_r` one-shot latch mirroring `chk_trap_raw`/`chk_trap_fired_r` exactly, same Icarus forward-reference workaround. **While verifying DIVS.L's own stall selection, found a real, previously-undiscovered correctness bug, not a timing bug**: DIVS.L measured DIVU.L's own stall value instead of its own — traced to `eu_seq.sv`'s `3'b110` decode case reading the signed/unsigned flag from `ext_data[6]`, empirically confirmed wrong via real vasm-assembled output (`DIVS.L`=`0x2802`, `DIVU.L`=`0x2002`, differing at exactly bit 11, both bit 6=0) and a definitive discriminating execution test (negative dividend through `vvp sim/timing` computed the UNSIGNED result for a real-encoded DIVS.L). Real 68020 extension-word layout: bits 14:12=Dh/Dr, **11=sign**, 10=size(MUL-only), 2:0=Dl/Dq — bit 6 has no meaning here. Every real-encoded DIVS.L in this RTL silently computed DIVU.L's result. Harte has zero coverage of the `.L` MUL/DIV forms (68000-captured corpus, 68020+-only feature), and every existing hand-crafted `tb/alu_reg_tb.sv` "signed" test had independently set bit 6 (matching the RTL's own wrong convention) — both self-consistently validating a fiction for 184 prior phases. User: "fix the decode bug first." **Fixed in `rtl/eu_seq.sv`**: both `ext_data[6]` occurrences → `ext_data[11]`, with a comment explaining the real layout and why nothing caught it before. **Fixed in `tb/alu_reg_tb.sv`**: recomputed the 3 affected extension-word literals (MUL-03 `0x6045`→`0x6805`, MUL-04 `0x6445`→`0x6C05`, DIV-02 `0x3042`→`0x3802`, clear bit 6/set bit 11), comments updated; the 3 unsigned-form tests needed no literal change (0 either bit position). Also fixed a self-inflicted testbench-margin mistake: a first attempt blanket-widened the shared `run_instr()` settle margin (affecting every unrelated test in the file), which blew the global timeout — reverted to the original margin, added a dedicated `wait_muldivl_stall` task called only after the ~8 specific `.L Dn,Dn` MUL/DIV invocations that need it, widened the global watchdog modestly (`#500000`→`#530000`). Added 4 new permanent timing-corpus tests (`a3_divs_l_dn_dn`/`a3_divu_l_dn_dn`/`a3_muls_l_dn_dn`/`a3_mulu_l_dn_dn`, each `<OP>.L Dn,Dn`) — all four now measure exactly gap=+1, confirming both fixes together; added to `known_issues.json` under the established +1clk-recal convention. **Deliberately left out of scope, documented in the RTL's own comment**: the memory-EA form of MUL/DIV.L (`<ea>,Dn` with a memory operand) is entirely unimplemented — a separate, pre-existing gap, not touched by this phase. Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `eu_seq.sv` decode changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. See `plan.md §Phase 185` for the full writeup. **Systematic sweep for other "too fast" gaps** (second half of the same request, investigation only): checked every purely-combinational execution unit (`eu_alu`/`eu_shifter`/`eu_bcd`/`eu_bitops`/`eu_bitfield`/`eu_mul_div`, via `grep "purely combinational" rtl/*.sv`) — all but `eu_mul_div` already had their register-direct gaps closed by Phase 162 Part D; `eu_mul_div` (this phase) was the last and largest remaining instance. Full single-instruction timing corpus re-survey (125 tests) after the fix: gap min=-5/max=+4/mean=+0.76, **0 unexplained non-zero gaps** — every remaining negative-gap test is already documented in `known_issues.json` as an unrelated, previously-characterized finding (bus-cycle-granularity/measurement-technique quirks), none a new instance of the "instant computation vs. real serial microcode" pattern. **MUL/DIV was the only remaining gap of this class — the sweep found nothing else to fix.**

215 (open-items backlog Stage 1). D-cache aliasing test timing sensitivity, root-caused (testbench artifact, confirmed) + a real new FC-aliasing test added. User asked to formalize a 13-item backlog of previously-documented, deferred findings and work through them one at a time (implement, test, commit/push, next). Stage 1 re-investigated Phase 158 Stage 2's own abandoned MOVES-based D-cache aliasing test. Root-caused via direct `rom[]` inspection (temporary `$display`s, removed before finalizing): the D-1..D-9 flowing ROM-address accumulator (starts at `a=0x900`) physically overlaps the fixed, literal-address exception-handler blocks D-5/D-6 use (0x0780-0x0C00) — confirmed directly that `rom[0xA00/4]` currently holds the flowing D-5 setup's own instruction, not the fixed `D5_CONT_A` handler continuation's intended content — a plain last-write-wins overwrite between two independently-grown ROM allocation schemes, not a simulation timing race at all. Currently harmless purely by luck of exactly which bytes overlap; this also explains D-9's own earlier documented relocation. **Concluded: testbench address-space-collision artifact, not an RTL bug** — deliberately did not attempt to fix the file's own underlying address map (real, currently-passing-by-luck state, disproportionate risk for this stage). Delivered the actual value instead: built the MOVES-based D-cache FC-aliasing test properly, placed at a genuinely isolated address (0x1900, confirmed free via a full `rom[]` survey) following D-9's own proven-safe "explicit-jump-only" convention. New `emit_set_sfc()` codegen helper; new `MOVES_L_A0_D6`/`_EXT` opcode constants (cross-checked in Python against `tb/system_tb.sv`'s own proven MOVES-01 example). Test: supervisor-FC read (cold miss, caches an FC=101-tagged entry) then two MOVES reads of the same logical address using SFC=1 (user data, FC=001) — first must miss (proving FC-aware tag prevents a false hit), second must hit (caching still works under the alternate FC). **Found and fixed a real bug in the new test itself**: the first attempt's "genuine miss" check failed even though `tag_d[4]` had genuinely transitioned to a fresh fc=001-tagged entry (proving the RTL fix works) — the bus-activity counter used (`data_ds_count`) is deliberately scoped to `ext_fc==3'b101` only, structurally blind to fc=001 accesses. Added a new `user_ds_count` counter (identical shape, filtered on fc=001) and rewired the checks. Results: all 6 new D-13 checks pass, `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12 — no Harte re-run needed (testbench-only, `git diff --stat rtl/` empty). **Closes Stage 1 of the 13-item open-items backlog.** See `plan.md §Phase 186` for the full writeup.
