216 (open-items backlog Stage 2). PLOAD/IC_BURST0/CACR hang root-caused and fixed — a real, previously-undiscovered RTL bug; a second real bug found and deliberately deferred. Reproduced the Phase 155 hang cleanly: a real `PLOAD (A0)` instruction fetched through the real IFU showed `decode_pc` wandering off to a wild address (`0x6BE6`) instead of completing. **Root cause**: `m68030_seq.sv`'s `ext_count` classifier has no entry anywhere for the entire F-line MMU family (PFLUSH/PFLUSHA/PTEST/PMOVE/PLOAD, `f_group=4'hF, f_dn=000`) — silently falls through to `ext_count=0`, so `drain` never advances past the mandatory extension word, leaving it in the prefetch queue to be misdecoded as the next instruction. Confirmed exactly for PLOAD: its own ext word (`0x6200`) decodes as `BRA.W`, taking the following word as displacement — hand-computed target (`0x3FAA+0x2C3C=0x6BE6`) matched the observed jump exactly. PFLUSH/PTEST/PMOVE share the identical bug (`mmu_op_type` lives in `ext_data`, invisible to this opcode-only classifier, so all four are structurally indistinguishable to it) but their own extension-word bit patterns happen to decode harmlessly when misinterpreted as a fresh opcode in every existing test, masking it until now. **Fixed in `rtl/m68030_seq.sv`**: added `else if ((f_group==4'hf) && (f_dn==3'b000)) ext_count=3'd1;` matching `eu_seq.sv`'s own unconditional-on-f_mode gate. **Found a second real bug investigating why CACR read "disabled"**: `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`'s own MOVEQ opcode `0x7201` decodes as `MOVEQ #1,D1`, not `D7` as its comment (and every phase since 135) assumed — confirmed via Python bit decode and direct trace — so its own `MOVEC D7,CACR` always wrote CACR=0; this test has never actually enabled the I-cache since it was written, despite passing this whole time (the DBF loop's checksum doesn't depend on caching actually happening). **Fixing that opcode exposed a THIRD, deeper bug**: with the I-cache genuinely active for the first time, the downstream `Indexed-EA-no-extra-read` test's own setup instructions fetched as garbage — traced to `biu_icache_if.sv`'s cache line at idx=0xE/tag=0x2D showing `valid_i=1` with a correctly-matching tag but wrong content, transitioning mid-sequence into a fresh miss-fill — consistent with a line marked valid before a fill genuinely completed, plausibly related to (not conclusively tied to) Phase 129's own partial-abandonment handling not fully covering multi-word `IC_SINGLE_0..3` sequences. **Not root-caused to completion or fixed this stage** — reverted the MOVEQ opcode back to its original wrong-but-stable value, with the full derivation left in-line as a comment, rather than risk a half-diagnosed fix. New permanent regression test `PLOAD-ext-count` in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv` — the first-ever real-IFU test of PLOAD, closing Phase 150 Stage 5's own reverted attempt. Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `m68030_seq.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline. **Closes Stage 2** (the PLOAD hang, its original scope) with two additional real findings documented for future stages. See `plan.md §Phase 187` for the full writeup.

217 (open-items backlog Stage 3). I-cache "stale-fill" bug root-caused fully — confirmed to be a testbench-structural artifact, not an RTL bug. User asked to insert this immediately after Phase 216 found it. Re-derived `biu_icache_if.sv`'s own `IC_SINGLE_0..3` state machine directly and found `valid_i`/`tag_i` are only ever written at the LAST word, ruling out the original "premature valid bit" hypothesis before writing any fix. A `valid_i[14]`/`tag_i[14]`-change monitor running from time 0 found the real picture: a first fill legitimately cached an unrelated page (I-6's own 0x1700 content) at idx=14; a second, later fill correctly replaced the tag but only `data_i[14][0]` picked up fresh content — words 1-3 stayed stale, yet tag/valid both committed as if the whole line refilled. Traced the second fill's own 4 individual reads directly: word 0 correct, words 1-3 all read an identical `0x4e714e71` (not garbage — a specific, repeated value). Traced one level deeper into `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own shared read-cycle machinery and found **`ext_d_in` itself — the raw combinational `rom[]` read, before any cache logic — was already wrong** for the requested address, conclusively ruling out both cache modules as the culprit. **Root cause**: `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`'s own long-standing convention interleaves each test's `rom[]` setup with the *previous* test's own check code (which contains real `@(posedge clk_4x)` calls) — safe for the file's entire history since instruction fetch was always just-in-time without a genuine I-cache. Once Phase 187's own MOVEQ fix made the I-cache genuinely active, its real speculative readahead — given ample time by `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`'s own 10-pass tight loop — raced ahead into idx=0xE before `CLR-non-indexed-no-extra-read`'s and `Indexed-EA-no-extra-read`'s own `rom[]` writes had executed in SV program order. Exactly the "ROM write issued after simulated time already passed that address" class this project has hit repeatedly (I-4/I-5 Phase 131, T4c/T4d Phase 126, this session's own Stage 1 finding for `cache_tb.sv`), just newly exposed via genuine readahead instead of direct PC execution — and confirmed this time all the way down to the raw memory-model read. **Fix (testbench-only, zero RTL changes)**: relocated `CLR-non-indexed-no-extra-read`'s, `Indexed-EA-no-extra-read`'s, and `PLOAD-ext-count`'s own `rom[]` content to execute up front, before `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`'s own loop even starts — each test's own check block stays in place. `PLOAD-ext-count` needed the identical relocation too (found via an otherwise-identical second failure). Results: all previously-affected checks pass cleanly, `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12 — no Harte re-run needed (`git diff --stat rtl/` empty). **Closes Stage 3, and with it, the loop Stage 2 opened — `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit` now genuinely exercises the I-cache for the first time in this project's history, with every downstream test passing correctly under real caching.** See `plan.md §Phase 188` for the full writeup.

218 (open-items backlog Stage 4). DSACK wait-states-on-FSM-beats breadth: MOVEP and single-address CAS. Extended Category H (`docs/stalls.md`) from 4 sources (TAS, MOVEM, CAS2, memory-indirect EA) to 6, adding MOVEP.L's own byte-interleaved store and single-address CAS.L's own indivisible RMW lock. New `WS-MOVEP`/`WS-CAS` tests in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`, reusing the exact opcode/ext-word encodings already proven by `INT-mid-MOVEP`/`INT-mid-CAS` (Phase 126). **Found and fixed a real placement mistake before the tests would even run**: the new addresses (0x2E20+) were unreachable by the DUT's own NOP-fall-through execution — program TEXT order placed the new `rom[]` writes between `WS-Memind` and `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`, but in ADDRESS order everything already routes elsewhere, ending at `PLOAD-ext-count`'s own permanent self-park. Fixed by redirecting via explicit JMP.L (matching this project's own established "isolated address, explicit-jump-only" convention) — replaced `WS-Memind`'s own trailing NOP, in the exact 6-byte gap before `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`'s own fixed start, with a JMP to the new tests, whose own tail JMPs back to preserve the original flow. Positioned entirely before `RAW-hazard-with-Ihit`'s own I-cache-enabling loop, so Stage 3's own readahead-race class doesn't apply. Results: `WS-MOVEP` (167→255 ticks) and `WS-CAS` (137→209 ticks) both show clean, unambiguous deltas; `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12 — no Harte re-run needed (testbench-only). **Closes Stage 4.** See `plan.md §Phase 189` for the full writeup.

219 (open-items backlog Stage 5). Interrupt-mid-FSM breadth: PACK and BFINS — plus a genuine, previously-unencountered testbench-methodology finding. Extended Category F from 7 sources to 9, adding PACK -(Ay),-(Ax),#0 (explicitly flagged by `INT-mid-ADDX`'s own comment as "not yet exercised") and BFINS D1,(An){o:w} (a genuinely different single-operand RMW FSM shape). **Hit a real, previously-unencountered testbench-methodology bug**: placing the new `run_int_mid_test(...)` calls in the same program-text position as their own setup (after `WS-CAS2`/`WS-Memind`/`WS-MOVEP`/`WS-CAS`'s own check blocks) meant SV program order no longer matched real DUT execution order — the new tests' own code is reached via a JMP redirect that executes *before* those four tests in the actual sequence. `run_int_mid_test`'s own synchronization (decode_pc-gate then sample `data_ds_count`) implicitly assumes the two orders match; when they don't, the wait resolves instantly against a decode_pc that's already raced far past the target, sampling arbitrary unrelated bus activity instead. Confirmed via direct trace (a `data_ds_count`-change monitor from time 0): decode_pc was already garbage and `exc_active` already 1 by the time "reached own code" fired, the interrupt having been injected against `WS-MOVEP`'s own unrelated tail activity. Two intermediate fixes (settle-NOP padding, suspecting "interrupt mid-JMP-redirect") were tried and discarded before the real mechanism was found. **Fix**: relocate only the `run_int_mid_test(...)` calls (not their own `rom[]` content, which stays written up front per the established pattern) to execute immediately after `T4d`'s own check block, matching real DUT execution order. Also empirically corrected `INT-mid-PACK`'s own expected bus-cycle count from a guessed 3 (ADDX's shape) to the measured, and architecturally correct, 2 — PACK's own destination is a pure write, unlike ADDX's addition which needs both operands read first. Results: both tests pass cleanly, `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12 — no Harte re-run needed (testbench-only). **Closes Stage 5.** See `plan.md §Phase 190` for the full writeup.

220 (open-items backlog Stage 6). Back-to-back FSM composition breadth: ADDX→TAS, the 4th Category D→D pair. Extended `docs/stalls.md`'s tally from 3 pairs (TAS→MOVEM, MOVEP→CAS, memory-indirect-EA→TAS) to 4, adding `ADDX.L -(A1),-(A0)` immediately followed by `TAS (A0)` — a new predecrement-memory-RMW-into-locked-RMW pairing, with a real cross-boundary data-flow check (not just "did it unstick", matching B-22's/T4c's/T4d's own established rigor): TAS must observe the exact byte ADDX itself just wrote. New `T4e` test in `tb/stall_fsm_tb.sv`, reached via redirecting `INT-mid-BFINS`'s own tail JMP and itself JMPing on to `WS-CAS2`'s own start, matching the file's own established isolated-address-plus-explicit-JMP convention. Applied Stage 5's own freshly-learned lesson *proactively* this time rather than discovering it reactively: the check block was positioned immediately after `INT-mid-BFINS`'s own call from the start (matching T4e's real DUT execution order), while `rom[]` content was written up front alongside `INT-mid-ADDX`'s own setup — the same "writes need to land before real time passes that address; calls need to match real execution order" split Stage 5 established, applied correctly on the first attempt. One check was deliberately written loosely rather than as an exact 32-bit match: ADDX's own sum depends on the incoming X-flag (unknown, left however prior tests set it — the same documented caveat `INT-mid-ADDX`'s own comment already carries) and can legitimately be 8 or 9 in the low byte, but always fits within the low byte regardless — so the check verifies only the top byte (0x00 before TAS, 0x80 after, bit 7 set) rather than asserting a specific low-byte sum that could spuriously fail on an unrelated X-flag difference, avoiding a repeat of Stage 5's "guessed value turned out wrong" pattern via a different route (narrowing the assertion to what's actually architecturally guaranteed, rather than guessing then correcting). Results: `T4e` passed cleanly on the first real run, including the exact bus-cycle-count guess (ADDX(3)+TAS(2)=5, correct without correction this time, unlike Stage 5's PACK). `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 12/12 — no Harte re-run needed (testbench-only, `git diff --stat rtl/` empty). **Closes Stage 6.** See `plan.md §Phase 191` for the full writeup.

221 (open-items backlog Stage 7). MUL/DIV.L memory-EA form. `dec_is_muldivl` was previously only ever set for the register-direct form (`f_mode==000`) — the `<ea>,Dl` memory-source forms of MULU.L/MULS.L/DIVU.L/DIVS.L were entirely undecoded, always producing an illegal-instruction result. Read the register-direct decode and the sibling MULU.W/MULS.W memory-source block (group C, already fully EA-extended) closely first: `md_src`/`md_dst` are already fully generic (`md_src=mem_rdata` when `dec_is_mem_src`, `md_dst=rd_b_data` always) — the only work needed was decode, mirroring `AND EA,Dn`'s own 2-port template (An→port A, Dl/Dq→port B) with zero register-port conflict for non-indexed EA modes. Confirmed via a full grep that no other decode claims `f_group==4/f_dn==110/f_mode!=000` (MOVEM's own `f_dn==110` sibling requires `f_ss[1]=1`, always 0 for MUL/DIV.L). **Scoped to non-indexed EA modes**: `(An)/(An)+/-(An)` (ext_count=1, descriptor in `ext_data[15:0]`), `(d16,An)/(xxx).W/(d16,PC)` (ext_count=2, descriptor in HIGH half `ext_data[31:16]` — the same "q1=other data, q2=EA descriptor" shape MOVEM/CMP2CHK2 already established, Phase 119/120), `(xxx).L` (ext_count=3, abs.L reconstructed from `ext_data[15:0]`+`q3_word`, matching MOVEM's own extraction). Deferred: indexed `(d8,An,Xn)/(d8,PC,Xn)` (would need the `dyn_bit_get_Dn` 3rd-operand-deferred-register trick, same shape as CHK's own indexed form) and `#imm` (needs a 3rd ext word). New `is_muldivl_mem`/`is_muldivl_2ext`/`is_muldivl_3ext` classifiers in `m68030_seq.sv`'s `ext_count` chain. **Found and fixed a real correctness bug via cosim**: the pre-existing artificial-internal-stall whitelist entry for MUL/DIV.L (Phase 214/185) is gated on `dec_is_muldivl` alone — since the new memory-EA decode also sets that flag (needed for the WB-stage Dh:Dl/Dr:Dq dual-register-write mechanism, unrelated to the stall), the new forms started incorrectly triggering the register-direct-calibrated ~168-352 tick stall too, re-issuing the memory read every tick instead of once (first cosim run showed the same address read 13-27 times in a row). Fixed by adding `&& !dec_is_mem_src` to the whitelist gate; a correctly-calibrated memory-EA stall (manual's own NCC row is the same 44/90/78 as register-direct, needing FIEA time added per the `**` footnote convention) is deliberately deferred as documented follow-up — this stage's priority was correctness, and the memory forms already have real natural bus-read timing unlike the purely-combinational register-direct form. New `tests/memind25.s` (4 instructions: MULU.L (A0),D2 / MULS.L (A1)+,D3 / DIVU.L ($8,A2),D4 / DIVS.L ($300).L,D5, covering register-indirect/autoincrement/displacement/absolute and both MUL+DIV signed+unsigned), each result written to a distinct memory address so the computed value (not just the source read) is diffable via buscmp.py; Musashi independently confirmed all four hand-derived expected values before the DUT ran. Needed a 600-cycle reference-log override (DIVS.L's own real divide-microcode-heavy Musashi run exceeds the generic 300-cycle default). Wired into `make cosim_memind` as `buscmp-memind25` — full trace (every read and write) matches Musashi exactly, no tolerance flags needed. Results: `make test` 36/36, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 13/13 (was 12/12), full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — `eu_seq.sv`/`m68030_seq.sv` changed) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions (Harte has zero coverage of `.L` MUL/DIV memory forms, a 68020+-only feature on a 68000-captured corpus — this sweep gates the shared decode/stall-whitelist code paths, not the new instructions directly). **Closes Stage 7.** See `plan.md §Phase 192` for the full writeup.

222 (open-items backlog Stage 8). Instruction-fetch FC hardcoding. `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s ordinary instruction-fetch dispatch hardcoded `cyc_fc=3'b110` (Supervisor Program Space) for every fetch regardless of the real S-bit — flagged by Phase 162 Stage 2 as blocking the I-cache's own FC-aware tag from ever discriminating user vs. supervisor fetches. Confirmed via direct reading that FC[1:0] is a constant `10` for program space (010 user/110 supervisor — only FC[2]=S toggles), so the fix is purely "thread the live S-bit through." Found, via a full grep, that this was actually **three** separate hardcoded sites, not one: `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own `grant_ifu` dispatch (had no `ifu_fc` input port at all); `m68030_biu.sv`'s `biu_icache_if` instantiation (its `ifu_fc` input existed since Phase 162 Stage 2 but was fed a literal, never made live); and `m68030_biu.sv`'s own `cg_burst_fc_mux` icache-burst fallback (a separate hardcode, since `biu_icache_if.sv` has no per-request `ic_burst_fc` output). All three needed fixing together. Plumbing mirrors `eu_seq.sv`'s own `mem_fc={sr_live[13],1'b0,1'b1}` convention: new `s_bit` input on `m68030_biu.sv`, `ifu_fc_computed={s_bit,2'b10}` consumed at all 3 sites (`biu_cycle_gen.sv` gained a matching new `ifu_fc` input port); `m68030_top.sv` wires `.s_bit(eu_sr_out[13])` — `eu_sr_out` was already available top-level for exception fault capture (its own port comment had said "read by exception ctrl, BIU FC" all along, the second half never actually wired until now). Blast radius confirmed small before starting: `m68030_biu`/`biu_cycle_gen` are each only instantiated once in `rtl/` plus one direct testbench (`tb/biu_int_tb.sv`, tied to `s_bit=1'b1`) — unlike Phase 158 Stage 7's CIIN/CIOUT addition (12 testbenches via `m68030_top`'s own port list), this stays internal to `m68030_biu.sv`'s narrow instantiator set. Updated `biu_icache_if.sv`'s own stale header comments that had documented this as a "separate, deeper, chip-wide undertaking." Predicted, before running anything, that this would be functionally **inert for the entire Harte corpus** (Harte never sets TC.E=1 or enables the I-cache, so FC has zero observable consequence for any final-state comparison even though ~4.3% of tests genuinely clear S mid-execution) — confirmed exactly as predicted. Results: `make test` 36/36 clean on the first attempt, `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 13/13, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — chip-wide blast radius, the highest-value Harte gate in this backlog) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. **Closes Stage 8.** See `plan.md §Phase 193` for the full writeup.

223 (open-items backlog Stage 9). CAS/CAS2 bus-level lock — investigated, deferred, no RTL change. The plan's own stated premise was half-stale: `bus_lock`'s own assign in `biu_cycle_gen.sv` has included `is_cas2` since the project's initial commit (never actually missing), and Phase 213's own `cas2_as_hold` fix already holds AS continuously across all 4 CAS2 sub-cycles — **CAS2 already has genuine bus-level lock in full**, contradicting the stale Phase 158-era `eu_seq.sv` comment this backlog's own Stage 9 description was written from. **Single-address CAS genuinely does lack it, confirmed real**: `mem_rmw` (the signal `biu_cycle_gen.sv` samples to route a request through the locked RMW states) is asserted only for `ex_is_tas`; CAS's own read+conditional-write dispatch through the ordinary unlocked path, returning to `ST_IDLE` between them. Read `docs/MC68030UM.pdf` directly (mapped PDF-page-to-manual-page via the front-matter TOC first): Section 3.5.1 ("no other instruction can change that location before CAS has written the new value") and Section 7.3.6's own per-state RMW description (States 4-7 never conditioned on anything) together confirm real CAS silicon performs the write bus cycle *unconditionally*, writing back the unchanged value on mismatch — this RTL currently skips the write entirely on mismatch, a second, related but distinct gap now confirmed against the manual rather than assumed. **Found the real blocking complexity via close FSM reading, not by writing code**: TAS's own write-phase readiness becomes valid exactly 1 cycle after the read's ack, matching `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own fixed-schedule `ST_RMW_READ_S7→ST_RMW_WRITE_S0` transition ("no bus release!"). CAS's FSM has a genuine intermediate register-only cycle (`cas_get_du_r`) to fetch Du via a register-port swap — `cas_ea_r`/`cas_rdata_r` (address + original value, enough for a mismatch's own no-op write) are captured with zero extra delay, but `cas_du_val_r` (needed only for the match case's real write) is one cycle later, and `biu_cycle_gen.sv`'s own RMW dispatch has no "not ready yet" mechanism. A correct fix needs either CAS's own register-port allocation restructured (reading Du simultaneously with the initial read, likely via the same `dyn_bit_get_Dn`-style deferred-swap trick used elsewhere) or new variable-timing tolerance in the shared, Harte-proven-for-TAS RMW machinery — real regression risk to TAS either way, not a 1-line gate extension. **Decision: investigated and precisely characterized, not implemented** — matches the project's own "confirmed real, but substantial — deferred to a dedicated future phase" precedent (closest prior example: Phase 158 Stage 8's BERR-during-fill deferral). No RTL/testbench changed. **Closes Stage 9 as an investigation.** See `plan.md §Phase 194` for the full writeup.

224 (open-items backlog Stage 10). Burst-cycle address freeze. Confirmed real bug from Phase 212's own investigation: `biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s `burst_addr_r` incremented per beat, feeding the address actually driven onto real pins during a burst, contradicting MC68030UM.pdf 7.3.7's "the address bus... remains driven to a constant value... incrementing... must be performed by external hardware." Confirmed nothing internal to the module depends on the increment (`burst_rdata_r[]`/`m16_wdata_mux` both already index off the separate `burst_beat_r` counter) — fixed by removing the increment line entirely. **Both testbench memory models needed coordinated updates**: `tb/mem_model.sv`'s own re-latch trigger (`ext_a != last_latched_addr`) and `tb/cache_tb.sv`'s own purely-combinational `rom[ext_a[13:2]]` read both relied on the address changing per beat. Added a new testbench-only `burst_beat_probe` input to `mem_model.sv` (explicitly documented as not a real pin — mirrors what a real peripheral's own internal beat counter would track, since real hardware doesn't put a beat index on the external bus either), hierarchically wired to the DUT's own already-existing `biu_burst_ctrl.sv` beat counter at all 6 `mem_model` instantiation sites across 3 testbenches (each at a different DUT instantiation depth); `cache_tb.sv` got the same `ext_a[13:2]+beat_probe` treatment applied directly. **Found and fixed a second real RTL bug via `make test`**: `burst_beat_r` had no general reset outside of a fresh burst dispatch — it stayed at its own last value (e.g. 3) forever after a burst completed, silently offsetting every ordinary access's address until the next burst overwrote it; first test run showed 15+ unrelated-looking `biu_tb.sv` failures (byte-lane writes, BKPT capture, plain reads) all traced to this. Fixed with `else if (at_idle) burst_beat_r <= 2'd0;`. Results: `make test` 36/36 (clean after both fixes), `cosim_grp` 8/8, `cosim_memind` 13/13, full 124-suite Harte sweep (mandatory — chip-wide shared machinery, though Harte itself never exercises burst mode) — PASS 702142, FAIL 2 (same documented ASL.b anomaly), SKIP 281221, TIMEOUT 0, bit-identical to baseline, zero regressions. **Closes Stage 10.** See `plan.md §Phase 195` for the full writeup.

225 (open-items backlog Stage 11). BERR-during-fill per-beat discrimination — investigated, deferred, no RTL change. Re-read Phase 158 Stage 8's own predecessor finding: real hardware faults on BERR only for the beat matching `woff_r` (the actually-requested word); any other beat just invalidates that entry, no fault — complicated by this project's burst mechanism always starting at word offset 0 (no address-wraparound to the requested word like real silicon), so "which beat is requested" varies. Traced every `ic_burst_berr`/`dc_burst_berr` site in both cache-if modules (4 each) — all unconditionally fault, confirming the gap is unchanged since Phase 158. **Attempted to scope a smaller first sub-piece**: a failing beat whose index comes *after* `woff_r`'s own beat is safe to complete without faulting (the requester's word was already captured into `fill_rdata_r` in an earlier successful beat, confirmed via the existing per-beat-conditional capture logic) — no retry needed, just a beat-index comparison. **Found this narrower piece is ALSO blocked, by a deeper gap than either this plan or Phase 158 anticipated**: only implementable for the degraded fallback path (`IC_FILL_1B/2B/3B`, genuine per-request ack/berr); the primary full-4-beat-burst-success path has **no per-beat BERR signal at all** — traced `ic_burst_berr`/`dc_burst_berr` back to `biu_burst_ctrl.sv`'s own `eu_burst_berr`/`eu_m16_berr`, a single registered pulse with zero beat-index information. Even the narrowed sub-piece needs new plumbing through the core burst state machine (the exact module Stage 10 just modified this same session) before any cache-if-level change is possible. **Decision: investigated and precisely re-scoped, not implemented** — matches Stage 9's own precedent. Concrete next step documented for a future phase: add a registered `burst_beat_at_berr` output to `biu_burst_ctrl.sv` capturing `burst_beat_r` at the moment BERR fires, threaded up through both cache-if modules — unlocks the after-woff_r sub-piece for the primary path too; the harder before-woff_r retry case stays separately deferred regardless. No RTL/testbench changed. **Closes Stage 11 as an investigation.** See `plan.md §Phase 196` for the full writeup.

## Verification Commands

```bash
make test          # 32/32 unit + integration regression
make buscmp        # smoke.s DUT vs Musashi bus log
make cosim_grp     # all 8 opcode group bus comparisons (grp0–grp7)
make buscmp-grp0   # single group (replace 0 with 1–7)
make dat-synth     # 50-vector synthetic register-state cosim (DUT vs Musashi)
make sim/harte_dat # (re)compile Harte testbench binary after RTL changes
# Tom Harte SingleStepTests (68000 one-instruction vectors):
python3 -u scripts/run_harte.py tests/harte/ADD.b.json.gz    # 100%
python3 -u scripts/run_harte.py tests/harte/SUB.b.json.gz    # 100%
python3 -u scripts/run_harte.py tests/harte/MOVE.b.json.gz   # 90.8% (arch gap)
# Run all: for f in tests/harte/*.json.gz; do python3 -u scripts/run_harte.py "$f"; done
```

**Fast path (Phases 110-111)**: `run_harte.py` above is what RTL-change verification gates
actually use (proven, one `vvp` process per test). For quick full-corpus checks,
`scripts/run_harte_batch.py` batches many tests per process — same `can_run`/`gen_hex`/
`compare` logic, validated to produce identical verdicts:

```bash
make sim/harte_batch                              # Icarus backend (build once)
make sim/harte_vbatch                             # Verilator backend (build once)
python3 -u scripts/run_harte_batch.py tests/harte/*.json.gz tests/harte/*.json.bin \
    --backend verilator -j 10 --chunk-size 300     # full 124-suite corpus in ~3m18s
# (chunk-size 150 is the tuned default for the Icarus backend specifically;
#  the Verilator full-sweep timing above was measured at chunk-size 300)
```

Bus log format: `BUS R|W %08x %08x fc=%b siz=%b` (siz: 00=longword, 01=byte, 10=word, 11=line)

`--dut-may-continue`: DUT may have extra trailing reads after STOP (IFU prefetch); REF ends at STOP.

`m68k_read_memory_32` fix (Phase 76): after first instruction word fetch, program-space 32-bit reads route through the siz=10 word cache so Musashi's extension-word fetches match DUT IFU bus cycles.

## SIZ[1:0] Encoding

| SIZ[1:0] | Transfer |
|----------|----------|
| 00 | Longword (32-bit) |
| 01 | Byte (8-bit) |
| 10 | Word (16-bit) |
| 11 | Line (16-byte burst) |

Bus width is determined dynamically per-cycle by the DSACK0/1 response. SIZ[1:0] are **outputs** that tell the peripheral the requested transfer size; the peripheral uses SIZ+A[1:0] to select which byte lanes to respond on.

## Style Rules

- Use SystemVerilog (`always_ff`, `always_comb`, `typedef enum`, `struct`) rather than plain Verilog-2001.
- Use `generate` loops for the 8 data registers, 8 address registers, and other replicated structures — do not copy-paste.
- The BIU's `biu_cycle_generator` is the only place S-state transitions live. Other modules consume `s_state` as an output — they do not drive it.
- Never combine two pipeline stages in the same `always` block; each stage needs its own flip-flop barrier.
